# database-architecture-audit - Work Plan

## 2026-08-09 owner-approved N1 authority amendment

This amendment supersedes every later clause that treats Google Sheets officer/president tabs or the frozen 22nd-officer payload as the direct PostgreSQL role-history source.

- Before N1 activation, existing Google Sheets officer/president tabs, the current Notion 22nd-officer/history pages and the frozen payload are migration inputs only.
- N1 creates one database under Notion `/home` named `조직·직책 이력 — 개발 중 편집 권위`. One record represents one person × organization × position × term. Historical officers, presidents, the current 22nd administration roster and the alumni faculty association are views, not independent authorities.
- N1 activates only after schema/view inventory, source revision capture, import, count/role/term/duplicate/member-match reconciliation, recoverable workbook export/archive digest verification and owner review. After activation, that Notion database is the sole pre-C1 role-history write authority.
- After N1 activation and archive verification, the active membership workbook retains only the integrated address book as the member identity/contact write authority; officer/president/faculty tabs are removed and never edited in parallel with Notion.
- Dues tiers are derived from approved database policy and are not editable Notion source fields.
- The frozen payload commit `9922cf3eccb65fa565380f9e7549628721602041` is retained as N1 seed provenance, not as a direct PostgreSQL source.
- Todo 14 is blocked by N1 and must replace the pre-decision logical-source rows, adapter codes and canonical manifest digest with the observed Sheets integrated-address-book and N1 Notion database contracts. No sequence 50 artifact may use the old digest.
- C1 remains separate: only the later owner declaration immediately before launch makes Production PostgreSQL the member/officer SSOT and turns Sheets/Notion into archive/read-only sources.

### 2026-08-09 N1-A execution checkpoint

- Created the Notion database `059feab5ea204f04a1cfed8dbca0b366` and eight review views under `/home`.
- Imported 92 preactivation records: 32 current 22nd officers, 22 historical presidents, 11 21st officers and 27 alumni-faculty members. Every record remains `검토필요`, non-public and unmatched.
- Read-only reconciliation found zero required-field/source-coordinate failures, two exact role duplicate groups (21st and 22nd president), eleven cross-source name candidates and one unresolved 21st term-boundary policy conflict.
- The active workbook was not mutated. Candidate post-archive removal tabs are sheet IDs `1578786852`, `1430874456` and `627911579`; no 22nd-officer tab exists.
- At this checkpoint N1 activation was blocked on recoverable workbook archive/digest verification, reconciliation resolution and one owner approval. PII-free evidence: `.omo/evidence/database-architecture-audit/n1/n1-a-preactivation-reconciliation.json`.
- The read-only XLSX archive was verified at Drive version `2122` and SHA-256 `9c710acd7a934e63882c54714255035beb8386b7550d26425da784d52c469518`. The subsequent disposable-copy path failed before creation with HTTP 403 `storageQuotaExceeded`, so no invalid batch or authority-source mutation occurred; this historical probe outcome is recorded as `blocked_atomic_write`, but it no longer describes the activated N1 state below.
- The owner manually deleted the three approved role tabs after the archive gate. Read-only metadata verified their absence and preservation of `통합주소록`. The two president provenance duplicates were moved to a separate Notion archive, 21st terms were rebound to the approved AGM36 close, and N1 activated at `2026-08-10T00:53:18+09:00` with 90 active records and zero exact-role duplicates. Todo 14 is now startable.


## TL;DR (For humans)
<!-- Fill this LAST, after the detailed plan below is written, so it summarizes the REAL plan. -->
<!-- Plain English for a non-engineer: NO file paths, NO todo numbers, NO wave/agent/tool names. -->

**What you'll get:** 현재 프로젝트 전체 DB를 빈 데이터베이스와 기존 Development Database 양쪽에서 똑같이 재현할 수 있는 스키마 원장으로 정리하고, 기존 회원·인증·탈퇴 동작을 보존하면서 무결성·보안·복구 체계를 보강합니다. 그 위에 회계·회비 스키마를 회원 식별, 직책 이력, 원천 권위, 수납·장부 연결과 감사 추적까지 결정 완료된 형태로 보정합니다.

**Why this approach:** 현재 DB는 정상 운영되지만 Drizzle·수동 SQL·런타임 DDL이 섞여 새 DB 재현과 적용 이력 증명이 어렵습니다. 먼저 하나의 검증 가능한 스키마 원장과 안전한 DB 대상 선택을 만든 뒤, 회계 원천과 경제사건을 분리하고 기존 웹 계정과 독립된 회원 ID를 사용해야 중복납부·탈퇴·직책 겸임·정정에도 기록이 흔들리지 않습니다.

**What it will NOT do:** 복식부기, 결제선생 연동, 은행 자동연동, 서비스 분리나 브라우저의 DB 직접 접속을 도입하지 않습니다. 운영 DB·배포·기존 회계 장부 행은 변경하지 않으며, 제외된 `KIKcd_B` 파일은 열지 않습니다.

**Effort:** XL
**Risk:** High - 기존 13개 테이블의 동작을 보존하면서 스키마 적용체계와 회계용 불변·시간·금액 제약을 동시에 강화해야 합니다.
**Decisions to sanity-check:** 단일 PostgreSQL 유지와 RLS 비활성/API 권한 경계, `users.id` 중심 행위자 기록, 2022–2025 장부·2026 은행·레거시 결제의 권위 순서, 총회의장 30,000원/400,000원 등급, 운영 변경의 별도 승인 원칙입니다.

Your next move: 필수 고정밀 이중검토가 모두 승인한 뒤 `$start-work database-architecture-audit`로 별도 실행합니다. Full execution detail follows below.

> TL;DR (machine): XL/high-risk, 4 waves and 23 implementation todos to establish a reproducible/checksummed whole-database foundation, harden current integrity/security/operations, correct the accounting schema and prove it in Development; 4 final verifier tasks plus one post-approval attestation; no Production mutation.

## Scope
### Must have
- Reconcile the verified Development baseline—13 tables, 112 columns, 13 PKs, 9 FKs, 6 non-PK UNIQUE constraints, 20 indexes and 9 sequences—against `shared/schema.ts`, checked-in migrations, runtime DDL and the schema catalog before changing anything.
- Replace the current mixed `db:push`/runtime/manual-DDL state with one reproducible, ordered and checksummed schema ledger. Ordinary Drizzle-owned objects and manual PostgreSQL-owned objects remain separate artifacts but share one DB-resident application ledger and one target-selection gate.
- Harden the existing schema without changing established account-deletion behavior: named CHECK constraints, missing uniqueness/FKs where clean data permits, supporting FK indexes, normalized identity keys, explicit deletion actions and drift tests.
- Keep one PostgreSQL modular monolith. Browser clients continue to use server APIs; RLS stays disabled in this phase, while server roles, schema/table/routine ACLs, function `search_path`, TLS behavior, pool limits and timeouts become explicit and catalog-verifiable.
- Amend the approved accounting/dues design before its feature implementation. The amended manifest must separate overlapping organizational positions from the derived non-overlapping dues tier, represent unmatched member candidates, preserve logical-source and coordinate versions, deduplicate one economic event across Sheets/bank/legacy inputs, use `users.id` as the authoritative mutation actor, and enforce the cashbook↔receipt handoff.
- Preserve a stable accounting member identity: internal `bigint` identity plus external immutable UUID; nullable links to `users` and `alumni_database`; match/relink history survives account deletion and roster changes.
- Encode the approved source authority order: finalized 2022–2025 ledger is historical economic-event authority; 2026 bank rows are bank authority; group rosters are allocation evidence only; after N1 the Sheets integrated address book is member identity/contact write authority and the unified Notion organization-role database is role-history write authority; eligible legacy `payments` rows are compatibility evidence that must be cross-linked or quarantined, never double-counted. After the owner-declared C1 timestamp, Production PostgreSQL is the sole member/officer read/write authority and those external sources are archive/read-only.
- Encode the owner-approved 22nd-administration design candidate without pretending board enactment: 21st terms end at the 36th AGM close (`2026-02-28 12:38 KST`); source appointment date and effective interval remain distinct; concurrent branch/class roles may overlap; every `*이사` maps to the 이사 tier; secondary branch/class roles add no dues tier; 총회의장 is a distinct officer position whose draft candidate uses monthly KRW 30,000 / annual KRW 400,000.
- Keep tests-after with Node test runner plus database integration/concurrency/catalog assertions. Run all executable validation in the Replit Development workspace/Database; keep Production read-only and unverified until the dedicated preflight todo succeeds.
- Update the canonical schema documentation and operations runbook in the application repository, including exact rollout, rollback/forward-fix, backup/restore drill, policy-pending RPO/RTO objectives, measured restore results, retention, actor, provenance and evidence requirements.

### Must NOT have (guardrails, anti-slop, scope boundaries)
- Do not implement double-entry bookkeeping, Payssam/결제선생 integration, provider webhooks, bank API automation, a service/database split or direct-browser database access.
- Do not inspect, import, move, normalize or delete `KIKcd_B.20250701.xlsx`, `KIKcd_B.20250701.txt` or unrelated `attached_assets/`.
- Do not mutate Production Database, Replit Secrets, Google Sheets/Notion sources, or deploy/Republish in this plan. The former Todo 23 disposable-copy probe and additive `22대 임원명단` tab publication are superseded by the 2026-08-09 owner authority decisions. Preserve the frozen payload as N1 seed provenance; do not execute the publisher, create a new intermediate Sheet authority tab, or bypass the unified Notion database with a direct PostgreSQL import.
- Do not drop or rename existing tables/columns, delete legacy `payments`, change tested account-deletion semantics, weaken Kakao/session security, store full bank account numbers, or copy roster contact/workplace/address fields into accounting tables.
- Do not use `db:push` as the sole migration ledger, generate environment-dependent SQL at apply time, silently auto-match by name, automatically approve classifications/allocations, or add speculative trigram/FTS/composite indexes without measured Development evidence.
- Do not claim Production matches Development or that the draft member/accounting regulation is effective before its board-resolution state and effective date are recorded.

### Approved architecture contracts

- **Identity:** existing `users.id`, `alumni_database.id`, `payments.id` remain integer keys. New domain keys are `bigint GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY`; externally stable member/receipt identifiers are UUIDs. Financial/audit provenance outlives web accounts.
- **Actors:** every member/accounting create/approve/reject/reverse/correct/close/reopen mutation requires `actor_user_id → users.id` at decision time and stores an immutable actor UID/display snapshot; the FK becomes nullable with `ON DELETE SET NULL` only when a later account deletion occurs. Schema-ledger bootstrap uses a non-person executor identity because `users` may not exist yet. `association_member_id` is nullable context only. Server `requireAdmin`/self checks remain the authorization boundary.
- **Time and money:** instants are `timestamptz`, calendar dates are `date`, ranges are half-open `[start,end)`, and new KRW amounts/balances are `bigint` serialized to JSON as canonical decimal strings.
- **Positions and dues:** `member_position_assignments` permits overlapping organizational positions with source appointment date, effective interval, precision and provenance. `member_dues_tier_history` is an immutable derived/versioned chain selected only from approved policy/mapping pairs; it never erases assignments and is never persisted from a draft policy.
- **Source identity:** logical source identity is stable across adapter releases. Source versions, source coordinates and content versions are separate. A changed previously-applied coordinate must become a superseding version or a blocker—never a second silent economic event.
- **Economic-event identity:** one canonical economic event may have many provenance links. 2022–2025 finalized-ledger rows, 2026 bank rows, group roster evidence and legacy-payment evidence are cross-linked through an explicit event/provenance/crosswalk model; receipt XOR applies only to its authoritative funding source, not to supporting evidence.
- **Cashbook/dues boundary:** an approved bank credit classified wholly or partly as dues must have approved receipts whose gross total equals the approved dues-category cashbook amount. Missing receipts, excess receipts or group/allocation mismatches block period close.
- **Immutability/versioning:** raw source rows, bank transactions, approved financial records, policy versions, rights snapshots and audit events are append-only. Corrections use supersession/reversal records with reason, recorded time and effective time.
- **Schema ownership:** Drizzle owns ordinary tables/columns/PK/FK/UNIQUE/CHECK/generated columns/indexes. Reviewed SHA-pinned SQL owns extensions, exclusion constraints, deferred/immutability triggers, functions and ACLs. Both artifacts are recorded in the same DB-resident migration ledger.
- **Rollout:** expand → capability/catalog preflight → apply ordinary artifact → apply manual variant → verify → backfill → compatibility read comparison → explicit cutover. Each artifact and its ledger row commit atomically; failure rolls back only the current artifact transaction. Earlier additive artifacts remain inert behind the legacy feature flag and are resumed forward from the first missing sequence. After cutover, inconsistencies use feature-flag read rollback or a forward-fix; no stage contracts the schema destructively.

### Normative execution input registry

- `$start-work` descriptor-opens this plan and records its exact approved SHA-256 as `architecture_plan_sha256`; execution is rejected if the copied bytes differ from the approval receipt. The two companion accounting inputs are exact: `.omo/plans/accounting-dues-prd.md` SHA-256 `07f02a89a70fa3be2888ddc1278b3e94bbbeda5631b7fa0abbc85af6d47e8cd6` and `.omo/drafts/accounting-dues-prd.md` SHA-256 `ecbe884b5105c7f697ee8ab5bb0bcb225c20117ecd309ced3e3249cdbb3e1ccb`. Todo 1 copies only these descriptor-opened bytes into the task worktree and fails on any mismatch.
- Baseline authority is read from commit `0e299abce9509456de0e8bfe224cb7ef392228d8`, never from a mutable sibling worktree: `git show 0e299abce9509456de0e8bfe224cb7ef392228d8:docs/database-schema.md` SHA-256 `6d6fc85bc51ebf00213c76a29a299f28bc619074e1d95e1c621b50cb9dac9778`; `git show 0e299abce9509456de0e8bfe224cb7ef392228d8:scripts/database-schema-catalog.sql` SHA-256 `7263c0736c96cbb3120252778b7adf8a463700ece8cd59b57be99cdb5fad757f`; `git show 0e299abce9509456de0e8bfe224cb7ef392228d8:docs/database-operations.md` SHA-256 `153b05be439e9b726df558337d346b40b94dbecf2a6dc75fb91635798480574d`. Todo 1 executes those literal commands and refuses changed bytes. Current shared-source authority is GitHub `main` merge commit `f0850820c0dd401cd316c08456eb427a1f0780ca` (PR 15); its three canonical baseline documents reproduce those hashes, so completed Todo 1 evidence remains immutable historical evidence rather than being rewritten. Every post-reconciliation task must contain `f0850820c0dd401cd316c08456eb427a1f0780ca`, use the reconciled manifest digest and rebound artifact descriptors, treat the expanded catalog as a read-only metadata-only superset, and keep Production catalog/drift explicitly unverified unless a separately authorized read-only dossier proves otherwise. The physical and generated-registry contract for `alumni_database.matched_user_id → users.id` is exactly `ON DELETE NO ACTION ON UPDATE NO ACTION`; no generator may normalize either action to `RESTRICT`. Historical receipts remain valid only for their recorded commit/manifest bytes, and post-reconciliation validation binds new downstream evidence to the current manifest rather than presenting earlier receipts as proof of changed bytes.

### Canonical database manifest v1

This appendix is a normative schema DSL and eliminates executor design choices. Todo 2 mechanically expands and serializes it as committed `docs/database-manifest.yaml`; that committed file, its SHA-256 and its containing commit become the sole implementation source of truth. Todo 11 may only point the accounting PRD at that digest—it cannot create a competing manifest. Any semantic change to this appendix requires a newly approved plan. Todo 2 must not invent a column, constraint, capability, artifact, sequence or dependency: the validator rejects every bare token or macro not declared below.

Global rules:

- Existing 13 tables and columns remain unless an additive alteration is listed below. Existing integer PKs stay integer. Every new table uses `id bigint GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY PRIMARY KEY`, except `business_operation_entities`, whose printed composite PK is the sole no-ID exception. UUID generation is closed, not inferred. Database-generated globally unique values are `users.user_uid`, `association_members.member_uid`, `accounting_source_releases.release_uid`, `accounting_import_batches.batch_uid`, `economic_events.event_uid`, `bank_accounts.account_uid`, `bank_reconciliations.reconciliation_uid`, and `accounting_audit_events.event_uid`; each is `uuid NOT NULL DEFAULT gen_random_uuid() UNIQUE`. `accounting_logical_sources.source_uid` is the sole deterministic-seed UUID exception: it is `uuid NOT NULL UNIQUE` with **no default**, and sequence 50 inserts exactly the ten literal UUIDs in the logical-source seed table below. Executor/caller-generated UUIDv4 values inserted without a database default are `schema_release_runs.release_uid`, `schema_capability_receipts.preflight_run_uid`, `business_operation_receipts.operation_uid|root_correlation_uid`, every `business_operation_entities.action_correlation_uid`, `source_decision_sets.decision_set_uid`, `economic_event_parties.party_uid`, `dues_receipts.receipt_uid`, and `dues_allocations.request_uid`; their printed UNIQUE constraints apply. Source-decision previews preallocate any new event-party/receipt/case/group-member UIDs before downstream insertion; a reused UID must already resolve to the same locked object. `dues_allocations.correction_group_uid` is nullable caller-generated UUIDv4, has no default, and is unique only with the printed correction-pair constraint. `schema_capability_receipts.disposable_run_uid` is nullable, resolver-supplied, has no default, and may repeat across preflights only inside the same disposable target; it is bound by the target/run CHECK and `(target_fingerprint,preflight_run_uid)` UNIQUE. VCHAIN logical UIDs `exception_uid`, `link_uid`, `assignment_uid`, `assessment_uid`, `classification_uid`, `claim_uid`, `collision_uid`, `cutover_uid`, and `carry_uid` are generated only on version 1 with `gen_random_uuid()`, copied byte-for-byte by successors, and unique with `version`. The VCHAIN UIDs `case_uid` and `group_member_uid` are instead caller-preallocated UUIDv4 values with no default on version 1 and are copied byte-for-byte by successors. Snapshot/reference UUIDs never have defaults or standalone uniqueness unless printed. Any UUID column not assigned by this registry fails manifest lint.
- In every new-table row, a listed column is `NOT NULL` unless prefixed `nullable` or declared nullable by a macro; it has no default unless the row/macro/global UID rule states one. All `bigint`/`integer` relationship columns named in the keys column use the literal referenced table and delete action there or the global FK rule. The manifest validator fails on an untyped, unknown or multiply-declared column.
- All unspecified new FKs are `NOT NULL ON DELETE RESTRICT ON UPDATE RESTRICT` and receive a btree index. No new financial, provenance, version or audit child uses `CASCADE`.
- `AUDIT_ACTOR(prefix,scope-set)` is the nonrecursive six-column bundle: `<prefix>_user_id integer NULL REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE SET NULL ON UPDATE RESTRICT`, `<prefix>_uid_snapshot uuid NOT NULL`, `<prefix>_name_snapshot text NOT NULL`, `<prefix>_scope text NOT NULL CHECK (<prefix>_scope IN (<literal scope-set>))`, `<prefix>_authorization_version char(64) NOT NULL CHECK (<prefix>_authorization_version ~ '^[0-9a-f]{64}$')`, and `<prefix>_at timestamptz NOT NULL`; it also emits btree index `(<prefix>_user_id)`. Allowed scopes are only `admin|member_self|migration_admin`. Its INSERT trigger requires an already first-class-locked live user, exact UID/name snapshot and `users.is_admin=true` for `admin|migration_admin`. Ordinary `member_self` is legal only for an entity whose manifest row declares `actor_target_member_id`; the trigger resolves that member, requires the same live `user_id`, and requires `status='active'`. Account deletion is the one separate self-target mode: the root `account:delete` receipt declares `actor_target_user_id=users.id`, and every enumerated child action inherits that same locked target-user snapshot by joining its `business_operation_entities.operation_uid` to the root receipt; no child needs its own member target column. It accepts zero or more currently linked `association_members` rows with status `active|ended`, including zero rows, and computes `authorization_version=sha256(RFC8785({authorization_version:"member-self-delete-v1",target_fingerprint,actor_target_user_id,user_uid,linked_members:[{member_uid,status}]}))` with canonical lowercase UUIDs, canonical unsigned-decimal user ID and the linked array sorted by member UID. The identity-link unlink child, if any, must be among that enumeration and target the same user; a member-less web account uses the canonical empty array. No other `member_self` action may use this mode. The trigger never requires another audit row.
- `ACTOR(prefix,scope-set)` expands the six `AUDIT_ACTOR` columns plus `<prefix>_correlation_uid uuid UNIQUE`. On a table without an optional transition bundle, correlation is `NOT NULL`; the INSERT trigger proves one matching `business_operation_entities` result and exactly one audit action. On a table with optional bundles, correlation is nullable: version-1/root rows require it, every successor requires it NULL, and the six recorded actor columns copy the populated transition bundle's immutable actor evidence without emitting a second audit row. The actor user FK/index, snapshot nullability and authorization CHECK remain exactly those of `AUDIT_ACTOR`.
- `OPTIONAL_ACTOR(prefix,scope-set,transition)` expands to seven nullable columns: `<prefix>_user_id integer REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE SET NULL ON UPDATE RESTRICT`, `<prefix>_uid_snapshot uuid`, `<prefix>_name_snapshot text`, `<prefix>_scope text CHECK (<prefix>_scope IN (<literal scope-set>))`, `<prefix>_authorization_version char(64) CHECK (<prefix>_authorization_version ~ '^[0-9a-f]{64}$')`, `<prefix>_at timestamptz`, and `<prefix>_correlation_uid uuid`; it emits a btree actor index and partial UNIQUE correlation index `WHERE <prefix>_correlation_uid IS NOT NULL`. Its CHECK is exactly `(correlation IS NULL AND uid_snapshot IS NULL AND name_snapshot IS NULL AND scope IS NULL AND authorization_version IS NULL AND at IS NULL) OR (correlation IS NOT NULL AND uid_snapshot IS NOT NULL AND name_snapshot IS NOT NULL AND scope IS NOT NULL AND authorization_version IS NOT NULL AND at IS NOT NULL)`. The INSERT trigger additionally requires `user_id IS NULL` in the first branch and a locked nonnull live `user_id` in the second; only a later guarded parent deletion may NULL that user ID while snapshots remain. A successor populates exactly the bundle whose printed parent→new predicate is true; all other optional correlations and the base correlation are NULL. A root has all optional bundles NULL. Deferred checks require exactly one active action correlation, except the explicitly actorless sequence-15 schema-exception root.
- The default actor expansion is literal: `association_members`, `member_match_cases`, `member_match_candidates`, `accounting_source_releases`, `accounting_import_coordinates`, `accounting_import_row_versions`, `accounting_periods`, `bank_accounts`, `bank_transactions`, and `bank_reconciliation_items` are the complete `DEFAULT_ACTOR` table set. Each receives `ACTOR(recorded_actor,admin|migration_admin)` with prefix `recorded_actor` and scope set `admin|migration_admin`; no other implicit prefix/scope exists. `accounting_import_batches` is expressly excluded because its row declares the single `preview_actor` root plus apply/failure transition bundles. Ledger/capability/exception primitives, `business_operation_receipts`, `business_operation_entities`, and `accounting_audit_events` are excluded. Every remaining business table already prints its literal actor macro invocation in its row. There are exactly two actor-bearing support-table exceptions, neither separately addressable in the audit map: `mutable_entity_action_history.mutation_actor` proves the parent `member|period` result/action, while `member_activity_events.activity_actor` byte-copies that same member creation/end/reactivate actor evidence and correlation without emitting another result or audit row. Todo 2 rejects `DEFAULT_ACTOR`, `ACTOR`, `OPTIONAL_ACTOR`, `VCHAIN`, `actor bundles`, `actor fields` or any other macro token after expansion.
- `VCHAIN(scope)` is insert-only and expands exactly once to `version integer NOT NULL CHECK (version>=1)`, `effective_at timestamptz NOT NULL`, `recorded_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT clock_timestamp()`, and `supersedes_id bigint NULL REFERENCES <same-table>(id) ON DELETE RESTRICT ON UPDATE RESTRICT`, plus UNIQUE `(<scope>,version)`, partial UNIQUE `(supersedes_id) WHERE supersedes_id IS NOT NULL`, and btree parent index. Version 1 has `supersedes_id IS NULL`; N+1 must target the first-class-locked structural tip, use `version=parent.version+1`, copy every scope column byte-for-byte, and satisfy `effective_at>=parent.effective_at` and `recorded_at>=parent.recorded_at`. A second child, skipped version, non-tip parent or scope change fails. Current as-of is greatest published `(effective_at,version,id)` not after the instant. Published sets are closed: schema exception=`open|resolved|waived`; match case=`approved|rejected`; identity link/position/tier/rights/legacy decision=`every successor`; mapping/policy/category=`approved`; pledge=`confirmed`; assessment=`approved|reversed`; classification=`approved|quarantined`; claim=`open|bound|rejected`; authority decision=`select|supersede|quarantine`; event collision=`open|resolved`; legacy cutover=`legacy|fenced|new|read_rollback`; group member=`unmatched|candidate|approved|rejected` current tip with `effect_kind='include'`, while a `cancel` successor removes it from the signed projection; carryforward=`open|bound|resolved`. Todo 2 rejects an unlisted VCHAIN table/state.
- `INTERVAL_TS(from,to)` is `<from> timestamptz NOT NULL`, `<to> timestamptz NULL`, `CHECK (<to> IS NULL OR <from> < <to>)`; `INTERVAL_DATE(from,to)` is the analogous `date` pair. `MONEY(name,zero)` is `bigint NOT NULL CHECK (name>=0)`; `MONEY(name,positive)` uses `>0`. Every bare time token ending `_at`, plus `opened_at`, `released_at`, `previewed_at`, `applied_at`, `statement_end_at`, `starts_at`, `ends_at`, is `timestamptz`; `joined_at/ended_at` and position/link `effective_from/effective_to` are also `timestamptz`. `posted_date`, `due_date`, pledge `effective_from/effective_to`, category `active_from/active_to`, and tier `obligation_from/obligation_to` are `date`.
- `CANONICAL_PHONE(raw)` is an ordinary generated-column expression, not a function or trigger. Let `d=regexp_replace(coalesce(raw,''),'[^0-9]','','g')`; the macro expands textually to `CASE WHEN raw IS NULL OR btrim(raw)='' THEN NULL WHEN d ~ '^8210[0-9]{8}$' THEN '0'||substring(d from 3) WHEN d ~ '^10[0-9]{8}$' THEN '0'||d WHEN d ~ '^0[0-9]{9,10}$' THEN d ELSE NULL END`, with every `d` replaced by the full `regexp_replace` expression. Todo 2 emits that exact built-in-only expression into sequence 20. A companion CHECK requires a nonblank raw value to produce non-NULL canonical output, so invalid text cannot hide behind nullable uniqueness. No canonical-phone trigger/function exists.
- Object names are deterministic: PK `<table>_pkey`; FK `<table>_<column>_fkey`; UNIQUE `<table>__<columns>__key`; CHECK `<table>__<rule>__check`; index `<table>__<columns>__idx`; trigger `<table>__<rule>__trg`; exclusion `<table>__<rule>__excl`. Names longer than 63 bytes use the first 52 UTF-8-safe bytes plus `_` and the first 10 lowercase hex characters of SHA-256 of the full name. Todo 2's validator recomputes every name.
- New status/type columns are `text` with the literal named CHECK sets below. New instants are `timestamptz`; dates are `date`; money is nonnegative/positive `bigint` KRW. All approved/version/audit rows are protected from direct `UPDATE/DELETE`; supersession/reversal is append-only.
- Actor authorization is split deliberately: Express middleware proves the endpoint/action scope before opening the transaction; the DB trigger independently proves the live user, admin/self relation, immutable snapshots, allowed state transition and same-transaction audit correlation. Neither layer trusts a client-supplied actor or snapshot.

Exact target and capability contract:

- `development` and an executor-created `disposable-test` database are the only mutable target kinds in this plan. Development resolves from Replit `PGHOST`, `PGPORT`, `PGUSER`, `PGPASSWORD`, `PGDATABASE`; `PGDATABASE` and `current_database()` must both be exactly `heliumdb`. `DATABASE_URL`, `PROD_DATABASE_URL` and `PROD_DATABASE_READONLY_URL` are rejected for a Development apply. Runtime, Drizzle and the runner share this resolver.
- `disposable-test` is executable only with `--target disposable-test --run-uid <lowercase UUIDv4>` and the same approved Development `PGHOST|PGPORT|PGUSER|PGPASSWORD`; URL credentials and every Production variable are rejected. The control connection must first reproduce Todo 1's Development fingerprint/current-user OID and require `(SELECT rolcreatedb OR rolsuper FROM pg_roles WHERE oid=current_user::regrole::oid)=true`. The database name is exactly `dgkma_test_` plus the first 20 lowercase hex characters of `sha256(run_uid)`. Creation uses `CREATE DATABASE <quoted validated name> TEMPLATE template0 OWNER <quoted current_user>` and immediately stores database comment `dgkma-disposable-v1|run_uid=<uuid>|parent=<development fingerprint>|owner_oid=<oid>`.
- A disposable name collision is resumable only when the comment bytes, owner OID, parent fingerprint and run UID all match and `pg_stat_activity` shows no session except the resolver's `application_name='dgkma-disposable:<run_uid>'`; otherwise abort without connecting, terminating or dropping. Every disposable connection must carry that exact application name and reproduce the stored fingerprint. A target-bound synthetic admin may be created only after sequence 40 inside that disposable database, carries the synthetic authorization source named below, and is invalid on Development or any other fingerprint. `finally` teardown revokes new connections, rolls back and closes **every target-database connection including the resolver**, then uses only the approved parent Development control connection to require zero `pg_stat_activity` rows for the target, drop it, and prove absence from `pg_database`. An unknown/remaining session or failed absence check makes the task fail with a redacted cleanup receipt; it never waits for a target session and then asks that session to drop its own database, and never uses `DROP ... FORCE`.
- The synthetic authorization source is exactly `DISPOSABLE_SYNTHETIC_ADMIN_V1`. After sequence 40, and never on Development/Production, the runner inserts one `users` row with `kakao_id=NULL`, `email='dgkma-disposable+'||first20hex(sha256(run_uid))||'@invalid.example'`, `name='Disposable Migration Admin'`, `graduation_year=NULL`, `is_verified=true`, `is_admin=true`, `kakao_sync_enabled=false`, every optional profile/phone/birthday/region field NULL, and database defaults for timestamps/user UID. It then inserts one append-only receipt `docs/database-targets/disposable/<run_uid>-admin.json` in the attempt directory with `{source_code,run_uid,target_fingerprint,user_id,user_uid,is_admin,authorization_version,created_at,row_digest}`; `authorization_version` uses the exact target-bound formula below and `row_digest` hashes canonical JSON excluding itself. The trigger requires the email token, target kind/fingerprint/run UID and source code to match; the user cannot authorize another database/run. The row and receipt die with database teardown and are never copied to Development, source documents or Git.
- Capability preflight on `development|disposable-test` generates `preflight_run_uid` as a lowercase UUIDv4 and sets `probe_token=first12hex(sha256('probe-v1'||E'\n'||target_fingerprint||E'\n'||preflight_run_uid))`; identifier validation requires exactly `[0-9a-f]{12}`. It then runs these literal rollback-only probes; neither may leave a catalog object. The canonical RFC-8785 receipt JSON is assembled **after** the probes and contains `preflight_run_uid`, `probe_token`, inputs/outcomes but omits `receipt_sha256`; `receipt_sha256=sha256(canonical_json_bytes)`. Thus token identity has no receipt-hash cycle. Production read-only inspection never executes a probe and never inserts this receipt:
  ```sql
  BEGIN;
  CREATE TABLE public.__dgkma_table_probe_<probe_token> (id bigint PRIMARY KEY);
  DROP TABLE public.__dgkma_table_probe_<probe_token>;
  ROLLBACK;
  ```
  ```sql
  BEGIN;
  CREATE FUNCTION public.__dgkma_routine_probe_<probe_token>() RETURNS integer
  LANGUAGE sql SET search_path = pg_catalog, public AS 'SELECT 1';
  REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION public.__dgkma_routine_probe_<probe_token>() FROM PUBLIC;
  DROP FUNCTION public.__dgkma_routine_probe_<probe_token>();
  ROLLBACK;
  ```
  Success requires both transactions to end `ROLLBACK`, both `to_regclass`/`to_regprocedure` absence checks to return NULL on a new connection, and the observed table/routine CREATE booleans to match the stored capability receipt. A denied probe selects no artifact that needs that privilege; any partial object or privilege mismatch blocks apply.
- `production-readonly` may resolve only from `PROD_DATABASE_READONLY_URL`; `current_database()` must be exactly `neondb`; the connection immediately enters `BEGIN READ ONLY`, requires `transaction_read_only='on'`, `has_database_privilege(current_user,current_database(),'CREATE')=false`, and no CREATE on `public`. It runs catalog SELECTs only and writes no row or probe. The uncommitted attempt-directory dossier `production-readonly-catalog-v1` is canonical JSON with exactly `{schema_version,target_fingerprint,current_database,current_user_oid,server_version_num,catalog_sha256,query_sha256,observed_at,result,receipt_sha256}`; `result` is `verified|unverified`, `query_sha256` binds the exact committed read-only SQL, and the self-hash excludes only itself. Failure leaves Production `unverified`. `PROD_DATABASE_URL` is never read by this plan.
- Target fingerprinting is one closed, precedence-safe contract. Each preimage is UTF-8 text with literal byte `0x0a` between fields, no trailing LF, lowercase kind/database/user, canonical unsigned decimal OID/port/version and the literal ASCII `local` for absent address/port. For `development|production-readonly`, `fingerprint=sha256("dgkma-target-v1\n"+kind+"\n"+database+"\n"+user+"\n"+address+"\n"+port+"\n"+server_version_num)`. For `disposable-test`, and only that kind, `fingerprint=sha256("dgkma-disposable-target-v1\n"+database_name+"\n"+current_user_oid+"\n"+parent_development_fingerprint+"\n"+run_uid+"\n"+server_version_num)`. The disposable formula supersedes the ordinary formula; applying the ordinary formula to a disposable target is an error. `dgkma_current_target_fingerprint()` handles only stored mutable kinds `development|disposable-test`; capability, ledger, synthetic-admin and actor-authorization receipts use that function and preimage fixture. The external Production dossier computes the ordinary `production-readonly` formula from read-only SELECT metadata and stores no DB receipt. The approved mutable tuple is `development|heliumdb|PG*`; any unknown URL fallback, database name or credential source is refused even if its fingerprint was seen before.
- Capability receipt fields are `target_kind`, nullable `parent_target_fingerprint`/`disposable_run_uid`, `preflight_run_uid`, `probe_token`, `server_version_num`, `gen_random_uuid_available`, `btree_gist_installed`, `btree_gist_available`, `btree_gist_create_privilege`, `current_user`, `current_user_oid`, database/schema/table/routine CREATE privileges, `table_create_probe_passed`, `routine_create_probe_passed`, `observed_catalog_sha256`, `observed_at` and separately computed `receipt_sha256`. Production/Development and ordinary disposable execution select deterministically: use the already-hashed `preferred_btree_gist` when `btree_gist_installed=true`, or when `btree_gist_available=true AND btree_gist_create_privilege=true`; otherwise use the already-hashed `deferred_trigger_fallback`. The sole test override is `--capability-variant fallback-test` on `disposable-test`: it may select the already-hashed fallback even when preferred is available, records `variant_test_override=true` only in the external attempt receipt, and is forbidden on Development/Production, from ledger/capability receipt rows, and from rollout evidence. `preferred_btree_gist` can never be forced when its capability predicate is false. Todo 1 freezes `docs/database-targets/development-approved.json` as RFC-8785 canonical JSON plus LF with `additionalProperties:false` and exactly `{schema_version:"dgkma-development-target-v1",target_kind:"development",target_fingerprint,current_database:"heliumdb",current_user_name,current_user_oid,server_version_num,pg_tuple_digest,approval_source_commit,observed_at,receipt_sha256}`. `pg_tuple_digest=sha256(RFC8785({host:lower(PGHOST),port:canonical_unsigned_decimal(PGPORT),database:lower(PGDATABASE),user:lower(PGUSER)}))`; neither password nor raw host is stored. `approval_source_commit` is the exact Todo-1 baseline commit. `receipt_sha256=sha256(RFC8785(the object excluding only receipt_sha256))`; the trailing LF is outside the self-hash. Todos 3/17 descriptor-open and reproduce every field in addition to requiring `development|heliumdb|PG*`; a different role or endpoint with the same database name is rejected. No SQL is generated during apply.

Additive alterations to existing tables:

| Existing table | Exact additive contract |
| --- | --- |
| `users` | Add `user_uid uuid NOT NULL DEFAULT gen_random_uuid()` plus rule token `user_uid_unique`; add `email_canonical text GENERATED ALWAYS AS (lower(btrim(email))) STORED` plus `email_canonical_nonblank` CHECK `(email IS NULL OR btrim(email)<>'')` and `email_canonical_unique`; add `phone_canonical text GENERATED ALWAYS AS (CANONICAL_PHONE(phone_number)) STORED`, `phone_canonical_source_valid` CHECK `(phone_number IS NULL OR btrim(phone_number)='' OR phone_canonical IS NOT NULL)`, `phone_canonical_format` CHECK `(phone_canonical IS NULL OR phone_canonical ~ '^[0-9]{10,11}$')`, and partial `phone_canonical_unique` WHERE `phone_canonical IS NOT NULL`; add `birthday_type_domain` CHECK `(birthday_type IS NULL OR birthday_type IN ('SOLAR','LUNAR'))`. Existing columns/uniques remain. Actor snapshots copy `user_uid` and display name. |
| `categories` | Add rule token `badge_variant_domain` with literal CHECK `(badge_variant IN ('default','secondary','destructive','outline'))`; add `sort_order_nonnegative` with CHECK `(sort_order >= 0)`. |
| `payments` | Add `amount_positive` CHECK `(amount > 0) NOT VALID`, `year_domain` CHECK `(year BETWEEN 2024 AND 2100) NOT VALID`, `type_domain` CHECK `(type IN ('연회비','기타')) NOT VALID`, `status_domain` CHECK `(status IN ('pending','completed','failed')) NOT VALID`, and btree index rule token `user_id_fk_support` on `(user_id)`. New writes are enforced immediately. Sequence 20 adds all four checks and, when the locked raw violation count is zero, validates all four in the same transaction and records `validated`; otherwise it records `not_valid_exceptions_present`. No legacy payment is rewritten/deleted; an open exception makes that row `ineligible` for automatic accounting migration, keeps the checks unvalidated, and blocks Production cutover until the optional sequence-65 validation artifact succeeds after a separately approved source-data remediation. |
| `alumni_database` | Add `mobile_canonical text GENERATED ALWAYS AS (CANONICAL_PHONE(mobile)) STORED`, `mobile_canonical_source_valid` CHECK `(mobile IS NULL OR btrim(mobile)='' OR mobile_canonical IS NOT NULL)`, `mobile_canonical_format` CHECK `(mobile_canonical IS NULL OR mobile_canonical ~ '^[0-9]{10,11}$')`, partial `mobile_canonical_unique` on `(mobile_canonical)` WHERE `mobile_canonical IS NOT NULL`, and partial `matched_user_id_unique` on `(matched_user_id)` WHERE `matched_user_id IS NOT NULL`; existing raw `mobile` remains. The existing `matched_user_id→users(id)` FK remains `ON DELETE NO ACTION ON UPDATE NO ACTION`; account deletion must explicitly NULL it before deleting the user. |
| `posts` | Add btree index rule tokens `category_id_fk_support` on `(category_id)` and `author_id_fk_support` on `(author_id)`; no column or FK semantics change. |
| `comments` | Add btree index rule tokens `post_id_fk_support` on `(post_id)` and `author_id_fk_support` on `(author_id)`; no column or FK semantics change. |
| `obituaries` | Add btree index rule token `author_id_fk_support` on `(author_id)`; no column or FK semantics change. |
| `community_events` | Existing baseline already has nullable `legacy_obituary_id integer` and its UNIQUE constraint; do not add either. Add only FK `legacy_obituary_fk` to `obituaries(id) ON DELETE SET NULL ON UPDATE RESTRICT`, reusing the existing UNIQUE as support, btree index rule token `author_id_fk_support` on `(author_id)`, plus `event_type_domain` CHECK `(event_type IN ('obituary','wedding','opening','other'))` and `status_domain` CHECK `(status IN ('draft','published'))`. |
| `event_parse_rate_limits` | Add `request_count_nonnegative` CHECK `(request_count >= 0)` and `updated_after_window_start` CHECK `(updated_at >= window_started_at)`. Keep the existing `user_id→users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE` FK and PK, but add the account-deletion guard: the closed user-FK registry classifies the row as `explicit`, locks it by numeric `user_id`, and the account-deletion command must issue `event_parse_rate_limit:delete` with one operation result/correlation/audit row before deleting the user. A guarded BEFORE DELETE trigger requires that exact root target-user context and rejects a direct delete or an unenumerated parent-cascade attempt; therefore the final user delete observes no remaining child row and the FK's legacy cascade action is preserved only as a catalog-compatible fallback, never as an unaudited execution path. |
| `pending_registrations` | **Sequence 20:** alter `status` to `text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending'`; add `status_domain` CHECK `(status IN ('pending','approved','rejected'))`; add `email_canonical text GENERATED ALWAYS AS (lower(btrim(email))) STORED`; add `email_canonical_nonblank` CHECK `(btrim(email)<>'')`; add partial `pending_kakao_id_unique` on `(kakao_id)` WHERE `status='pending'`, partial `pending_email_canonical_unique` on `(email_canonical)` WHERE `status='pending'`, and retention index `(created_at,id)`. **Sequence 30 after `business_operation_receipts`:** retain existing `kakao_id|email|name NOT NULL` and existing nullable `user_data`; add nullable `pii_digest char(64)`, nullable `pii_digest_version text`, nullable `pii_redacted_at timestamptz`, and nullable `pii_redaction_operation_uid uuid` FK to `business_operation_receipts(operation_uid) ON DELETE RESTRICT ON UPDATE RESTRICT`. Redacted rows must satisfy `status='rejected'`, `pii_digest_version='pending-registration-v1'`, nonnull time/digest/operation UID, exact placeholders and `user_data IS NULL`; non-redacted rows require all four redaction fields NULL and retain normal application validation. Account deletion does not infer a user FK: under the locked target user it enumerates every row with byte-equal `kakao_id`, prelocks by ID with `{pending_id,kakao_id_digest}`, emits one explicit delete result/audit per row and rechecks the set before DML. |
| `kakao_oauth_states` | Add `expiry_after_start` CHECK `(expires_at > started_at)` and retention index `(expires_at,state_hash)`; keep PK/UNIQUE. |
| `kakao_identity_terminations` | Add retention index `(terminated_at,identity_hash)`; keep PK. |
| `session` | Keep columns/PK and existing `session_expire_idx`; add retention index `(expire,sid)` so stable batches use the literal tuple. Account deletion binds only the authenticated request's exact current SID: the root payload stores `sha256(sid)`, the procedure locks that PK after rank-20 resolution, verifies the digest, emits one `session:delete` result/audit and deletes it. It never scans/parses session JSON or claims to delete other sessions; after the user row is absent, any stale session's `userId` fails the ordinary live-user authentication lookup and retention removes it. |
| all other existing tables | No column deletion/rename/type narrowing. Runtime creation moves to the ledger; current FK delete semantics remain except the explicit additions above. |

Every alteration rule token above is expanded through the deterministic object-name rule as `<table>__<rule-token>__{check|key|idx|fkey}`; its literal type/default/generated expression/predicate/validation state is part of the manifest byte stream. Sequence 20 contains exactly the unlabelled and `Sequence 20` tokens, including the eight explicit existing-FK support indexes printed in this table (`posts` two, `comments` two, `payments` one, `obituaries` one, `community_events` one, and `alumni_database.matched_user_id` via its partial UNIQUE); `event_parse_rate_limits(user_id)` and `community_events(legacy_obituary_id)` reuse their baseline PK/UNIQUE and emit no index. The `pending_registrations` retention-nullability/digest/time/operation-FK/check tokens are the sole labelled Sequence-30 existing-table extension and are emitted only after the referenced operation table. A missing token, inferred type, alternate generated syntax, implicit predicate or additional existing-table alteration fails manifest lint.

Brownfield baseline registration requires the exact pre-change catalog fingerprint below; clean bootstrap never inserts a baseline shortcut. |

Continuation after a verified partial run creates a new release UID with a strictly greater requested-through boundary, revalidates prior ledger bytes, and starts at the first missing sequence; a run never changes its boundary or claims later artifacts. |

UNIQUE `(target_fingerprint,receipt_sha256)`; UNIQUE `(target_fingerprint,preflight_run_uid)`; CHECK kind `development|disposable-test`; disposable requires parent/run UID, development requires both NULL; probe token must equal the exact preimage formula; both probe booleans must equal the literal rollback-only probe outcomes; canonical JSON is redacted/non-secret, excludes receipt SHA, and its separately computed SHA must match; FK-free bootstrap, append-only. Production inspection is represented only by the external dossier above. |

A successor is exactly `open→resolved` with `resolved_actor` or `open→waived` with `waive_actor`, retains tuple/detected time and has exactly one audit row. A `pre_anchor_blocking` root cannot receive a successor in this plan; payment exceptions are never auto-resolved by source mutation. |

Its `expected_results` array must be byte-equal after RFC-8785 normalization to `result_entity_keys` and to the complete ordered `business_operation_entities` rows; its `reservation_slots` array is the sole durable reserved-ID record. CHECK actor scope `admin|member_self|migration_admin`. Only the `account:delete` root may populate the two target-user columns; it requires both equal the actor ID at insertion, and every enumerated child inherits the snapshot through its operation context. Every other operation requires both target-user columns NULL. Result is RFC-8785 canonical nonempty ordered array of `{ordinal,entity_type,entity_key,entity_action,action_correlation_uid}`. Root correlation identifies the command only and never authorizes an audit action. The transaction entry procedure locks/validates/snapshots the live actor and optional self-delete target, preallocates IDs/correlations, inserts the receipt/results, and performs DML. The receipt and canonical payload are append-only except the declared parent-driven live actor/target FK nulls, which do not alter payload bytes. A same-UID retry first authenticates the live actor, then descriptor-reads the stored payload and is permitted only when the canonical external command projection, actor/authorization/target/root/result bytes and every server-derived payload field reproduce exactly; it returns the stored result/slots without `nextval`. After account deletion nulls the live actor/target FK, retry returns terminal `actor_deleted_operation_not_retryable` and performs no DML. Historical receipts remain readable only through a separately authorized admin/audit query, never as retry authorization. |

Every business trigger requires its entity/action, action correlation and receipt payload digest to match the transaction-local operation/result context; exactly one matching audit row uses that action correlation. A later operation may address the same entity under a different action without colliding. |
| `mutable_entity_action_history` | nullable `association_member_id bigint`, nullable `accounting_period_id bigint`, `mutation_no integer`, `action text`, `from_state text`, `to_state text`, `effective_at timestamptz`, `before_digest char(64)`, `after_digest char(64)`, `reason_code text`, `ACTOR(mutation_actor,admin|member_self)` | exactly one target FK is nonnull; both FKs `RESTRICT`; mutation number `>=1`; partial UNIQUE `(association_member_id,mutation_no) WHERE association_member_id IS NOT NULL` and `(accounting_period_id,mutation_no) WHERE accounting_period_id IS NOT NULL`. Member actions are exactly `end:active→ended`, `reactivate:ended→active`, or `correct:state→same-state`; period actions are exactly `reconcile:open→reconciling`, `close:reconciling→closed`, or `reopen:closed→open`. `effective_at` equals the canonical operation payload instant; for member end/reactivate it is respectively the new inactive/active boundary, and correction cannot alter activity. Period actions and ordinary member actions require `admin`; `member_self` is legal only for a member `correct` inside the enumerated `account:delete` operation, must clear exactly that target user's live `user_id`, preserve `alumni_record_id` and every other member field, and inherits the root self-delete authorization snapshot. A rejected set has no downstream root and may be replaced only by a fresh complete preview set after its live key is released. CHECK allocation kind `dues|special_assessment`; CHECK effect `payment|cancellation|refund`; CHECK rights mode `immediate_positive|next_month_negative|retroactive_error`; CHECK status `proposed|approved|rejected`; CHECK nullable correction kind only `reassignment`; CHECK nullable correction reason only `source_error|false_transaction`; amount `>0`. `correction_reason_code IS NOT NULL` iff rights mode is `retroactive_error`; every other mode requires it NULL. Cancellation requires receipt+original, forbids funding/receipt-reversal, uses a negative mode, and matches the original's member/year/receipt/kind/assessment/group member/amount. Refund requires receipt+original+funding event+receipt reversal, uses a negative mode, and matches original identity fields. Partial UNIQUE `(correction_group_uid,effect_kind)` and deferred trigger require exactly that pair and equal amount. A true amount change is not a noncash correction: decrease uses a cash refund for the delta or reassignment of the full original amount, and increase uses a new payment; no hidden delta exists.
