UUID ownership is closed by the single registry in Global rules. This heading adds no second category: Todo 2 must prove that the ten logical-source UUID constants have no default and reproduce across every target; `schema_release_runs.release_uid`, `schema_capability_receipts.preflight_run_uid`, `source_decision_sets.decision_set_uid`, `economic_event_parties.party_uid`, `dues_receipts.receipt_uid` and `dues_allocations.request_uid` are no-default globally unique caller UUIDv4 values; `member_match_cases.case_uid` and `dues_group_members.group_member_uid` are no-default caller UUIDv4 values on version 1 and repeat only across their own VCHAIN successors; `schema_capability_receipts.disposable_run_uid` is nullable/no-default and target-bound; `dues_allocations.correction_group_uid` is nullable/no-default and pair-scoped; every other VCHAIN logical UUID repeats only across its own versions; actor/user/platform snapshots are no-default non-unique references. Any incompatible default/key or unregistered UUID fails lint.

Literal constraint/transition expansion:

- SHA columns use `CHECK (value ~ '^[0-9a-f]{64}$')`; positive codes/versions/years use the ranges printed in their row. Every state/type set is exactly the literal set printed in its row. Non-VCHAIN terminal transitions are a closed table, never `IS DISTINCT FROM`: `economic_events|bank_transfer_matches|dues_receipts|dues_receipt_reversals|dues_payment_groups|dues_allocations` permit only `OLD.status='proposed' AND NEW.status IN ('approved','rejected')`; `member_match_candidates` only `candidate→approved|rejected`; `cashbook_entries` only `draft→approved|discarded`; `bank_reconciliations` only `draft→approved|rejected`; `source_decision_sets` only `previewed→approved|rejected` or `approved→superseded`; `accounting_import_batches` only `previewed→applied|failed`; `bank_reconciliation_carryforwards` is VCHAIN and follows its separate registry. Each one-shot branch requires only its same-named optional actor bundle. The repeatable mutable rows are a separate closed class: `association_members` and `accounting_periods` may update only through one append-only `mutable_entity_action_history` row per operation with the exact action/pair/digests printed above. Once a one-shot row is approved/rejected/discarded/failed/superseded, it is immutable except the source-decision approved→superseded branch or a declared mechanical parent-delete actor FK null; corrections use the printed append-only child/reversal/VCHAIN path. For a VCHAIN table, `OLD` means the locked `supersedes_id` parent and `NEW` means the inserted successor—no UPDATE occurs; the same literal predicate controls the successor's optional actor bundle. A transition trigger rejects a populated wrong bundle, a missing required bundle, terminal-state rewrite, or reuse of one actor bundle for two actions except an explicitly combined `decision_actor`, whose audit action is still state-specific.
- `dgkma_classification_descendant_count(coordinate_id bigint)` is a stable, non-`SECURITY DEFINER`, fixed-`search_path` SQL function over locked rows. It counts distinct claim roots plus distinct event roots reached from every classification version for the coordinate, regardless of whether a financial child exists, and additionally checks explicit `UNION ALL` branches for `bank_transactions|cashbook_entries|dues_receipts|dues_receipt_reversals|dues_payment_groups|dues_allocations|bank_transfer_matches|bank_reconciliation_items|economic_event_canonicalizations` and reversal events. Todo 2 prints the complete generated SQL from that closed table list and fails if a claim/event/financial FK has no branch. Classification quarantined→approved or semantic supersession requires the result zero under all corresponding class locks; after claim creation every classification and source-decision semantic byte is immutable.
- `association_members`: `CHECK ((status='active' AND ended_at IS NULL) OR (status='ended' AND ended_at IS NOT NULL AND ended_at>=joined_at))`. Partial unique indexes are on `user_id` and `alumni_record_id` wherever the value is nonnull, independent of member status; an ended member may retain its one live identity link, but one user/alumni row can never be current on two members.
- `member_match_cases/candidates`: the approval trigger locks case then candidates by ID and requires exactly one candidate with `status='approved'`; that candidate's member must be active. `evidence_kind='name_only'` can never approve. Rejection/approval writes the decision actor and audit event atomically.
- `member_identity_link_history`: `link|correct` locks and validates its populated historical ID/UID snapshot branch against then-live parent rows but deliberately creates no FK from the history row to those deletable parents. `unlink_user|unlink_all` retain all historical snapshots; the result kind alone determines which live member FKs remain. Account deletion uses only `unlink_user`, clearing user while preserving alumni when the parent result was both. Active identity overlap is computed from the derived successor interval and current result kind and is prevented by deferred chain/member checks; no prior row receives an end-time or parent-delete UPDATE.
- `dues_position_tier_mappings` has exactly three mutually exclusive CHECK branches. Ordinary is `(adds_obligation=true AND priority>0 AND policy_id IS NOT NULL AND ((position_code='president' AND tier_code='president') OR (position_code='senior_vice_president' AND tier_code='senior_vice_president') OR (position_code IN ('vice_president','auditor','general_assembly_chair') AND tier_code='vice_president_auditor_chair') OR (position_code ~ '(^|_)director$' AND position_code NOT LIKE 'branch_%' AND tier_code='director') OR (position_code='member' AND tier_code='member')))`. Thus the owner phrase “모든 `*이사`” means exactly the canonical English code regex `(^|_)director$`, which includes the six frozen 22nd codes `general_affairs_director|planning_director|legal_director|external_cooperation_director|public_relations_director|director`; any future matching code is an obligation director, while branch/class codes never match. Honorary is `(adds_obligation=true AND position_code='member_kind:honorary' AND tier_code='honorary' AND priority=0 AND policy_id IS NOT NULL)`. Secondary is `(adds_obligation=false AND tier_code IS NULL AND priority=0 AND policy_id IS NULL AND position_code IN ('busan_branch_president','busan_branch_vice_president','busan_branch_general_affairs','busan_branch_finance','class_1_captain','class_3_captain','class_7_captain','class_41_captain','class_41_vice_captain','class_42_captain','class_42_vice_captain'))`. No ordinary cross-product outside those pairs is valid.
- On mapping approval the trigger locks policy then mapping VCHAIN scopes and requires the referenced policy to be the approved current tip with exact year/tier/priority equality. `member_dues_tier_history` requires `obligation_from=make_date(dues_year,1,1)`, `obligation_to=make_date(dues_year+1,1,1)`, `mapping.policy_id=policy_id`, and exact year/tier/priority equality across history, mapping and policy. For an officer mapping, `basis_assignment_id` is mandatory and its current-as-of assignment tip must belong to the same member, have `position_code=mapping.position_code`, have become known/effective by the tier row's `effective_at`, and overlap the KST dues-year interval. For `member` or `member_kind:honorary`, basis is NULL and the member kind must match.
- The tier derivation trigger locks every current-as-of assignment/mapping candidate for that member/year, adds the baseline member-kind candidate only when an append-only `member_activity_events`-derived active interval overlaps the KST dues-year interval, ignores `adds_obligation=false`, and requires the chosen mapping priority to equal `MAX(priority)` across every obligation assignment that has overlapped that dues year and was known by the derivation instant. Ties must resolve to the same tier/policy or block. A later-discovered higher appointment appends a new tier-history version; a lower/ended role never lowers that year's maximum. Current/as-of selection uses immutable VCHAIN tips/order plus immutable activity events, so an incorrect lower tier, inactive-year baseline or another member's assignment cannot be inserted.
- `dues_policies`: `CHECK (monthly_minimum>=0 AND annual_minimum>=0 AND priority>=0 AND due_day=10 AND reminder_day=11)`; its VCHAIN approval requires nonblank resolution ref, approved time and full approval actor but no mapping. Mapping approval is the second step and requires the approved same-year/tier policy, eliminating circular approval. `dues_pledges` requires first-of-month bounds and non-overlap for confirmed tips. Every rights-snapshot amount is nonnegative and is recomputed from the exact evaluation contract below; in particular `policy_shortfall=GREATEST(monthly_required_to_date-paid_total,0)` and `pledge_shortfall=GREATEST(pledge_target_to_date-paid_total,0)`.
- Import batch apply locks the batch and source release, re-hashes the current source fingerprint, and requires equality with the preview. For a new digest, the row-version trigger joins its origin `row.batch_id→batch.source_release_id→release.logical_source_id` and `row.coordinate_id→coordinate.logical_source_id` and requires exact equality. Every observation, including identical-content reuse, inserts one `accounting_import_batch_rows` link whose batch release source, coordinate source, row-version coordinate and preview digest/status all match; indexes exist on every joined FK path. A source-backed position row additionally requires the exact approved member-match decision item/case/candidate chain printed in its table contract; that chain's row version, content digest and selected member must equal the position source/member, and its descendants participate in replacement-set zero-child checks. A mapping/policy row with `source_row_version_id` requires that row's coordinate source equal its own `source_logical_id` and authority role respectively `policy|policy`. A group member requires the exact roster-batch/source equality printed in its row. Coordinate version trigger accepts identical digest by reusing the existing row version, otherwise requires `supersedes_id` equal to the locked structural tip; two tips fail.
- Bank source/account binding is executable, not a display seed: the immutable manifest map contains exactly `(BANK_TOSS_2026,TOSS_OFFICER_2026)` and `(BANK_IBK_2026,IBK_ASSOCIATION_2026)`. Bank transaction/anchor triggers join source row→coordinate→logical source, require `authority_role='bank'`, `event_authority_rank=300`, the mapped account ID and source validity interval, and reject every unmapped pair. The transaction row stores the normalized instant and requires it to equal both its event instant and source instant. Composite supporting indexes are `(logical_source_id,id)` on coordinates, `(source_release_id,id)` on batches, `(coordinate_id,batch_id,id)` on row versions and `(account_id,occurred_at,id)` on bank transactions.
- Event claim locks the three adjacent KST-date candidate keys as specified, while source-row version identity supplies claim uniqueness. The claim trigger derives, rather than accepts, amount/date/direction from the logical source's normalized payload schema and requires exact equality; `dues_year` is present only when the normalized row is dues-attributed. A **dues event** is a credit event with an approved `DUES_INCOME` cashbook tip/receipt/allocation or the source-backed bank debit selected for a `DUES_REFUND`; before classification it is merely a proposed bank/historical/compatibility event. Every source-event approval requires the claim state shape above, a current authority select/supersede tip, positive amount, an approved active cashbook classification totaling the event, and full actor/audit bundle. A bank refund follows that same ordinary source pipeline through proposed event/provenance/authority/`bank_transactions`; the later `receipt:refund` command locks that existing event and original receipt, proves `reverses_event_id`, amount/year/member and DUES_REFUND classification equality, then adds/approves the receipt reversal, cashbook and refund allocations and finally approves the event under deferred equations. No claimless refund or standalone `economic_event:reverse` command exists in v1. A non-refund source/classification error that cannot be handled by immutable-equivalent cashbook correction or noncash allocation cancellation is quarantined and blocks close until a separately approved future remediation design; the executor may not synthesize a generic reversal. The refund event is new; the original remains approved, cumulative approved reversal amount cannot exceed it, and cycles fail. Proposed→rejected requires the complete deferred child count to be zero; approved cashbook children and balance/report-visible bank transactions require an approved parent event at commit. Late higher authority appends candidate provenance plus a VCHAIN decision; it never updates an authority flag.
- Legacy decision CHECK is `cross_link → candidate_event_id only`, `new_compatibility_event → created_event_id only`, other decisions → neither; payment ID is globally unique. Period transitions lock the period; `closed` blocks later financial mutations unless a same-transaction reopen audit exists.
- Bank transfer approval locks both transactions by ID, requires different accounts, debit/credit directions, equal event/declared amounts and no prior approved use of either leg. Bank reconciliation approval additionally recomputes the exact source-bound anchor/ending/active-cashbook equations below; zero difference or zero unresolved items alone is insufficient.
- Cashbook/receipt/group/allocation triggers execute the literal equations and global lock graph below. Receipt/event/group natural keys are the printed UNIQUEs. An allocation's payment/refund/cancellation shape is enforced with the three mutually exclusive branches. For each approved receipt reversal R, `R.refund_event_id` is unique, every linked approved refund allocation has `funding_event_id=R.refund_event_id`, and locked `SUM(refund allocation.amount)=R.amount=SUM(active approved DUES_REFUND cashbook-tip amount on R.refund_event_id)`; cancellation+refund capacity can never exceed the original payment allocation.
- Audit entity-key trigger uses `^[1-9][0-9]*$` for every manifest/existing numeric row ID, `^payments:[1-9][0-9]*$` only for `legacy_payment_decision`, `^session:[0-9a-f]{64}$` for the HMAC-free SHA-256 session-row locator, and `^kakao_identity_termination:[0-9a-f]{64}$` for the already-hashed termination identity. Before/after JSON keys are checked against the exact group allowlists above and forbidden PII/account/credential keys.
- Every append-only table has a shared BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE trigger that permits only parent-driven nulling of declared `ON DELETE SET NULL` actor FKs and operation-receipt live actor/target FKs; all business corrections otherwise require a new superseding/reversal row. A nonnull `association_members.user_id` must be cleared explicitly by a history-backed `member:correct` before its parent user is deleted; if an FK action nevertheless attempts to NULL that live identity column, the member trigger rejects it. The sole existing-row business mutation outside that list is the one-way `pending_registrations` retention redaction with exact OLD/NEW fields, operation receipt and audit event defined above; it cannot be reversed or applied twice. The fallback temporal variant implements the same predicates with deferred constraint triggers and canonical lock order as the preferred EXCLUDE variant.

Physical expansion is decision-complete:

- Todo 2 must emit fully expanded YAML—no macro tokens remain in the committed manifest. Expansion is set-based by physical column/constraint name: an explicit and macro-derived definition with identical bytes coalesces once; different definitions for the same name are fatal. Every CHECK gets the deterministic name rule above and a literal SQL expression in YAML; every composite key lists ordered columns; every interval lists `[)` bounds and its exact predicate. The executor may not infer columns from English prose.
- FK dictionary (all new FKs `ON UPDATE RESTRICT`): ledger `release_run_id→schema_release_runs` and `capability_receipt_id→schema_capability_receipts`; operation `business_operation_entities.operation_uid|pending_registrations.pii_redaction_operation_uid→business_operation_receipts.operation_uid`; mutable history `mutable_entity_action_history.association_member_id→association_members|accounting_period_id→accounting_periods`, plus `member_activity_events.member_id→association_members|mutation_history_id→mutable_entity_action_history`; match/source fields `source_row_version_id|row_version_id→accounting_import_row_versions`, including batch-row/classification/decision/party-alias/position rows; source-decision `decision_set_id→source_decision_sets`, `decision_item_id→source_decision_items`; import-version self `accounting_import_row_versions.supersedes_id→accounting_import_row_versions ON DELETE RESTRICT`; member fields `member_id→association_members`; nonmember party fields `party_id|event_party_id→economic_event_parties`; case/candidate fields `root_case_id|case_id|match_case_id→member_match_cases` and `match_candidate_id→member_match_candidates`, with `member_match_candidates.root_case_id` required to target a `version=1` root and `case_uid_snapshot` checked against it; live identity fields `association_members.user_id→users ON DELETE SET NULL`, `association_members.alumni_record_id→alumni_database ON DELETE RESTRICT`; every `*_user_id` emitted by ACTOR/AUDIT_ACTOR plus `business_operation_receipts.actor_user_id|actor_target_user_id→users ON DELETE SET NULL`; `member_identity_link_history.linked_user_id_snapshot|linked_alumni_record_id_snapshot` are validated historical scalars and intentionally have no FK; policy fields `policy_id→dues_policies`, `mapping_id→dues_position_tier_mappings`, `basis_assignment_id→member_position_assignments`, `tier_history_id→member_dues_tier_history`, `pledge_id→dues_pledges`, `assessment_id→member_assessments`; source fields `source_logical_id|logical_source_id→accounting_logical_sources`, `source_release_id→accounting_source_releases`, `coordinate_id→accounting_import_coordinates`, `batch_id|preview_batch_id|roster_batch_id→accounting_import_batches`; event fields `event_id|candidate_event_id|created_event_id|canonical_event_id|duplicate_event_id|reverses_event_id|refund_event_id|funding_event_id→economic_events`, `selected_provenance_id→economic_event_provenance`; legacy `legacy_payment_id→payments`; period `period_id|target_period_id→accounting_periods`; bank `account_id|target_account_id→bank_accounts`, `anchor_id→bank_balance_anchors`, `ending_transaction_id|debit_transaction_id|credit_transaction_id|bank_transaction_id|resolved_by_transaction_id→bank_transactions`; category `category_id→accounting_categories`; cash correction `corrects_entry_id→cashbook_entries`; receipt `receipt_id|refund_receipt_id→dues_receipts`; receipt reversal `receipt_reversal_id→dues_receipt_reversals`; group `group_id→dues_payment_groups`, `group_member_id→dues_group_members`; allocation `reverses_allocation_id→dues_allocations`; reconciliation `supersedes_reconciliation_id|reconciliation_id|to_reconciliation_id→bank_reconciliations`, `from_item_id→bank_reconciliation_items`; every VCHAIN `supersedes_id` targets its own table, including identity-link/classification/reconciliation-carryforward chains. All are `ON DELETE RESTRICT` except the live association-member user link and ACTOR/AUDIT_ACTOR/operation-receipt live actor/target FKs, which are `ON DELETE SET NULL`. Existing `alumni_database.matched_user_id→users(id)` remains `ON DELETE NO ACTION ON UPDATE NO ACTION`; account deletion explicitly NULLs it under the operation/audit contract before deleting the user.
- The dictionary additionally requires `bank_source_account_mappings.account_id→bank_accounts`; `accounting_periods.boundary_source_row_version_id|boundary_evidence_row_version_id→accounting_import_row_versions` and `accounting_periods.decision_item_id→source_decision_items`; `member_match_cases.decision_item_id→source_decision_items`; `source_row_classification_decisions.member_match_case_id→member_match_cases`, `.member_match_candidate_id→member_match_candidates`, `.member_match_decision_item_id→source_decision_items`; `dues_receipts.decision_item_id→source_decision_items`, `.legacy_decision_id→legacy_payment_decisions`; `dues_allocations.legacy_decision_id→legacy_payment_decisions`; `dues_group_members.match_candidate_id→member_match_candidates`; `economic_event_collisions.lower_event_id|higher_event_id→economic_events`; `economic_event_collisions.canonicalization_id→economic_event_canonicalizations`; and every new VCHAIN self FK. All are `RESTRICT` with a left-prefix btree index. `business_operation_receipts.actor_user_id_snapshot` and all other `*_snapshot` scalars are deliberately non-FK. Manifest lint fails a missing or extra FK/index.
- Self-version FKs emitted by `VCHAIN` always target the same physical table. Every FK gets a btree index unless the FK columns are already the leftmost prefix of a PK/UNIQUE index; the validator records `reused_constraint_name` instead of creating a duplicate. Exact existing-table FK indexes required by sequence 20 are `posts(category_id)`, `posts(author_id)`, `comments(post_id)`, `comments(author_id)`, `payments(user_id)`, `alumni_database(matched_user_id)` via its new partial UNIQUE, `obituaries(author_id)`, and `community_events(author_id)`. `event_parse_rate_limits(user_id)` reuses its PK. `community_events(legacy_obituary_id)` reuses the existing UNIQUE and must not receive a duplicate index.
- Actor/action matrix: creation/import actors are mandatory for every business row through the global default; explicit transition actors are additionally mandatory for source-decision preview/item-create/approve/reject/supersede, party/alias creation, match candidate/case decisions, classification approval/quarantine/resolve/supersession, mapping/policy/category approval, group-member decision, import apply, event claim bind/reject, event/provenance/authority-decision/canonicalization approval, member end/reactivate/correct, period reconcile/close/reopen, transfer/reconciliation approval **and rejection**, carryforward bind/resolve, cashbook approve/discard, receipt/reversal/group/allocation approval **and rejection**, legacy decision creation plus later resolution, schema-exception resolve/waive and pending-registration redaction. Repeatable member/period actions take their actor/correlation only from the new mutable-history row; one-shot transitions take the matching optional bundle. Each explicit business action maps to one literal audit entity/action and requires exactly one matching audit row; `accounting_audit_events` uses `AUDIT_ACTOR` and is the sole nonrecursive endpoint. Ordinary `member_self` is pledge-only and active-member bound; the complete account-deletion result set instead uses the root target-user self-delete rule for active/ended/no-member cases. Permitted parent-table UPDATEs are only a history-backed member/period mutation or the expressly auditless parent-driven FK null declared above. Account deletion first appends `identity_link:unlink_user` and the history-backed `member:correct`, explicitly clears `association_members.user_id`, and only then deletes the user; it never clears the alumni FK merely because the user is deleted. Each exception requires matching transaction-local context and byte-identical untouched fields/snapshots; parent-triggered actor nulls additionally require `pg_trigger_depth()>1`, while a history-backed member/period UPDATE uses the ordinary top-level operation procedure and direct SQL attempts fail.
- Actor authorization version is deterministic. Let `target=dgkma_current_target_fingerprint()` recompute the exact target formula and match the committed capability receipt. Admin/migration and ordinary target-member actions store `sha256('authz-v1'||E'\n'||target||E'\n'||user_id::text||E'\n'||lower(user_uid::text)||E'\n'||scope||E'\n'||CASE WHEN users.is_admin THEN '1' ELSE '0' END||E'\n'||member_uid_or_empty)`, where an ordinary `member_self` requires the bound active member UID. Account deletion instead uses the exact `member-self-delete-v1` RFC-8785 object printed in `AUDIT_ACTOR`, including target fingerprint and target user ID, for the root and every child. A stale UID/admin/member link, changed linked-member set or different target changes the digest and rejects the action. Disposable synthetic actors use the admin formula plus their target-bound user row; Production cannot synthesize.
- Correlation is exact, and receipt order is deliberately distinct from execution order. `business_operation_entities.ordinal` is only the canonical idempotency/hash order sorted by the printed rank/entity/key/action tuple; it is never a DML schedule. After all locks, the receipt and canonical result rows are inserted, the generated closed command registry derives a separate dependency-topological `execution_steps` list of references to those already stored result rows, and the server/runner follows that list. Immediately before each business/audit action it resolves that result's correlation by `(operation_uid,entity_type,entity_key,entity_action)` and executes `set_config('dgkma.correlation_uid', action_correlation_uid, true)`. Every execution step references exactly one canonical result, every canonical result appears exactly once, and a generated validator rejects missing/duplicate/cyclic steps or a dependency inversion such as approve-before-create; no rank lock is acquired during DML. Correlations are server-generated UUIDv4 values stored in `business_operation_entities`, never accepted from HTTP/import payloads. Every action trigger requires the current GUC to equal its result row's action correlation; exactly one audit tuple may use it. `root_correlation_uid` is command tracing only and is rejected as an action GUC. Missing/reused/mismatched correlation or one correlation covering two results fails deferred commit.
- Exact audit mapping is a one-row/one-action closed set. A source-decision set uses `preview|approve|reject|supersede`; a source-classification root uses exactly `approve|quarantine` from its approved-set decision item, a quarantined→approved successor uses `resolve`, and a same-state successor uses `supersede`. A claim root uses `create`; its first terminal successor uses exactly `bind|reject`; later evidence-only correction uses `supersede`. Candidate/case/group-member terminal successors use exactly `approve|reject`; policy/mapping/category/assessment draft roots use `create`, and their approved successors use `approve`; event/transfer/receipt/reversal/group/allocation/reconciliation terminal transitions use one of `approve|reject`; cashbook uses `approve|discard`; an import batch uses `apply|fail`, while a human decision-set rejection uses `source_decision_set:reject` and never an import-batch reject action; period uses `reconcile|close|reopen`; carryforward uses `bind|resolve|supersede`; schema-exception/collision use `resolve|waive|supersede` only when their printed successor predicate matches. Root INSERT action predicates are mutually exclusive and literal: every `economic_events` root uses `create`, including a proposed bank-refund event whose `reverses_event_id` is nonnull; reversal semantics are represented only by the later `dues_receipt_reversals` root/action, never by `economic_event:reverse`. `cashbook_entries` is `correct` iff `corrects_entry_id IS NOT NULL`, otherwise `create`; every `dues_receipt_reversals` root is `reverse`; `dues_allocations` is `reverse` iff `effect_kind='refund'`, `correct` iff `effect_kind='cancellation' OR correction_group_uid IS NOT NULL`, otherwise `create`; `bank_reconciliations` is `supersede` iff `supersedes_reconciliation_id IS NOT NULL`, otherwise `create`. Each branch requires its printed shape, recorded actor and one correlation; overlap or no match fails before DML. Parent-driven actor-FK nulls are excluded by the exact mechanical exception and demand no action. No DML result can demand two actions/correlations/audit rows.
- The VCHAIN actor/action transition registry is exhaustive and mutually exclusive. `schema_data_exceptions` version-1 `open` is actorless/auditless; only `open→resolved`=`resolved_actor/resolve` and `open→waived`=`waive_actor/waive` follow. `member_match_cases` root=`recorded_actor/create`, nonterminal→`approved|rejected`=`decision_actor/approve|reject`, terminal same-state correction=`supersede_actor/supersede`; `member_identity_link_history` root or successor uses its base `decision_actor` and action equal to exact operation `link|correct|unlink_user|unlink_all`; `member_position_assignments` root=`recorded_actor/create`, successor=`recorded_actor/supersede`. `dues_position_tier_mappings|dues_policies|accounting_categories` draft root=`recorded_actor/create`, `draft→approved`=`approval_actor/approve`, `approved→approved`=`supersede_actor/supersede`. `member_dues_tier_history` root=`derived_actor/create`, successor=`derived_actor/supersede`; `dues_pledges` root=`recorded_actor/create`, successor=`recorded_actor/supersede`; `member_assessments` draft root=`recorded_actor/create`, `draft→approved`=`approval_actor/approve`, `approved→reversed`=`reversal_actor/reverse`, `approved→approved`=`supersede_actor/supersede`; `dues_status_snapshots` root=`recorded_actor/create`, successor=`recorded_actor/correct`.
- The non-VCHAIN lifecycle registry is equally closed. Root INSERT requires exactly: `member_match_candidates.status='candidate'`, `accounting_import_batches.status='previewed'`, `source_decision_sets.status='previewed'`, `economic_events|bank_transfer_matches|dues_receipts|dues_receipt_reversals|dues_payment_groups|dues_allocations.status='proposed'`, `cashbook_entries|bank_reconciliations.status='draft'`; every optional transition actor bundle is wholly NULL on that root. Only the printed one-step transition may populate exactly one matching optional bundle, and terminal rows are immutable. Direct INSERT in `approved|rejected|applied|failed|discarded` state, a populated transition bundle on a root, or a state UPDATE without the matching operation result/correlation/audit fails immediately. The manifest emits one named CHECK plus INSERT/UPDATE trigger per listed table and the negative fixtures exercise every forbidden terminal-root shape.
- Source/compatibility transitions are closed as follows. `source_decision_sets` root=`preview_actor/preview`, `previewed→approved|rejected`=`approval_actor/approve|rejection_actor/reject`, and `approved→superseded`=`supersede_actor/supersede`; human rejection leaves the batch previewed, and no other set update exists. `accounting_import_row_versions` root=`recorded_actor/create`, successor=`recorded_actor/supersede`. `source_row_classification_decisions` root exists only for an approved or quarantined item and uses base `decision_actor` with action `approve|quarantine`; an item outcome `reject` creates no classification row. `quarantined→approved` uses only `resolve_actor/resolve`; every same-state successor uses only `supersede_actor/supersede`; no rejected root or other pair exists. `economic_event_claims` root=`claim_actor/create`, `open→bound|rejected`=`decision_actor/bind|reject`, terminal same-state correction=`supersede_actor/supersede`. `economic_event_authority_decisions` root uses base `decision_actor` and `decision_kind select|quarantine→select|quarantine`; a successor with `decision_kind=supersede` uses base actor/action `supersede`, while a quarantine successor uses base actor/action `quarantine`. `economic_event_collisions` open root=`recorded_actor/create`, unresolved `open→open` review successor=`recorded_actor/supersede` with reason `COLLISION_REVIEW_REQUIRED`, `open→resolved`=`recorded_actor/resolve`, and resolved correction=`recorded_actor/supersede`; no other collision pair exists. `legacy_payment_decisions` root uses base `decision_actor` and `quarantine→quarantine`, every other root→`create`; `review|quarantine→cross_link|new_compatibility_event|ineligible`=`decision_actor/resolve`, every other successor=`decision_actor/supersede`. `legacy_cutover_states` root `legacy`=`recorded_actor/create`, `legacy→fenced`=`recorded_actor/fence`, `fenced|read_rollback→new`=`recorded_actor/cutover`, `new→read_rollback`=`recorded_actor/read_rollback`.
- Remaining VCHAIN transitions are closed as follows. `dues_group_members` root=`recorded_actor/create`, nonterminal include→terminal include=`decision_actor/approve|reject`, every terminal correction including approved include→approved cancel=`supersede_actor/supersede`. `bank_reconciliation_carryforwards` open root=`recorded_actor/create`, `open→bound`=`binding_actor/bind`, `bound→resolved`=`resolution_actor/resolve`, resolved correction=`supersede_actor/supersede`. Any parent/new pair, actor bundle or action outside this registry fails before audit insertion; a predicate overlap is a manifest-lint error and a deferred-constraint failure.
- The literal map is exhaustive: identity/dues—`association_members→member:create|end|reactivate|correct`, `member_match_cases→match_case:create|approve|reject|supersede`, `member_match_candidates→match_candidate:create|approve|reject`, `member_identity_link_history→identity_link:link|correct|unlink_user|unlink_all`, `member_position_assignments→position:create|supersede`, `dues_position_tier_mappings→position_mapping:create|approve|supersede`, `member_dues_tier_history→dues_tier:create|supersede`, `dues_policies→dues_policy:create|approve|supersede`, `dues_pledges→pledge:create|supersede`, `member_assessments→assessment:create|approve|reverse|supersede`, `dues_status_snapshots→rights_snapshot:create|correct`; source—`accounting_logical_sources→logical_source:create`, `accounting_source_releases→source_release:create`, `bank_source_account_mappings→source_account_mapping:create`, `accounting_import_batches→import_batch:preview|apply|fail`, `accounting_import_coordinates→source_coordinate:create`, `accounting_import_row_versions→source_row:create|supersede`, `accounting_import_batch_rows→source_batch_row:create`, `source_decision_sets→source_decision_set:preview|approve|reject|supersede`, `source_decision_items→source_decision_item:create`, `economic_event_parties→event_party:create`, `economic_event_party_aliases→event_party_alias:create`, `source_row_classification_decisions→source_row_classification:approve|quarantine|resolve|supersede`, `economic_event_claims→event_claim:create|bind|reject|supersede`, `economic_event_provenance→event_provenance:create`, `economic_event_authority_decisions→event_authority_decision:select|supersede|quarantine`, `economic_event_canonicalizations→event_canonicalization:create`, `economic_event_collisions→event_collision:create|resolve|supersede`; finance—`economic_events→economic_event:create|approve|reject`, `accounting_periods→period:create|reconcile|close|reopen`, `bank_accounts→bank_account:create`, `bank_balance_anchors→bank_balance_anchor:create`, `bank_transactions→bank_transaction:create`, `bank_transfer_matches→bank_transfer:create|approve|reject`, `accounting_categories→category:create|approve|supersede`, `cashbook_entries→cashbook_entry:create|approve|discard|correct`, `dues_receipts→receipt:create|approve|reject`, `dues_receipt_reversals→receipt_reversal:reverse|approve|reject`, `dues_payment_groups→group:create|approve|reject`, `dues_group_members→group_member:create|approve|reject|supersede`, `dues_allocations→allocation:create|approve|reject|reverse|correct`, `bank_reconciliations→reconciliation:create|approve|reject|supersede`, `bank_reconciliation_items→reconciliation_item:create`, `bank_reconciliation_carryforwards→reconciliation_carryforward:create|bind|resolve|supersede`; compatibility/ops—`legacy_payment_decisions→legacy_payment_decision:create|quarantine|resolve|supersede`, `legacy_cutover_states→legacy_cutover:create|fence|cutover|read_rollback`, `schema_data_exceptions→schema_exception:resolve|waive`, `pending_registrations→pending_registration_redaction:redact`; account deletion—`users→account:delete`, `event_parse_rate_limits→event_parse_rate_limit:delete`, `kakao_identity_terminations→identity_termination:create`, `session→session:delete`, `community_events→community_event:delete_draft|null_author`, `posts→post:null_author`, `comments→comment:null_author`, `obituaries→obituary:null_author`, `alumni_database→alumni_record:unlink_user`, `pending_registrations→pending_registration:delete`, `payments→legacy_payment:null_user`. Every account-deletion action resolves the root target-user snapshot; each linked member also emits `identity_link:unlink_user` and `member:correct` to clear only its live user FK. Sequence-15 exception roots are ledger capture before audit. If two predicates match or none matches, the write fails.
- Audit JSON is optional but, when present, is filtered before insert through these exact groups; extra keys or values not equal to the live OLD/NEW row fail. `identity` (`member|match_case|match_candidate|identity_link`) allows `{id,member_uid,status,reason_code,evidence_digest,effective_from,effective_to,user_uid_snapshot,alumni_id_snapshot}`; `dues` (`position|position_mapping|dues_tier|dues_policy|pledge|assessment|rights_snapshot`) allows `{id,member_id,dues_year,position_code,tier_code,policy_id,version,status,amount,monthly_minimum,annual_minimum,effective_at,effective_from,effective_to,due_date,rights_status,reason_code,derivation_digest}`; `source` (`logical_source|source_release|import_batch|source_coordinate|source_row|source_batch_row|source_decision_set|source_decision_item|event_party|event_party_alias|source_row_classification|event_claim|event_provenance|event_authority_decision|event_canonicalization|legacy_payment_decision`) allows `{id,source_code,source_kind,source_row_version_id,coordinate_id,batch_uid,decision_set_uid,decision_kind,decision_payload_sha256,party_uid,status,state,classification_kind,party_kind,decision,evidence_role,authority_rank_snapshot,content_digest,source_fingerprint,row_count,warning_count,error_count,reason_code}`; `financial` (`economic_event|period|bank_account|bank_balance_anchor|bank_transaction|bank_transfer|category|cashbook_entry|receipt|receipt_reversal|group|group_member|allocation|reconciliation|reconciliation_item|reconciliation_carryforward`) allows `{id,event_uid,receipt_uid,request_uid,carry_uid,account_id,target_account_id,target_period_id,event_id,member_id,dues_year,category_code,direction,event_kind,effect_kind,allocation_kind,resolution_status,resolution_kind,amount,gross_amount,balance,bank_balance,ledger_balance,difference,status,posted_date,occurred_at,effective_at,statement_end_at,reason_code,row_fingerprint,snapshot_digest,revalidation_digest}`; `schema_exception` allows `{id,table_name,row_key,rule_code,row_digest,status,resolution_code}`; `pending_registration_redaction` allows only `{id,status,pii_digest,pii_digest_version,pii_redacted_at,pii_redaction_operation_uid}`. Display names, subject/payer names, descriptions, notes, noncash explanations, locators, raw/normalized payloads, contact/address/workplace data, access tokens and account identifiers are never allowed.
- The source allowlist also covers `source_account_mapping|event_collision|legacy_cutover` and permits only `{id,source_code,account_id,status,phase,watermark_payment_id,reason_code}` for those entities; the financial allowlist additionally permits `note_code,resolution_reason_code,supporting_document_digest` for reconciliation item/carryforward only and contains no `note|noncash_explanation` key. Collision IDs enter only through redacted numeric row identity, never payer/source payload. `schema_exception` adds `exception_uid,version`; `group_member` adds `group_member_uid,version,effect_kind`. Account-deletion entities allow only `{id,action,row_digest}` where `row_digest` is over PII-free row identity/action fields. No name, locator, raw ETag, explanation text or normalized payload is admitted.

- `source_row_classification`, `reconciliation_carryforward` and `pending_registration_redaction` are included in the audit entity/action CHECK and their respective source/financial/schema-redaction JSON allowlists; `business_operation_receipts` is the nonrecursive idempotency primitive and does not itself require another operation/audit receipt.

Exact brownfield normalization contract:

- Canonical email is `lower(btrim(email))`; a non-null email that normalizes to `''` is an exception. Canonical Korean phone/mobile uses the exact `CANONICAL_PHONE` generated expression: remove every non-digit, convert exactly `82` followed by `10` and eight digits to leading `010`, accept only `^0[0-9]{9,10}$`, map absent/blank raw to NULL, and reject every other nonblank form. The shared TypeScript normalizer fixtures must equal the generated SQL expression byte-for-byte in outcome; clients never choose canonical values.
- Sequence 15 materializes `(row id, canonical digest, conflict count, rule code)` into `schema_data_exceptions` and commits before any conflicting UNIQUE can be attempted. Sequence 20 repeats those queries under the schema advisory lock; any duplicate non-null canonical email/phone/mobile or pending identity key leaves the durable sequence-15 exceptions intact and aborts before DDL. No winner is selected and no raw value is rewritten. NULL cannot bypass validation for a nonblank raw phone/mobile because the generated-column companion CHECK rejects it.
- Sequence-15 exception classes are exact. `pre_anchor_blocking` covers every predicate named by its closed registry: blank/duplicate canonical user email, invalid/duplicate user phone, unknown birthday type, invalid category badge/sort order, invalid/duplicate alumni mobile or duplicate alumni match, orphan/type/status community event, invalid event-rate counters/times, blank/duplicate pending identity, NULL/unknown pending status and invalid OAuth expiry. Any such row is terminal before sequence 20 and cannot be resolved/waived in this plan. `legacy_not_valid` covers only pre-existing `payments.amount_positive|year_domain|type_domain|status_domain` violations; these do **not** block required sequence 20, but they make the row ineligible for accounting migration, keep the four checks NOT VALID, and block Production rollout/sequence 65. Sequence 15 must produce exactly one registered rule code per failing row/predicate and abort on an uncovered predicate. Sequence 20 repeats every raw locked `pre_anchor_blocking` query independently of exception status and requires zero; it separately proves that every raw legacy violation has an exact durable exception, adds the four payment checks, and validates all four in the same transaction iff the raw count is zero. Otherwise its sidecar records the exact violation count/digest and `not_valid_exceptions_present`. No sequence-15 row is a permission to mutate source data.
- `pending_registrations.status IS NULL` is therefore not normalized. NULL and unknown values remain unchanged as `pre_anchor_blocking`; the target default/NOT NULL/CHECK and two pending partial UNIQUEs are added only after a separately approved remediation/new baseline yields zero raw blockers. Todo 9, artifact sequence 15/20, Todo 6/7 and their negative fixtures use these same two literal class names and rules.

Exact date/time normalization:

- A source date `YYYY-MM-DD` used as an effective start becomes `YYYY-MM-DDT00:00:00+09:00`; an inclusive source end date becomes the next calendar day at `00:00:00+09:00` for `[)` storage. Unknown/month/year precision remains text plus precision and cannot be promoted to a day. Economic-event calendar dates are computed as `(occurred_at AT TIME ZONE 'Asia/Seoul')::date`.
- The 21st source end text `2026-01-31` remains immutable source evidence, while the separately authorized normalization row uses `effective_to='2026-02-28T12:38:00+09:00'`. For the 22nd 총회의장 and 감사, `source_appointment_date='2026-02-27'` and `effective_from='2026-02-28T12:38:00+09:00'`; all other exact 22nd start dates use KST midnight.
- Bank-account validity is half-open. `TOSS_OFFICER_2026` is `[2026-01-01,2026-03-17)` so 2026-03-16 remains included; `IBK_ASSOCIATION_2026` is `[2026-03-16,∞)`. The one-day overlap is intentional for transfer matching and never implies duplicate event authority.

Exact global lock graph:

- The literal class-rank map is: `10 actor_user`, `15 kakao_identity`, `20 business_operation`, `23 event_parse_rate_limit`, `24 kakao_identity_termination`, `25 session`, `26 community_event`, `27 post`, `28 comment`, `29 obituary`, `30 schema_exception`, `35 alumni_database`, `40 pending_registration`, `50 legacy_cutover`, `60 legacy_payment`, `70 legacy_decision`, `80 logical_source`, `90 source_account_mapping`, `100 source_release`, `110 import_batch`, `120 import_coordinate`, `130 import_row`, `135 source_decision_set`, `136 source_decision_item`, `140 source_classification`, `145 event_party`, `146 event_party_alias`, `150 association_member`, `160 event_claim`, `170 identity_link`, `180 match_case`, `190 match_candidate`, `200 position_assignment`, `210 dues_policy`, `220 dues_mapping`, `230 dues_tier`, `240 pledge`, `250 assessment`, `260 rights_snapshot`, `270 accounting_period`, `280 bank_account`, `290 bank_anchor`, `300 candidate_day`, `310 economic_event`, `320 event_provenance`, `330 authority_decision`, `340 event_collision`, `350 event_canonicalization`, `360 bank_transaction`, `370 transfer_match`, `380 reconciliation`, `390 reconciliation_item`, `400 reconciliation_carryforward`, `410 accounting_category`, `420 cashbook_entry`, `430 receipt`, `440 receipt_reversal`, `450 payment_group`, `460 group_member`, `470 original_allocation`, `480 new_allocation`, `490 audit_event`. Manifest lint requires every business table/action and every existing-table account-deletion action to name exactly one rank and forbids an unlisted class.
- Schema apply first computes `schema_lock_digest=sha256("dgkma-schema-lock-v1\n"+target_fingerprint)`, converts its first 16 lowercase hex digits to signed two's-complement `bigint`, and takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock`; manifest SHA is deliberately excluded so competing manifests serialize on the same target. Under that lock it rechecks the target fingerprint, the one-nonterminal-run predicate and every prior ledger/manifest checksum before selecting/inserting a release run. Every business transaction acquires only touched classes in the numeric order above.
- Every VCHAIN advisory key uses `sha256('vchain-v1|'||table_name||'|'||canonical_scope_json)`, sorted by all 64 lowercase hex characters and converted from the first 16 hex characters to signed two's-complement `bigint`. Member/match/policy/dues classes use the same transform and order; no trigger or service owns a private lock namespace. Actor-user row locks are also sorted by their full canonical business-lock digest and always precede every association-member/business lock.
- Every business class has one canonical lock key. Existing/preallocated row identities use `sha256(RFC8785({lock_version:"business-lock-v1",class,key_kind:"row_id",key:"<canonical unsigned decimal id>"}))`; the operation key is `sha256(RFC8785({lock_version:"business-lock-v1",class:"business_operation",key_kind:"operation_uid",key:"<lowercase uuid>"}))`; a not-yet-materialized stable key is `sha256(RFC8785({lock_version:"business-lock-v1",class,key_kind:"stable_code_or_uid",key:"<NFC code or lowercase uuid>"}))`; an existing session key is `sha256(RFC8785({lock_version:"business-lock-v1",class:"session",key_kind:"session_digest",key:sha256(sid)}))`; VCHAIN and candidate-day use their separately printed preimages. These are the only key kinds. Every touched key is sorted by the **full 64-hex digest** within its numeric rank, and row locks are acquired in that digest order; raw numeric ID order is never a competing rule. `dgkma_lock_advance` stores the full digest, converts the first 16 hex to the advisory bigint, and rejects a decreasing rank/digest or unlisted class.
- Account deletion follows the same order but resolves its entire target set before any rank-20 acquisition. Todo 2 emits `account_delete_user_fk_registry` with one row for **every and only actual** `pg_constraint` FK to `users(id)`: `{table,column,on_delete,class,rank,key_projection,mechanical_or_explicit}`. Manifest lint compares it bidirectionally to the target catalog and fails any missing/extra FK, including every ACTOR/OPTIONAL_ACTOR/AUDIT_ACTOR user column, `business_operation_receipts.actor_user_id|actor_target_user_id`, `accounting_audit_events.actor_user_id`, live association/alumni/payment identity, existing author FKs and `event_parse_rate_limits.user_id ON DELETE CASCADE`. `session`, `pending_registrations` and `kakao_identity_terminations` are explicitly forbidden in this FK registry because they have no `users(id)` FK. `mechanical` is allowed only for an actor/target FK physically declared `ON DELETE SET NULL`; `event_parse_rate_limits.user_id` and every live identity/content change are `explicit`.
- Todo 2 separately emits a closed `account_delete_non_fk_registry`, validated against the literal manifest rather than `pg_constraint`, with exactly three entries: authenticated `session.sid` (`rank=25`, `action=explicit_delete`, key projection `{sid_digest}`); every `pending_registrations.kakao_id` byte-equal to the locked target identity (`rank=40`, `action=explicit_delete`, key projection `{pending_id,kakao_id_digest}`); and the same target identity's `kakao_identity_terminations.identity_hash` (`rank=24`, `action=prospective_insert`, key projection `{identity_hash}`). The two registries are disjoint and their sorted union—not either registry alone—is the account-delete target-set/prelock digest; every explicit row becomes exactly one operation result and every mechanical FK null remains only in the declared mechanical subset.
- Kakao-identity concurrency is a first-class rank-15 lock. `kakao_identity_digest=HMAC-SHA256(ACCOUNTING_PII_HMAC_KEY_V1,"kakao-identity-lock-v1\n"||NFC(kakao_id))`; its business lock uses the printed stable-key transform with class `kakao_identity` and the lowercase digest as key. After locking the target user at rank 10, account deletion acquires this rank-15 key before enumerating either registry or rank-20 operation keys. Every route/command that can insert, approve, reject, redact or delete a pending registration for that Kakao ID, or insert/check its termination row, must acquire the identical rank-15 key before reading/writing either table. Pending creation under the lock rejects an existing termination and rechecks pending uniqueness before insert; therefore a concurrent post-enumeration pending phantom either commits before deletion's locked enumeration or waits and then fails after termination. The same lock and exact raw equality under it—not a digest collision—govern row selection.
- Under rank 10/15 the procedure enumerates all actual-FK and non-FK rows and computes the exact sorted `{rank,class,key_digest,table,primary_key,column_or_identity,mechanical_or_explicit}` prelock digest. Rank 20 is acquired once by combining the new account-delete operation key with every referenced historical operation row key and sorting their full digests; the current UID never goes first by privilege. It then acquires **every enumerated row across the complete printed class map from rank 23 through rank 490**, skipping empty classes and sorting full digest within rank; the short application-deletion path is not a competing whitelist. It re-enumerates both registries under locks and requires a byte-identical union/digest before inserting the root receipt. For each linked member it appends `unlink_user`, adds the member-correction history/audit and clears only `association_members.user_id`; a surviving `alumni_record_id` remains live. It creates the exact termination row, explicitly deletes each enumerated `event_parse_rate_limits` row through `event_parse_rate_limit:delete`, then performs pending/session deletes, the existing draft-event delete, author nulling, payment `user_id` nulling, `alumni_database.matched_user_id` unlink and final user delete in the registered dependency order. Every explicit business mutation is an exhaustive operation result. Before delete, the root payload stores the sorted exact mechanical subset `{table,primary_key,column}` in `inputs.mechanical_actor_fk_null_targets`; those registry-enumerated rows are already rank-locked and byte-checked, but FK nulling emits no result/correlation/audit. The append-only trigger accepts only that declared subset with matching account-delete context and `pg_trigger_depth()>1`; the rank-490 audit actor FK is included and its immutable snapshots remain. The rate-limit DELETE guard requires its exact operation result/correlation/audit and proves no target rate-limit row remains before `users` deletion, so an unaudited CASCADE never occurs. A registry/catalog/literal mismatch, post-enumeration FK or non-FK child, rank-15 omission, opposite order, residual rate-limit child or direct delete aborts.
- For account deletion specifically, “entire target set before rank 20” means the complete pre-existing actual-FK rows, pre-existing session/pending rows and declared prospective termination action under the rank-15 identity lock. Immediately after rank-20 ID/correlation reservation, the operation deterministically adds the prospective actor-FK targets created by this same command to the hashed mechanical-null set, as defined in the Round-32 closure contract. The second comparison immediately before `users` deletion is against the union of both registry projections plus same-operation prospective actor targets; the earlier phrase “before inserting the root receipt” cannot exclude that receipt, termination or same-operation support/audit rows.
- Every shorthand phrase elsewhere saying “rank-10/20 locks” is governed by this exact order: rank 10 live actor/target rows → rank 15 Kakao identity when and only when the command touches pending/termination identity → the complete sorted rank-20 operation set. A command not touching Kakao identity has no rank-15 key and proceeds directly 10→20. Numeric/correlation reservation, committed-retry lookup and payload construction occur only after every applicable 10/15/20 lock and before rank 23; Todo 20 tests both the 10→20 and 10→15→20 shapes. No pending/termination command may treat rank 15 as an optional private lock.
- Every application business mutation runs through generated non-`SECURITY DEFINER` transaction-entry procedure `dgkma_<entity>_<action>(operation_uid uuid, canonical_payload jsonb)`. A single helper `dgkma_lock_advance(class_rank integer,key_digest char(64))` stores transaction-local `(last_rank,last_key)`; it rejects any decreasing class rank, any decreasing key within a repeated class, unknown rank, malformed key, or direct business trigger lacking its monotonic receipt. The procedure performs an optimistic read-only resolution of the touched graph, locks the live actor at rank 10 and the complete sorted set of current/historical operation keys at rank 20, and checks for a committed receipt. A byte-identical committed retry returns stored results after fresh actor/target authentication and reserves no ID. For a new command it reserves every required numeric ID under those rank-10/20 locks, derives the final stable/numeric touched-key set and payload/result bytes, then acquires all rank-23–490 locks in the printed order. Under those locks it re-resolves the graph and requires the object-existence plan, existing IDs, source/version tips and stable-key projection to equal the optimistic snapshot; drift aborts without a receipt and any consumed sequence values remain harmless gaps. Only then does it store/recheck the prelock digest, insert the operation receipt/result links and perform dependency-topological DML. No stable/numeric rank-23–490 lock is acquired before ID reservation, and no new lock is acquired during DML. BEFORE triggers reject missing/stale/out-of-order receipts in normal code paths. Because the approved topology deliberately uses one database owner and creates no separate runtime role, this is **not** claimed as a hostile-owner security boundary: that trusted owner could forge a GUC, disable a trigger or issue direct DML. The enforceable application boundary is server authorization plus repository lint/tests/catalog drift; introducing a non-owner runtime credential requires a future secret/role rollout. The plan no longer claims table privilege separation from its owner.
- Operation idempotency is universal and generated from one manifest action registry. The external/per-action command schema is `business-operation-command-v1`, has `additionalProperties:false`, and accepts exactly `{schema_version,operation_uid,entity_type,action,actor_user_id,actor_scope,actor_target_user_id_or_null,target_fingerprint,effective_at,inputs}`; it never accepts expected results, correlations, reserved IDs or reservation slots. After actor/operation locks and numeric/correlation reservation, the server constructs the canonical receipt payload with exact `additionalProperties:false` shape `{schema_version:"business-operation-payload-v2",operation_uid,entity_type,action,actor_user_id,actor_scope,actor_target_user_id_or_null,target_fingerprint,effective_at,inputs,expected_results,reservation_slots}` and hashes/stores only those v2 bytes. Only `entity_type="account",action="delete",actor_scope="member_self"` permits a nonnull target and requires it to equal `actor_user_id`; every other payload uses JSON null. `inputs` contains every non-generated, non-ID, non-actor, non-time business column supplied for that action; a successor instead contains its stable parent entity key plus every new business value. Server-derived IDs, correlations, snapshots, hashes, totals and timestamps are forbidden in external input and recomputed under locks. `expected_results` is a nonempty array sorted by `(global lock rank,entity_type UTF-8,canonical entity_key UTF-8,entity_action)`, then assigned contiguous `ordinal=1..N`; numeric keys are canonical unsigned decimals, session/termination/payment keys use their exact formats above, and each item has exactly `{ordinal,entity_type,entity_key,entity_action,action_correlation_uid}`. `reservation_slots` is the contiguous `slot_ordinal=1..M` array sorted by the reservation key below; each item has exactly `{slot_ordinal,phase,result_ordinal,slot_kind,slot_kind_order,qualified_table_name,local_ordinal,sequence_name,reserved_id}` where `phase=0|10|20|30`, `result_ordinal=0` only for the operation-receipt slot and otherwise references one expected-result ordinal, `slot_kind=receipt|business|history_or_activity|audit` maps one-to-one to `slot_kind_order=0|10|20|30`, table/sequence names are validated lowercase schema-qualified identifiers, `local_ordinal` is a positive contiguous integer within the same phase/result/kind/table, and `reserved_id` is a canonical positive decimal **string**. The payload hash covers the whole exact array; a committed retry compares it byte-for-byte and performs no `nextval`, while an uncommitted retry may reserve different IDs and therefore produces new v2 bytes only after confirming no receipt exists. The server preallocates every numeric key/correlation and resolves existing canonical keys before hashing the v2 payload, so receipt payload/result bytes are final before any rank-23 lock or business DML.
- Composite receipt commands are a closed non-audited command layer over audited results: `dues_year:activate`, `source_decision_set:repreview|supersede`, `member_identity:change`, `allocation:correct`, `receipt:refund`, `import_batch:preview|apply|fail`, `event_collision:resolve`, `period:reconcile`, `legacy_payment:materialize`, `reconciliation:create|supersede|approve`, and `account:delete`. Human rejection is the single-result `source_decision_set:reject` command, not an import-batch command. The receipt command itself has no audit row; every result item uses only an entity/action from the exhaustive literal audit map and has exactly one correlation/audit row. Single-result commands use their result's entity/action. Any unlisted composite command, result action outside the map, or command/result topology not equal to the following expansion fails manifest lint and transaction entry.
- Numeric-ID reservation is exact and is the only exception to “server-derived IDs after receipt.” After the complete rank-10 actor and sorted rank-20 operation locks, after committed-retry lookup, and before any rank-23–490 lock or payload hashing, the procedure expands the manifest-owned `reservation_slots` array for the complete optimistic execution graph. It resolves every slot's identity sequence with `pg_get_serial_sequence(qualified_table,'id')`, requires exact catalog equality, sorts by `(phase,result_ordinal,slot_kind_order,qualified_table_name,local_ordinal)` where phase is `0 operation receipt|10 result business|20 support|30 audit` and slot-kind order is `0 receipt|10 business|20 history_or_activity|30 audit`, and calls `nextval` once per slot. This includes `business_operation_receipts.id`, every result-created business/VCHAIN row, every support history/activity row and exactly one audit row per result; the composite-key `business_operation_entities` table is the only no-slot table. The manifest validator proves a bijection between slots and all new identity rows in the command's dependency graph. It never calls `setval`, never accepts an ID from the caller, and inserts the reserved value with `OVERRIDING SYSTEM VALUE`; the target owner must have sequence USAGE/UPDATE. The complete reserved array participates in the final lock-key set, payload and results before the first rank-23 lock. PostgreSQL sequence advancement is deliberately nontransactional, so graph drift or an aborted first attempt may leave gaps but no receipt/business row. A retry with no committed receipt reserves fresh IDs; a retry with a committed receipt reuses stored results and performs no `nextval`. A competing operation that wins a stable key causes the loser’s post-lock graph recheck to abort rather than substitute another ID or mutate its payload. This rule applies to every `GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY` table and leaves ordinary application inserts without `OVERRIDING SYSTEM VALUE` database-generated.
- The registry explicitly expands multi-result operations; the following order is the dependency-topological `execution_steps`, while receipt ordinals remain independently canonical-sorted. Annual policy activation executes all policy approvals, all mappings by `position_code`, tier-history rows by `member_id`, then missing default pledges by `member_id`. Source-decision replacement executes old-set supersede → new-set preview → new items by source coordinate/kind → new-set approve → permitted quarantine successors or first roots for formerly rejected items. Member identity change executes the identity-link root/successor → matching history-backed `member:correct` → live member FK change; its activity state is unchanged. Allocation reassignment executes original cancellation → replacement, ordered by original-allocation ID then new ID. Bank cash refund consumes the already imported proposed refund event and executes cashbook draft → receipt-reversal proposed → refund allocations proposed by original-allocation ID → cashbook approve → receipt-reversal approve → allocations approve → refund event approve; every step has its own mapped result/action except the previously committed import results. Import preview is one committed registered composite in immediate-FK order: optional source release → batch root referencing that existing/new release → coordinates → new-or-reused row versions → batch-row links → decision-set root → complete items. Human reject transitions only the set and leaves the batch previewed for a fresh complete set; source-integrity failure alone transitions the batch failed. A later single- or multi-batch apply executes all decision-set approvals → all batch applies → approved period materializations →, for each coordinate, match case/candidate creation and approvals or party/alias creation → classification → open claim root → proposed event → bound claim successor → provenance → authority → bank transaction if applicable → financial drafts and their approvals. Companion roster batches are discovered/locked/applied with their primary bank batch exactly as printed. Immediate classification/match/party/event FKs therefore exist before each referencing insert, and every provenance insert sees the required bound claim. The exact refund-classified bank branch stops after its proposed event, bound claim, provenance, authority and bank-transaction results and creates no cashbook/reversal/allocation/approval until `receipt:refund`. Rank ordering governs only pre-acquired locks. Collision resolution executes chosen duplicate `economic_event:reject` → `event_canonicalization:create` → collision resolved successor; all three results/audits are mandatory and the canonical event remains proposed for the later ordinary financial publish/approval topology. Period reconcile executes current-period `reconcile` mutation/history → next-period `create` at the identical boundary. Legacy decision-only materialization executes open claim root → proposed compatibility event → bound claim successor → provenance → authority → terminal legacy-decision successor → cashbook/receipt/allocation drafts bound to that successor → cashbook/receipt/allocation approvals → event approval. Every amount/member/year/source digest is prebound. Reconciliation draft creation executes reconciliation root → complete items → `open→bound` carry successors; approval executes reconciliation approve → missing open carry roots. A superseding revalidation executes reconciliation supersede → complete items → same bindings and reuses stable carry chains. Account deletion executes all present `identity_termination:create` rows, then each linked member's `identity_link:unlink_user→member:correct`, then `event_parse_rate_limit:delete`, `community_event:delete_draft|null_author`, `post:null_author`, `comment:null_author`, `obituary:null_author`, `legacy_payment:null_user`, `alumni_record:unlink_user`, `pending_registration:delete` for locked target-Kakao matches, the exact authenticated `session:delete`, and finally `account:delete`; rows within one action are ordered by canonical entity key, absent row classes contribute no result, every explicit affected row appears exactly once, and mechanical actor-FK nulls are bound in the root input list after same-operation prospective IDs/correlations are reserved. Support-only member activity rows execute adjacent to their parent member create/end/reactivate and reuse that result/audit as printed. Every other action has exactly the single result printed in the exhaustive actor/action map. The generator materializes every named composite template, proves a bijection with canonical results/reservation slots and topologically sorts immediate FKs plus deferred dependencies; a runtime byte mismatch fails before DML. Same UID returns stored results without new DML/audit only after fresh authentication of the still-live same actor and byte-identical canonical payload, actor snapshots, authorization version, target and ordered results; any mismatch fails. A parent-deletion-null actor makes retry terminal `actor_deleted_operation_not_retryable`. Correlation UUID remains per-action audit linkage and can never substitute for the durable operation UID.
- Re-preview is the only way to create a fresh set after human rejection. `source_decision_set:repreview` is legal only for an existing `accounting_import_batches.status='previewed'` batch whose prior live set is terminal `rejected`; it locks/reuses that batch, release, coordinate, row-version and batch-row graph, then creates one fresh `source_decision_sets(status='previewed')` root and its complete fresh items by `(coordinate,kind)`. It creates no batch/release/coordinate/row-version/batch-row or downstream business row. Its canonical results are exactly `source_decision_set:preview` plus every `source_decision_item:create`; its reservation slots are the operation receipt, the new set/item identity rows and one audit per result; its execution steps are set root then items. It requires a new set UID, complete strict manifest and full item payload array under the API contract below. A missing/extra item, stale/reused UID, changed source row, non-rejected prior set or attempt to call `import_batch:preview` for the existing batch fails before DML.
- `receipt:refund` has the one strict action-specific input object `{refund_event_uid,receipt_uid,original_allocation_request_uids,correction_reason_code_or_null,correction_evidence_sha256_or_null}`. The original-allocation request UIDs are a nonempty, sorted, duplicate-free array of lowercase UUID strings and every other field is a lowercase UUID, JSON null or lowercase SHA-256 as named; amount, member, year, assessment, reversal row, allocation IDs and effective instants are DB-derived from the locked event/receipt/original allocations. The two correction fields are both NULL for an ordinary refund, which gives every refund allocation `rights_effect_mode='next_month_negative'`, NULL code and the approved refund event's `occurred_at`. They are both nonnull only when the code is exactly `source_error|false_transaction` and the evidence digest reproduces the locked source-decision/classification correction evidence; then every refund allocation in that operation stores the identical code, `rights_effect_mode='retroactive_error'` and its own original payment allocation's `effective_at`. Mixed modes/codes/evidence, a code without evidence, evidence without a code, caller-supplied amount/time/ID, or an original outside the locked receipt/member/year/capacity set fails before DML. The canonical payload, receipt/result expansion and every refund allocation/audit digest include the nullable pair.
- Advisory key conversion is exact: create the three full lowercase SHA-256 candidate keys for KST date−1/date/date+1, sort by all 64 hex characters, convert the first 16 hex characters to signed two's-complement `bigint`, and call `pg_advisory_xact_lock` in that order. Matching candidate events are then row-locked by their full rank-310 canonical digest; the full candidate key remains in the predicate, so a 64-bit collision serializes safely but cannot merge identities.
- Approval code and deferred triggers may acquire a suffix of this graph only after `dgkma_lock_advance` proves no earlier-class lock is already held out of order. Event provenance/authority decisions, canonical roots, category version tips, transfer legs, reconciliation items/carryforwards and all VCHAIN tips use this graph. The complete bank-source import/approval **lock acquisition** workflow is `actor user→all operation keys→logical source→source release→import batch→import coordinate→row-version tip→source decision set→decision item→classification→event party/alias when nonmember→association member when member→claim→policy VCHAIN→mapping VCHAIN→tier VCHAIN→accounting period→bank account→bank-balance anchor(if the row supplies one)→candidate advisory keys→economic event→provenance→authority decision→bank transaction→category VCHAIN→cashbook→receipt→group→group member→new allocation→audit`; batch-row links reuse their batch/coordinate/row locks. The bank-refund command locks the already imported graph in that same rank order through bank transaction, then category→cashbook→receipt→receipt reversal→original allocation→new refund allocation→audit. These arrows never prescribe DML order; DML follows the separate registry above after the complete lock set is held. Every adjacent class pair and every operation-UID/nonexistent-row key is tested in both operation shapes; PostgreSQL deadlock detection, lock timeout, unordered acquisition or a private advisory transform is a failure rather than a retry-success.

Exact artifact and release manifest:

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

| 10 | `current-schema-baseline-v1` / `migrations/0001_current_schema_baseline.sql` | Drizzle / ordinary | 1 | clean and brownfield use identical bytes; clean creates the 13 baseline tables, brownfield executes exact `IF NOT EXISTS` DDL only after the catalog gate proves no definition drift |

| 20 | `existing-integrity-v1` / `migrations/0020_existing_integrity.sql` | Drizzle / ordinary | 15 | requires zero raw `pre_anchor_blocking` violations, adds identity objects and the four payment CHECKs `NOT VALID`, then immediately `VALIDATE CONSTRAINT` for all four when the locked raw legacy-violation count is zero; otherwise complete `legacy_not_valid` capture is required and the ledger sidecar records `not_valid_exceptions_present` |
