| 50 | `accounting-reference-seed-v1` / `migrations/data/0050_accounting_reference_seed.sql` | reviewed data SQL / ordinary data | 40 | requires explicit `--actor-user-id` matching Todo 17's post-sequence-40 Development actor receipt (or the same-target disposable synthetic receipt) and re-proves that UID/user/role/target at transaction start; inserts all ten logical-source identities but releases only the two currently approved editorial sources (`MEMBERSHIP_INTEGRATED_ADDRESS_BOOK`, `NOTION_ORGANIZATION_ROLE_HISTORY`), plus two bank accounts, the exact two source→account mapping rows, 16 draft policies, 46 draft position mappings and six draft categories. The other eight releases remain absent until Todo 18 obtains each source profile and mapping approval. Category approval is a named post-50 admin transition in Todo 17; dues policy/mapping approval remains separately board-authorized. |
| 60 | `database-security-v1` / `migrations/manual/0060_database_security.sql` | reviewed SQL / manual | 50 | exact one-owner database/schema/relation/sequence/routine/default-privilege matrix below, fixed function `search_path`, RLS off and no unexpected PUBLIC object access; its sidecar additionally binds standalone restore-only `migrations/manual/0060_restore_security_reconcile.sql` by `restore_reconcile_sha256`, but that file is not a ledger artifact |
| 65 | `legacy-payment-check-validation-v1` / `migrations/0065_validate_legacy_payment_checks.sql` | Drizzle / optional ordinary | 60 | pre-generated but not startup-required; may run only after a separately approved data-remediation plan leaves zero raw violations and zero open `legacy_not_valid` tips, then validates all four checks atomically and records `validated`; already-validated targets are a checksum-verified no-op |

- Artifact kind is exact per sequence: `1|40|60=manual`, `10=baseline`, and `15|20|30|50|65=ordinary`; sequence 65 additionally carries `required_for_startup=false|required_for_production=true`. The manifest and ledger reject every other assignment. A release run is inserted in `preflight` with immutable `requested_through_sequence_no`, moves to `applying` immediately before its first not-yet-ledgered required artifact at or below that boundary, and may then move only to `verified|failed`. `verified` is legal only when every manifest-required sequence `<= requested_through_sequence_no` is present with matching bytes; it does not assert later sequences. Development therefore records a verified through-40 run before the admin receipt and a distinct verified through-60 continuation run afterward; the second run revalidates 1–40 and owns newly applied 50/60 ledger rows. `verified→cutover` is allowed only on a through-60-or-later run after the Development feature-switch proof; `cutover→read_rollback` and `read_rollback→cutover` are the only post-cutover edges. `failed` is terminal; retry creates a new release UID with the same or greater requested-through boundary after proving all already-ledgered checksums and begins at the first missing required sequence. `finished_at` is nonnull only for `verified|failed|cutover|read_rollback`; failure sequence/code are nonnull only for failed. State/feature pairs are exactly `preflight|applying|verified|failed ↔ legacy|shadow`, `cutover ↔ new`, and `read_rollback ↔ legacy`; no run mutates an artifact row, changes its requested boundary or skips a required sequence within that boundary.
- Every artifact carries its own SHA-256, manifest SHA-256, sequence, dependency IDs, owner, kind and capability predicate in a checked-in sidecar generated before apply. A changed byte with the same artifact ID is fatal. Sequence gaps are permitted only for the literal values above; reordering is forbidden.
- Artifact materialization always precedes any real apply. Development applies sequences 1→10→15 first. Sequence 20 ignores exception `status` as an admission shortcut and repeats the raw locked uniqueness queries; any actual pre-anchor conflict leaves the durable sequence-15 rows intact, stops this architecture execution permanently before sequence 20, and requires a separately approved remediation/new-baseline plan. There is deliberately no pre-UID/pre-audit resolve path. Only zero actual pre-anchor conflicts permit 20→30→40. Sequence 20 also locks the four raw payment-domain queries: zero violations means add+validate and state `validated`; nonzero means complete captured exception equality plus state `not_valid_exceptions_present`. Sequence 65 remains a checked-in optional descriptor and cannot run until an approved remediation produces zero; Production rollout requires either sequence-20 `validated` or sequence-65 `validated`. After sequence 40 and a reconnect, Todo 17 may re-resolve Todo 1's exact pre-anchor candidate ID, now carrying the sequence-20 `user_uid`, and atomically commit the strict receipt described below. Only then may the runner apply 50→60. Missing/drifted actor stops at 40 with `blocked_actor`, legacy feature state and no false success. Clean disposable bootstrap creates an explicitly marked, target-bound synthetic admin after sequence 40 and immediately before sequence 50; its receipt includes that disposable fingerprint and can authorize only that database/run UID. Teardown destroys the entire disposable database and invalidates the receipt. Production may never synthesize an actor.
- Development actor receipt bytes are RFC-8785 canonical JSON plus LF with `additionalProperties:false` and exactly `{schema_version:"dgkma-development-admin-v1",target_fingerprint,candidate_user_id,candidate_user_uid,is_admin,authorization_version,through_40_release_uid,observed_ledger_sha256,observed_at,receipt_sha256}`; `is_admin` is const true and self-hash excludes only `receipt_sha256` (the trailing LF is outside the hash). `through_40_release_uid` identifies the unique latest verified release run for this target/manifest with `requested_through_sequence_no=40`. `observed_ledger_sha256=sha256(RFC8785({digest_version:"development-ledger-through-40-v1",target_fingerprint,covering_release_uid:through_40_release_uid,rows}))`, where `rows` are the exact ledger/artifact-owning-release/capability projection for sequence numbers `1,10,15,20,30,40`, sorted ascending, and each object has exactly `{sequence_no,artifact_id,artifact_sha256,manifest_sha256,artifact_release_uid,artifact_release_state,capability_receipt_sha256,target_fingerprint}`. Owning state may be `verified|failed` because a later verified continuation can safely cover byte-verified artifacts committed by a failed run; the covering through-40 run must be `verified`, have matching target/manifest, and its verification query must reproduce every row/checksum. Missing/extra/duplicate sequence, a nonterminal owning state, or a covering mismatch fails. Disposable actor receipt is the same closed shape with schema `dgkma-disposable-admin-v1` plus required `disposable_run_uid,parent_target_fingerprint` and cannot be committed. Raw name/email/contact data is forbidden. The runner descriptor-opens, hashes and live-reproves every field before sequence 50.
- Brownfield admission requires both the exact tuple `13 tables, 112 columns, 13 PK, 9 FK, 6 non-PK UNIQUE, 20 indexes, 9 sequences` and baseline-contract SHA `b4cda7d4dd08afee7b773654740b968b0880aa6a4a776338492b279967ca6595`, computed from `dgkma-dev-baseline-v1|tables=13|columns=112|pk=13|fk=9|unique_nonpk=6|indexes=20|sequences=9|schema_doc_sha=6d6fc85bc51ebf00213c76a29a299f28bc619074e1d95e1c621b50cb9dac9778`. The full observed canonical catalog SHA is also recorded. Any object-level diff aborts; counts alone never admit a baseline.
- An artifact and its `schema_change_ledger` row commit in one transaction under one advisory lock. A crash before commit leaves neither. A crash after commit leaves both. Recovery marks the interrupted run failed when necessary, creates a new release run with the same or greater immutable requested-through boundary, and begins at the first missing literal sequence after rechecking every prior checksum; a verified run is never reopened. “Rollback” before cutover means current-transaction rollback plus legacy feature state; previously committed additive artifacts remain. After cutover, `read_rollback` restores legacy reads while a forward-fix artifact receives a new sequence/ID after a new approved manifest.

Exact sequence-60 owner/ACL/default-privilege contract:

- This Replit phase creates or alters **no database role**. The only application/migration principal is the target receipt's exact `current_user_oid`; the database owner and every application table, sequence, view and routine in `public` must be that principal, while the `public` schema owner must be PostgreSQL 15's exact implicit role `pg_database_owner`. Any mismatch makes sequence 60 abort rather than silently reassigning ownership. Role memberships are cataloged before/after and must be byte-identical. Browser clients have no DB credential and there is no anonymous/member database role.
- Database ACL: a pre-hashed `DO` block quotes `current_database()` with `format('%I',...)`, grants only `CONNECT` to `PUBLIC`, revokes `CREATE,TEMPORARY` from `PUBLIC`, and leaves the owner principal's implicit privileges unchanged. Schema ACL: `REVOKE ALL ON SCHEMA public FROM PUBLIC`; the owner retains implicit `USAGE/CREATE`. Relation ACL: `REVOKE ALL PRIVILEGES ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA public FROM PUBLIC`. Sequence ACL: the analogous revoke on all sequences. Routine ACL uses two literal statements, `REVOKE ALL PRIVILEGES ON ALL FUNCTIONS IN SCHEMA public FROM PUBLIC` and `REVOKE ALL PRIVILEGES ON ALL PROCEDURES IN SCHEMA public FROM PUBLIC`; every routine is non-`SECURITY DEFINER` with fixed `search_path=pg_catalog,public`.
- Default privileges are exact for the target owner, which the preceding contract requires to equal `CURRENT_USER`; no `SET ROLE` occurs. Tables and sequences use `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES IN SCHEMA public REVOKE ALL PRIVILEGES ON TABLES FROM PUBLIC` and its `SEQUENCES` analogue. PostgreSQL 15 routine defaults are global, so the one routine statement is exactly `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES REVOKE EXECUTE ON ROUTINES FROM PUBLIC` with **no** `IN SCHEMA` clause; omitting `FOR ROLE` targets the current role, while a schema-local revoke cannot cancel the global default PUBLIC EXECUTE grant and is forbidden. No duplicate `FUNCTIONS` statement or positive object grant to another role exists. Existing system/extension-schema objects are untouched; a required existing extension routine in `public` is covered by the explicit current-object revoke above, remains executable by the owner, and is executable by nobody through PUBLIC. The post-DDL `pg_default_acl` expectation proves the owner-global routine ACL contains no PUBLIC execute entry and the owner/public table/sequence schema defaults match these exact statements.
- RLS is explicitly disabled on every manifest table and `pg_policies` must be empty for them. Sequence 60's canonical catalog expectation lists owner OID and ACL arrays for database/schema/every relation/sequence/routine plus default ACL rows; a missing object, extra PUBLIC grant, changed membership or different owner blocks ledger verification. This one-owner matrix is the approved current topology, not a claim of multi-role least privilege; introducing a separate runtime role requires a future capability/secret/rollout plan.

Exact runtime, restore and retention parameters:

- The shared pool is `max=10`, `min=0`, `connectionTimeoutMillis=10000`, `idleTimeoutMillis=30000`, `maxUses=10000`; each checkout sets `statement_timeout=30000`, `lock_timeout=5000`, `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout=30000`, and application name. Shutdown stops intake, waits 10 seconds, then fails if borrowers remain. TCP connections require certificate verification (`sslmode=verify-full` semantics and platform CA), except for the owner-approved Replit internal transport recorded by `dgkma-owner-transport-decision-v1`: non-TLS TCP is permitted only when `REPL_ID` is present, literal `PGHOST='helium'`, and target kind is `development|disposable-test`. Ordinary target fingerprint address/port fields are the approved connection endpoint tuple—lowercase `PGHOST` plus canonical `PGPORT` for Development and the parsed read-only URL host/port for Production—not proxy-reported `inet_server_addr/port`; only an absent Unix-socket endpoint uses `local|local`. Live database, user name/OID, server version and the independently pinned PG tuple digest are still verified before DDL/DML; disposable control and target connections repeat their target-bound identity checks. Every other host/target, including Production read-only, requires verified TLS. Only an explicitly detected Unix-domain socket may otherwise omit TLS. There is no `rejectUnauthorized:false`, TLS-error fallback or Production exception.
- The Development restore drill opens connection A on the approved Development target with `BEGIN ISOLATION LEVEL REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY`, records `pg_export_snapshot()`, `txid_current_snapshot()`, database/user/time/current WAL LSN and keeps A open. It runs `pg_dump --format=custom --compress=9 --no-owner --no-acl --snapshot=<exported_snapshot_id>` into a `mktemp -d` attempt directory with directory mode `0700` and dump mode `0600`; every source catalog, row-count and content-digest query runs on A under the same snapshot, then A commits only after dump completion and source-evidence serialization. It records dump SHA-256, server/tool versions and monotonic durations and restores with `pg_restore --exit-on-error --single-transaction --no-owner --no-acl` into the UUID-bound disposable database. The restored database is validation-only: its copied ledger correctly retains Development target fingerprints, so application startup, ordinary apply/reapply, `dgkma_current_target_fingerprint()` ledger comparison and synthetic-admin creation are forbidden. The external attempt receipt `dgkma-restore-validation-v1` is RFC-8785 canonical JSON plus LF with exactly `{schema_version,source_target_fingerprint,disposable_target_fingerprint,restore_run_uid,source_server_version_num,pg_dump_version,pg_restore_version,source_snapshot_observed_at,source_snapshot_id_sha256,source_txid_snapshot,source_snapshot_started_at,source_snapshot_finished_at,source_current_wal_lsn,dump_started_at,dump_finished_at,dump_duration_ms,restore_started_at,restore_finished_at,restore_duration_ms,dump_sha256,source_manifest_sha256,sequence_60_artifact_id,sequence_60_artifact_sha256,restore_reconcile_sha256,pre_security_catalog_sha256,post_security_catalog_sha256,schema_catalog_sha256,data_catalog_sha256,observed_at,result,receipt_sha256}`; durations are canonical nonnegative integers from the same monotonic clock, timestamps are RFC-3339 instants with snapshot-start≤dump-start≤dump-finish≤snapshot-finish≤observed, raw snapshot ID is never logged, WAL LSN is lowercase PostgreSQL canonical text, and self-hash covers the RFC-8785 object excluding only `receipt_sha256` (not the trailing LF). It is the only mapping between the two fingerprints. Because dump flags omit ownership/ACL state, the validation runner descriptor-opens only `migrations/manual/0060_restore_security_reconcile.sql`, verifies its SHA against both the checked-in sequence-60 sidecar and receipt, rejects every statement outside the closed owner/ACL/default-privilege subset, and executes that standalone idempotent file in one transaction. The restore file contains no extension/table/function/trigger/ledger/capability DDL or DML; it is generated and reviewed together with sequence 60 but is never itself applied or ledgered during ordinary migration. Calling the migration runner or executing a parsed subset of `0060_database_security.sql` is forbidden. Any absent/drifted restore bytes, lost exporter transaction, snapshot import/source-database/digest mismatch, forbidden startup/apply, catalog mismatch or ledger mutation aborts. Post-reconcile comparison requires target-neutral schema catalog equality, exact application-table row counts/content digests from that same exported snapshot (including copied Development ledger bytes), and expected disposable-owner security catalog; database name/OID/owner identity and the external receipt table itself are intentionally outside equality. The dump is never committed/uploaded, contains no logged connection string, and is securely unlinked after verified teardown. Production backup/restore is out of scope until separately authorized; RPO/RTO remain policy-pending.
- Retention runs hourly through one advisory-locked job with 500-row/5-second batches and these literal cursors/indexes: sessions `(expire,sid)` where `expire<now()`; OAuth states `(expires_at,state_hash)` where `expires_at<now()-24 hours`; termination markers `(terminated_at,identity_hash)` where `terminated_at<now()-30 days`; rejected pending registrations `(created_at,id)` where `created_at<now()-30 days AND pii_redacted_at IS NULL`. The server-only Replit Secret `ACCOUNTING_PII_HMAC_KEY_V1` is required, never committed/logged and never exposed through `VITE_`; absence blocks the batch before DML. Before pending redaction, normalize strings to NFC without trimming, compute `pii_digest=lowerhex(HMAC-SHA256(key,"pending-registration-v1\n" || RFC8785({kakao_id,email,name,user_data})))`, store `pii_digest_version='pending-registration-v1'`, and set exact nonnull placeholders/name, `user_data=NULL`, time and operation UID; email/name nullability is never weakened. The job uses the frozen target admin with `migration_admin`, operation receipt and redaction audit, is idempotent, commits each batch and stops after 10. Actor/audit/source/financial rows are never retention-deleted. Metrics contain only table, cutoff, count, duration and outcome.

- Mapping seeds are literal and initially `draft`. Each obligation mapping stores the FK `policy_id` of its same-year/same-tier draft policy row; secondary mappings store NULL. For 2024–2025: `president→president/500`, `senior_vice_president→senior_vice_president/400`, `vice_president→vice_president_auditor_chair/300`, `auditor→vice_president_auditor_chair/300`, `general_affairs_director|planning_director|legal_director|external_cooperation_director|public_relations_director|director→director/200`, and `member→member/100`; `general_assembly_chair` is intentionally absent and therefore blocks derivation. For 2026 add `general_assembly_chair→vice_president_auditor_chair/300` and the special member-kind mapping `member_kind:honorary→honorary/0`.
- Every sequence-50 draft policy root and every same-year mapping root has the exact VCHAIN `effective_at` of that dues year's KST start: 2024=`2024-01-01T00:00:00+09:00`, 2025=`2025-01-01T00:00:00+09:00`, 2026=`2026-01-01T00:00:00+09:00`. Every six-category draft root has `effective_at='2022-01-01T00:00:00+09:00'`, equal to its `active_from`; its approval successor uses that same effective instant and a later actual `recorded_at`. Sequence 50 supplies no other root effective instant, and successor monotonicity uses these literal values.
- The exact 2026 secondary codes are `busan_branch_president`, `busan_branch_vice_president`, `busan_branch_general_affairs`, `busan_branch_finance`, `class_1_captain`, `class_3_captain`, `class_7_captain`, `class_41_captain`, `class_41_vice_captain`, `class_42_captain`, and `class_42_vice_captain`; each has `adds_obligation=false`, null tier and priority 0. The six director display positions—`총무이사`, `기획이사`, `법률이사`, `내외협력이사`, `홍보이사`, `이사`—normalize respectively to `general_affairs_director`, `planning_director`, `legal_director`, `external_cooperation_director`, `public_relations_director`, `director`, and all six map to the `director` tier.
- Mapping CHECK uses only the three exact ordinary/honorary/secondary branches in the literal transition expansion, including the required/non-required `policy_id` shapes.
- Seed SQL never fabricates board approval. Historical candidates and the 2026 draft become selectable only through a live `admin` approval transition with resolution reference, approval timestamp, immutable user snapshot and audit correlation. Rights evaluation fails closed when no approved policy exists.
- Policy activation is one annual composite command, `entity_type="dues_year",action="activate"`, never a single-policy operation. In one transaction and global-lock order it locks all draft policy/mapping tips for the year and the full append-only activity projection; appends approved successors for the complete literal policy-tier set sorted by `(priority DESC,tier_code)`; appends approved successors for the complete literal position/member-kind mapping set sorted by `position_code`; requires ordinary/honorary mappings to reference their just-approved same-year policy and secondary mappings to keep NULL policy/tier with priority 0; derives exactly one highest-tier history tip for every member in `dues_year_members(Y)`; and creates a confirmed default pledge for every such member lacking one. The operation result registry contains every policy approval, every mapping approval, every derived tier and every created pledge in global-rank/key order. Deferred completeness uses that identical year-overlap set or rolls back all approvals. No separately committed policy-activation prerequisite exists, but inside this atomic command every policy successor must be inserted before any mapping successor that immediately references it; no partially active year can commit. This live activation is outside architecture execution until the user supplies board-resolution authority; synthetic tests use synthetic resolution references only.

Exact dues-rights evaluation contract:

- A snapshot evaluation instant is its VCHAIN `effective_at` (`E`); `evaluation_date` must equal the `Asia/Seoul` calendar date of `E`. Select the current approved tier-history tip as of `E`, then require the referenced approved policy/mapping to be the exact same year/tier/priority tips. `monthly_minimum` and `annual_minimum` are independent stored values. A role increase appends a new tier tip whose full-year minima apply from that tip's `effective_at`; earlier snapshots are never recomputed.
- Let `D` be the KST date of `E`, `M=month(D)`, `day=day(D)`, `month_start=make_timestamptz(year(D),M,1,0,0,0,'Asia/Seoul')`, `compliance_months = CASE WHEN day>=11 THEN M ELSE M-1 END`, and `rights_months=M-1`. Then `monthly_required_to_date=policy.monthly_minimum*compliance_months`, `rights_required_to_date=policy.monthly_minimum*rights_months`, and `annual_required=policy.annual_minimum`. These integer equations implement current-month reminders from the 11th while monthly rights remain on the prior-month threshold through month end.
- `paid_total` is the locked signed sum of approved `dues_allocations` for the same member/year with `allocation_kind='dues'` and `effective_at<=E`: payment is `+amount`, cancellation/refund is `-amount`. `rights_paid_total` uses the same rows, but includes every positive payment immediately, includes a `next_month_negative` cancellation/refund only when its `effective_at<month_start`, and includes a `retroactive_error` negative from its audited original effective instant. Thus annual threshold payment grants rights immediately, an ordinary refund/cancellation can suspend only from the next KST month, and a proven false/error transaction may restate the original effective history through a superseding snapshot.
- For each calendar month `n=1..compliance_months`, resolve the confirmed pledge tip effective on that month's first day and sum its `monthly_amount`; this is `pledge_target_to_date`. `pledge_id` is the tip current at `E`, while `pledge_derivation_digest` is SHA-256 of RFC-8785 canonical ordered `{month,pledge_id,version,amount}` inputs. On activation of an approved policy/mapping set, the same admin operation must create a confirmed default pledge for every member in `dues_year_members(Y)` lacking a confirmed pledge: `monthly_amount=the selected tier policy.monthly_minimum`, `effective_from=dues-year Jan 1`, source evidence is the exact approved policy/mapping/tier/activity IDs and derivation digest, and actor/correlation are the policy activation admin. A member-confirmed pledge supersedes it only from a first day of a later month. Missing default creation for any member in that exact set is a transaction blocker. `policy_shortfall=GREATEST(monthly_required_to_date-paid_total,0)` and `pledge_shortfall=GREATEST(pledge_target_to_date-paid_total,0)`; pledge and pledge shortfall are display/compliance information and never decide rights.
- `special_assessment_required` sums latest published `approved|reversed` chain tips, treating reversed amount as zero. Paid credit is the signed sum across all version IDs in that assessment chain, capped by published liability; supersession carries credit. Rights-required uses the same chain credit and next-month boundaries. The digest pins every chain/version/allocation ID; another member/year/chain cannot contribute.
- The predicates are literal: `annual_complete := rights_paid_total >= annual_required`; `monthly_current := rights_paid_total >= rights_required_to_date`; `assessment_current := special_assessment_rights_paid >= special_assessment_rights_required`. For February–December, `rights_member := (annual_complete OR monthly_current) AND assessment_current`. For January, recompute `prior_annual_complete := prior_rights_paid_total >= prior_annual_required`, `prior_monthly_complete := prior_rights_paid_total >= 12*prior_policy.monthly_minimum`, and `prior_assessment_current := prior_assessment_rights_paid >= prior_assessment_rights_required` using all signed allocations effective through `YYYY-01-01T00:00:00+09:00` exclusive. Let `prior_dues_carry := no_prior_liability OR prior_annual_complete OR prior_monthly_complete` and treat `prior_assessment_current=true` when there was no prior assessment liability. January `rights_member := (annual_complete OR prior_dues_carry) AND prior_assessment_current AND assessment_current`; `january_carry` as a reason is true only when annual_complete is false and prior-dues carry supplied the dues predicate. A current-year annual completion overrides only the prior dues carry test, never a current or prior assessment shortfall. Honorary bypasses all predicates and remains `honorary`.
- The truth table is closed: honorary→`honorary/honorary`; non-honorary with dues=true and assessment=true→`rights_member` with reason `annual_complete` when annual, otherwise `january_carry` in January when carry supplied the dues predicate, otherwise `monthly_current`; dues=false/assessment=true→`member/monthly_shortfall`; dues=true/assessment=false→`member/assessment_shortfall`; both false→`member/monthly_and_assessment_shortfall`. No other status/reason pair is legal.
- `reason_code` is exactly one of `annual_complete`, `monthly_current`, `january_carry`, `monthly_shortfall`, `assessment_shortfall`, `monthly_and_assessment_shortfall`, or `honorary`, selected in this precedence: honorary; both dues condition and assessment false; assessment false; annual complete; January carry; monthly current; monthly shortfall. Snapshot insertion recomputes every stored amount, boolean predicate and reason under the global locks and rejects any supplied mismatch. Event-driven snapshots are created at annual-threshold crossing, role-tier change, assessment payment, and negative-effect boundary; a daily/month-start job creates the reminder/rights boundary snapshots. Corrections append VCHAIN versions, never overwrite a closed historical row.

- Every seed row starts `status='active'`; sequence 50 explicitly inserts the paired UUID rather than invoking a default. Its `contract_fingerprint` is generated from the literal canonical fields above and checked into the seed sidecar, so Development/disposable fingerprints are byte-identical. A seed or provenance row may not supply a different UUID/rank. Source release/batch rows carry the logical source FK and contract fingerprint, so adapter upgrades cannot change authority.
- The literal seed count is **ten**. The 2024–2025 `dues_policies.source_logical_id` and mapping source must reference `LEDGER_DUES_POLICY_2024_2025`, never the rank-400 `LEDGER_FINAL_2022_2025` event source; 2026 candidates reference `NOTION_DUES_REGULATION_DRAFT`.
- Before C1, `MEMBERSHIP_INTEGRATED_ADDRESS_BOOK` is the sole member identity/contact editorial source and `NOTION_ORGANIZATION_ROLE_HISTORY` is the sole organization/role-history editorial source. Todo 18 imports only their approved mappings; identity rows may support member/match decisions, while only Notion role rows may populate `member_position_assignments.source_row_version_id`. The frozen 22nd payload remains N1 seed and AGM boundary provenance, never a current direct-import role source. At the owner-declared C1 timestamp Production PostgreSQL becomes the sole read/write authority and both external sources become archive/read-only source-of-origin evidence.

Exact release, category and accounting-period seed contract:

- Sequence 50 creates exactly two releases: `membership-integrated-address-book-v1` for `MEMBERSHIP_INTEGRATED_ADDRESS_BOOK` and `notion-organization-role-history-v1` for `NOTION_ORGANIZATION_ROLE_HISTORY`. Both use `adapter_version='1.0.0'`, `released_at='2026-08-10T00:00:00Z'` and the exact checked-in schema, module, mapping and provider-bound mapping-approval-receipt digests. The other eight logical-source identities intentionally have no release in sequence 50. Todo 18 may create each remaining release only after that source's own `source-profile-v1`, strict mapping and provider-bound mapping approval are complete; no `[]`, placeholder or runtime-selected mapping may substitute. A later adapter creates a new release with strictly later `released_at`; the current release is the unique greatest `(released_at,id)` for the source, and a timestamp tie blocks import.
- Release paths are literal: schema `docs/source-contracts/schemas/<adapter-code>.schema.json`, module `server/accounting/adapters/<adapter-code>.ts`, mapping `docs/source-contracts/mappings/<adapter-code>.json`. Each mapping is RFC-8785 canonical JSON plus LF with `additionalProperties:false` and exactly `{schema_version:"source-input-map-v1",adapter_code,source_code,surface,locator,source_profile_sha256,record_selectors,columns,parsers,constants,output_family}`. `surface` is `google_sheet|notion_page|postgres_table|constant_authority`; `record_selectors` is an ordered array of exact `{selector_code,tab_id_or_null,tab_title_snapshot_or_null,a1_range_or_null,header_row_or_null,data_start_row_or_null,record_kind}`; `columns` is ordered by normalized target field and contains exact `{record_kind,target_field,source_selector,parser_code,required}`, where `source_selector` is exactly one of `{kind:"header",value:NFC_header,occurrence:positive_integer}`, `{kind:"column_index",value:positive_integer}`, `{kind:"database_column",value:NFC_identifier}`, or `{kind:"constant",value:canonical_scalar}`. `parsers` is an ordered closed dictionary of only the parser codes actually referenced; `constants` contains only declared normalized fields. Every selector/header/index is bound to `source_profile_sha256`; the separate approval receipt hashes the exact mapping bytes, and the release stores both hashes without a cycle. Missing/duplicate header, unexpected merged cell, wrong tab ID/title/range, parser ambiguity or revision/profile mismatch returns `mapping_review_required` before batch creation; the adapter never guesses.
- Todo 14 obtains a read-only source profile before release materialization. Its strict file is RFC-8785 canonical JSON plus one LF and has exactly `{schema_version:"source-profile-v1",source_code,source_revision,surface,locator,tabs:[{tab_id,title,max_row,max_column,header_candidates:[{row,values_sha256}]}],database_columns,observed_at,profile_sha256}` with arrays sorted by ID/row/name and no cell values beyond header digests. `profile_sha256=sha256(RFC8785(the exact object excluding only profile_sha256))`; the trailing LF is outside the preimage. The executor renders the exact mapping preview including selected headers/indices and sample **digests only**, then pauses for an explicit owner/admin approval message in the current Codex task. Todo 1's already required `tool_search` discovery also binds the exact `codex_app.read_thread` schema. Per the owner-approved 2026-08-10 provider amendments, Todo 14 calls `codex_app.read_thread({threadId:<current platform thread ID>,hostId:<current host ID when nonnull>,includeOutputs:false,maxOutputCharsPerItem:20000,turnLimit:10})` and requires exactly one new user-authored approval message after the preview with returned opaque `messageId` and exact UTF-8 text. When the message item exposes RFC-3339 `createdAt`, that is authoritative; when it omits `createdAt`, the executor must use the containing turn's numeric `startedAt` epoch seconds, convert it losslessly to UTC RFC-3339 whole seconds, and record timestamp source `parent_turn_started_at_projection_v1`. Missing message ID/text, missing or nonintegral parent `startedAt`, ambiguous item-to-turn containment, non-user author or truncated content rejects approval. It commits the mapping plus RFC-8785 canonical JSON+LF `{schema_version:"source-mapping-approval-v1",provider:"codex-app",project_id,platform_thread_id,host_id_or_null,platform_message_id,platform_message_created_at,platform_message_timestamp_source,source_code,source_profile_sha256,mapping_sha256,approval_text_sha256,read_response_sha256,approved_at,receipt_sha256}`. `mapping_sha256` hashes the exact mapping file bytes including its LF; `approval_text_sha256` hashes exact message UTF-8 bytes; `read_response_sha256` hashes the RFC-8785 non-secret projection `{threadId,hostId_or_null,messageId,createdAt,timestamp_source,author_role:"user",text_sha256}`; `approved_at=platform_message_created_at`; `receipt_sha256=sha256(RFC8785(receipt object excluding only receipt_sha256))`, with file LF outside. Before sequence 50, the executor repeats the same `read_thread` call, resolves the same message ID/author/time/text and exact timestamp source, reproduces `read_response_sha256`, re-hashes profile/mapping/receipt exact bytes and rejects any mismatch, later source revision, non-user author or message preceding the preview. Sequence 50 stores the reproduced receipt SHA. If the discovered provider schema exposes neither message `createdAt` nor a valid containing-turn `startedAt`, the approval gate is `blocked_provider_approval` and no alternate repository fixture substitutes. This is a bounded input decision, not implementation discretion.
- The physical field contracts per adapter are closed. `final-ledger-v1` has separate `economic` and `period_metadata` selectors and maps exactly the finalized-sheet coordinate/period/date-or-instant/direction/amount/description/payer/dues-year fields or the period code/start/end/boundary fields. `dues-policy-sheet-v1` selects exactly `회비수입!O2:O7` and `O9:O14` and maps year/tier/monthly/annual/due/reminder. Each bank adapter selects its exact named tab and maps provider ID, posted date, occurred instant, direction, amount, balance and payer; direction parser is exactly one approved mode `signed_amount` or `credit_debit_pair`, and provider ID parser is `source_id` or, only when the profile proves no stable ID column, `coordinate_key`. `group-roster-v1` maps group/member/generation/amount. `legacy-payments-v1` is fixed without owner choice to `public.payments(id,user_id,year,amount,type,status,created_at)` plus the captured timezone. Any required normalized field without exactly one mapping blocks release.
- Role conversion is literal. `notion-organization-role-history-v1` maps the activated N1 database through the approved profile/map bytes. Name and free-text provenance are digest-only; organization, administration, position, appointment basis, editorial status, publication flag and member-match state use closed parsers. Position and appointment parsers may use `조직구분` as the sole declared context column; unknown Korean role/basis/organization text blocks. Mapping approval never changes a record's review/publication/match status. `membership-integrated-address-book-v1` maps only name digest, generation, admission/graduation dates and group/status evidence digests; address, phone, department, legacy role and memo are excluded. `agm-period-boundary-v1` remains constant authority with the two exact period rows printed below.
- The eight schema families are `final-ledger-coordinate-v1`, `bank-row-v1`, `member-identity-row-v1`, `role-row-v2`, `period-boundary-v1`, `allocation-roster-row-v1`, `policy-row-v1`, `legacy-payment-row-v1`; both bank adapters and both policy adapters share their family bytes. Every ordinary family schema is RFC-8785 canonical JSON plus LF with exact top-level keys `{"$schema":"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema","$id":"<family>","type":"object","additionalProperties":false,"required":[<the field list in Exact normalized source contracts, in printed order>],"properties":{<same fields in printed order>}}`. The sole structural exception is `final-ledger-coordinate-v1`, whose top level has `oneOf` exactly two `additionalProperties:false` object branches: the economic-row branch and the period-metadata branch printed below; each has discriminator `coordinate_kind` const `economic|period_metadata`. Scalar definitions are fixed: digest=`{"type":"string","pattern":"^[0-9a-f]{64}$"}`, money=`{"type":"string","pattern":"^(0|[1-9][0-9]*)$"}`, signed legacy money=`{"type":"string","pattern":"^(0|[1-9][0-9]*|-[1-9][0-9]*)$"}`, date=`{"type":"string","format":"date"}`, instant=`{"type":"string","format":"date-time"}`, nullable=`{"anyOf":[<base>,{"type":"null"}]}`, enum and integer use their printed domains. Arrays require exact item schema and preserve printed order. Only the legacy amount evidence envelope may use signed legacy money; its required top-level `source_amount_signed` property is explicitly `nullable(signed legacy money)` so invalid source text remains representable as JSON null, while the valid/invalid cross-field rule below determines legality. `-0`, plus signs and leading zeros are invalid, and every business financial amount remains positive. The generator has no optional property beyond those explicitly nullable and fails if canonical bytes differ.
- Digest preimages are closed. `accounting_logical_sources.contract_fingerprint=sha256(RFC8785({digest_version:"logical-source-v1",source_uid,source_code,source_kind,source_locator,display_name,authority_role,event_authority_rank,source_timezone,valid_from,valid_to,status}))`. `source_fingerprint=sha256(RFC8785({digest_version:"source-batch-v1",source_uid,release_uid,source_revision,captured_timezone,coverage_from,coverage_through,rows:[{coordinate_key,content_digest,issue_status}]}))`; rows sort by UTF-8 coordinate key and duplicate keys fail. Preview manifest uses that same ordered rows array. Provenance `evidence_digest=sha256(RFC8785({digest_version:"event-provenance-v1",event_uid,source_row_content_digest,evidence_role,authority_rank_snapshot,normalization_version}))`. Match evidence uses `sha256(RFC8785({digest_version:"member-match-v1",case_uid,member_uid,evidence_kind,source_row_content_digest,score_basis}))`; name-only remains unapprovable.
- Coordinate defaults are exact: `coordinate_normalization_version='coordinate-v1'`; row `normalization_version='<adapter-code>@1.0.0'`. `source_display_snapshot` is the NFC tab title for Sheet sources, `__page__` for Notion, and `__legacy__` for PostgreSQL; it is versioned display evidence, never coordinate identity. Bank Google-Sheet sources retain their real tab title, not `__bank__`. Revision acquisition is: Google files canonical `{drive_version,modifiedTime,etag_sha256}`; Notion `{page_id,last_edited_time,response_body_sha256}`; legacy under one repeatable-read snapshot `{captured_timezone,txid_snapshot,max_payment_id,row_count,ordered_row_digest}`. Revision is RFC-8785 of that object; raw ETag/body/rows are not stored.
- The six category roots are the sole set: `DUES_INCOME|회비수입|income|dues_credit`, `OTHER_INCOME|기타수입|income|none`, `DUES_REFUND|회비환급|expense|dues_refund`, `GENERAL_EXPENSE|기타지출|expense|none`, `INTERNAL_TRANSFER_IN|내부이체입금|transfer|none`, `INTERNAL_TRANSFER_OUT|내부이체출금|transfer|none`. Each root is draft with `active_from='2022-01-01'`, NULL end; Todo 17's approval successor has `effective_at='2022-01-01T00:00:00+09:00'` and recorded/approved time at actual execution. Missing/extra/different seed blocks.
- Historical accounting periods through `2026-01-01T00:00:00+09:00` are generated from finalized-ledger metadata coordinates whose normalized payload is exactly `{period_code,starts_at,ends_at,boundary_source_code,boundary_coordinate_key,boundary_content_digest}`. The source-code/key pair resolves one coordinate and the digest resolves its exact immutable row version; the ledger cannot claim validity after its source's `valid_to`. The separate `AGM36_PERIOD_BOUNDARY` adapter emits `PRE_AGM36_2026`=`[2026-01-01T00:00:00+09:00,2026-02-28T12:38:00+09:00)` and `AGM36_TO_AGM37` starting at that same close instant with NULL end. Thus the officer source remains role authority while the period-boundary source alone is temporal-boundary authority. Absent, overlapping or conflicting metadata blocks preview rather than inferring calendar-year boundaries. The `open→reconciling` operation later sets the next authoritative AGM close exactly once and creates the next open period at the same instant. Event period selection is unique containment `starts_at<=occurred_at AND (ends_at IS NULL OR occurred_at<ends_at)`; zero or multiple matches blocks classification/import.
- For both 2026 period rows, `boundary_source_row_version_id` is the applied `AGM36_PERIOD_BOUNDARY` `period-boundary-v1` metadata row and `boundary_evidence_row_version_id` is the exact row resolved by its normalized `boundary_source_code|boundary_coordinate_key|boundary_content_digest` triple for the 22nd-president/AGM-close evidence. The exact instant is the owner-approved close `2026-02-28T12:38:00+09:00`; the source appointment date remains `2026-02-28`. `override_reason='OWNER_APPROVED_21ST_TERM_TO_AGM36_CLOSE'` is stored on the corresponding role assignment but is supporting evidence, not the period metadata or boundary authority.
- The period INSERT trigger locks both referenced row versions and the approved period-materialization decision item. It accepts only (a) a `LEDGER_FINAL_2022_2025` `coordinate_kind='period_metadata'` row whose start/end lie wholly inside that logical source validity, or (b) an `AGM36_PERIOD_BOUNDARY` `period-boundary-v1` row; requires exact payload equality with `period_code|starts_at|ends_at|boundary_source_code|boundary_coordinate_key|boundary_content_digest`; resolves the evidence row within that named logical source; and rejects a role-row metadata FK, arbitrary admin timestamp, source/key ambiguity, digest mismatch, validity gap or overlapping interval. Apply materializes these rows before any event containment lookup.
- A period may enter `reconciling` only when every overlapping source's **current release** has exactly one applied batch whose coverage contains the full source/period intersection and whose `(coverage_through,applied_at,id)` is greatest among containing batches; ties block. Its revision/fingerprint must equal a fresh preview, and every preview coordinate must resolve to the same current row/classification tip. Policy/publication/role/allocation-only sources may have zero economic rows but still require this receipt. Close additionally requires zero blocked/quarantined row, open collision, proposed financial row, and one current approved reconciliation tip per account-period intersection.
- Reconciliation always selects current tips, not every historical attempt: for each `(account,period,statement_end_at)` choose the approved row with no approved successor; its active items alone cover `(anchor.as_of_at,statement_end_at]`. The first approved interval owns each bank transaction; a superseding revalidation may repeat that transaction in its replacement item set but atomically makes the parent's item set inactive, so no two active approved intervals cover it. Reopen leaves the former approved tip immutable and requires an explicit superseding draft/new approval before re-close. A timing transaction's stable carry tuple is reused across those revalidation item sets; the original carry remains attached to the next period, is neither recreated nor added to a second balance sum, and a bound carry follows the target reconciliation's superseding descendants without rebinding. The immediately-next-period rule uses periods ordered by `starts_at`, and close blocks any unresolved carryforward whose target is the period being closed.
- The first reconciliation for `TOSS_OFFICER_2026` and `IBK_ASSOCIATION_2026` uses the opening anchor derived from each mapped source's actual earliest accepted row by the exact manifest formula; that row itself must be balance-bearing, and a balance-null earliest row blocks rather than permitting a later substitute. The account transition is not a balance shortcut: an approved cross-account transfer must link the final TOSS debit and first matching IBK credit when present; both legs remain in their own account reconciliations, while reporting excludes the approved `INTERNAL_TRANSFER` pair from income/expense. No inferred transfer, shared anchor or carried account balance is allowed.

Exact correction, cutover and privacy closure:

- Late equal-candidate or higher-authority evidence always creates/locks the normalized unordered event pair in `economic_event_collisions` before any canonicalization. A childless pair is resolvable only while both events are proposed: the same command selects the authoritative canonical event, rejects the other event while it still has zero canonicalization/financial children, inserts the canonicalization, and appends the collision's `resolved` successor atomically. The rejected duplicate is terminal and no longer a proposed close blocker; the canonical event remains proposed until its ordinary source/financial topology approves it. A pair with either event approved/rejected beforehand or with any financial child stays open, both roots are excluded from current reports, and period close/cutover is blocked; a future owner-approved remediation is required. This durable pair identity makes retry and reviewer queues deterministic.
- Legacy cutover is database-enforced: in one short transaction lock the current `payments-v1` tip, `LOCK TABLE payments IN SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE MODE`, and materialize the immutable old-side snapshot entirely through each `payments:<id>` row version plus its terminal `legacy_payment_decisions` tip. Eligibility, member, year, amount, status and source-content digest are evaluated once from those locked pre-fence bytes; the canonical eligible-old projection/comparison digest is then appended with `phase='fenced'` and `watermark_payment_id=COALESCE(MAX(id),0)`. The already-installed trigger rejects every legacy write except the exact deletion unlink. After commit, every payment ID through the watermark has one terminal decision bound to that frozen row version. Rows satisfying the frozen eligibility predicate must end `cross_link|new_compatibility_event`; rows outside it must end `ineligible` with a closed reason code and are excluded from both old/new comparison projections. `review|quarantine` is never terminal for cutover and blocks. A later account deletion may NULL live `payments.user_id`, but eligibility/comparison never rereads that mutable column after fence and instead uses the frozen row-version/decision snapshot. A second transaction re-locks table/state, rechecks `MAX(id)=watermark`, allows only byte-identical account-deletion user nulls relative to frozen source rows, proves zero unresolved eligible decisions/collisions, exact eligible signed totals and the same canonical new projection digest, then appends `new` retaining both values and switches the read feature atomically. Open `legacy_not_valid` exceptions keep Production rollout blocked but do not block the Development compatibility read switch when their rows have terminal `ineligible` decisions. Any failure appends no state; an audited `read_rollback` retains both values, changes reads only and never resumes legacy writes. This replaces the unsafe two-zero-scan rule.
- Pending-registration redaction rejects any source row whose canonical email is blank before normal insert. Exact stable placeholders are `kakao_id='redacted:'||id::text||':'||left(pii_digest,16)`, `email='redacted+'||id::text||'-'||left(pii_digest,16)||'@invalid.example'`, and `name='탈퇴·거절 회원 '||id::text`; they are nonblank and deterministically unique. `pii_digest` is the versioned HMAC computed before replacement, and the one-way operation/audit contract remains unchanged.
- `no_prior_liability` is true only when the append-only activity-event projection has no active interval overlapping the prior KST dues year and there is no approved policy-tier history or approved assessment touching that year; otherwise it is false. The mutable `association_members.ended_at` is never historical evidence for this calculation. `special_assessment_required` sums current approved tips whose due date is on or before `evaluation_date`; `special_assessment_rights_required` includes only `rights_effect='suspend_when_unpaid'` with `due_date<evaluation_date`, preserving rights through the due date's KST end. A positive payment resumes rights at its effective instant; ordinary negative effects start next month, and audited error corrections use their original effective instant.
- Annual policy activation uses the complete `dues_year:activate` topology above: all policy tips, all obligation and secondary mapping tips, every `dues_year_members(Y)` tier history, then missing default pledges for that same set. Commit fails unless every literal year policy/mapping is active and every year-overlap member has the applicable tier plus pledge; members ended before Y or first joined after Y are excluded, and secondary mappings satisfy completeness without a policy FK. The authorized 21st-term end normalization uses the exact `override_reason='OWNER_APPROVED_21ST_TERM_TO_AGM36_CLOSE'`; no alternate free text passes.

Exact normalized source contracts:

- All normalized JSON is Unicode NFC with collapsed internal whitespace for source display strings, keys are UTF-8 byte-order canonical, ordinary money is an unsigned canonical decimal string without leading zeros except `"0"`, instants are RFC-3339 with explicit offset, dates are `YYYY-MM-DD`, absent optionals are JSON `null`, and structural digests use RFC 8785 UTF-8 bytes. The sole exception is the nullable legacy source-evidence `source_amount_signed` field: when nonnull it uses the signed canonical pattern printed above and is never copied into a positive financial amount until eligibility proves `>0`; invalid raw source text produces JSON null plus its parse-error digest, never a substitute numeric string. Work-relevant person, payer, group, description and provenance text is retained as an immutable `*_snapshot` for the restricted administrator review surface and paired with a lowercase hex domain-separated `SHA-256("<field-domain>-v1\n"||normalized_value)` `*_key_digest|*_digest` for bucketing, drift detection and audit binding. These digests are not identity and use no secret or `ACCOUNTING_PII_HMAC_KEY_V1`; stable member identity and rename continuity use `member_uid`. No adapter may add an undeclared key, match or allocate by display text alone, or serialize snapshots into command receipts, logs or Git evidence.
- `accounting_import_row_versions.raw_payload` is not a whole-source dump. Its exact v2 per-family allowlists are: finalized ledger economic `{coordinate_kind,coordinate_key,period_code,occurred_date,occurred_at,direction,amount,dues_year,description_snapshot,description_digest,payer_name_snapshot,payer_name_key_digest}` or finalized ledger metadata `{coordinate_kind,period_code,starts_at,ends_at,boundary_source_code,boundary_coordinate_key,boundary_content_digest}`; bank `{provider_row_id,posted_date,occurred_at,direction,amount,balance_after,payer_name_snapshot,payer_name_key_digest,transaction_description_snapshot,transaction_description_digest}`; member identity `{name_snapshot,name_key_digest,generation,admitted_on,graduated_on,member_kind_evidence_snapshot,member_kind_evidence_digest,source_status_snapshot,source_status_digest,source_timezone}`; role `{name_snapshot,name_key_digest,generation,admission_year,administration_no,organization_code,position_code,display_position,source_appointment_date,source_date_text,date_precision,effective_from,effective_to,appointment_basis,editorial_status,publication_allowed,member_match_status,matched_member_uid,note_snapshot,note_digest,source_locator_snapshot,source_locator_digest,verification_evidence_snapshot,verification_evidence_digest,source_timezone}`; period boundary `{period_code,starts_at,ends_at,boundary_source_code,boundary_coordinate_key,boundary_content_digest}`; allocation roster `{group_name_snapshot,group_key_digest,member_name_snapshot,member_name_key_digest,generation,proposed_amount}`; policy `{dues_year,tier_code,monthly_minimum,annual_minimum,due_day,reminder_day}`; legacy `{payment_id,user_id,year,source_amount_signed,amount_parse,type,status,created_at,source_timezone}`. `amount_parse` is exactly `{status,raw_digest,source_amount_signed_or_null}` and preserves a valid signed value or redacted invalid-input evidence. Email, phone, address, workplace, bank account/owner identifier, CMS code, raw invalid amount text, access token and provider response remain forbidden. `normalized_payload` is always one coordinate-specific object in exactly one family—never a whole-document `{periods,rows}` container.
- Finalized-ledger economic row v2 schema is exactly `{coordinate_kind:"economic",coordinate_key,period_code,occurred_date,occurred_at,direction,amount,description_snapshot,description_digest,dues_year,payer_name_snapshot,payer_name_key_digest}`. Historical period metadata is stored as separate `meta:<source-code>:period:<period-code>` coordinates with `{coordinate_kind:"period_metadata",period_code,starts_at,ends_at,boundary_source_code,boundary_coordinate_key,boundary_content_digest}` under the same final-ledger release; the separate `AGM36_PERIOD_BOUNDARY` source uses the same six boundary fields without `coordinate_kind`. Every ledger row's instant is uniquely contained by its referenced period. Bank row v2 schema is `{provider_row_id,posted_date,occurred_at,direction,amount,balance_after,payer_name_snapshot,payer_name_key_digest,transaction_description_snapshot,transaction_description_digest}`. Member identity row v2 and role row v3 are the exact checked-in schemas `member-identity-row-v2` and `role-row-v3`. Allocation roster v2 is `{group_name_snapshot,group_key_digest,member_name_snapshot,member_name_key_digest,generation,proposed_amount}`; policy and legacy shapes are unchanged. Source snapshots preserve the text observed at that coordinate/version even after the linked member's current display name changes. Every type/encoding follows the preceding canonical rules.
- Party kind is exactly `member|group|unmatched|non_dues`. Claim values are derived from the approved source-decision item, classification and normalized row, never supplied independently: member requires member ID and NULL event party; group/unmatched/non-dues require NULL member plus an approved `economic_event_party_aliases` row to the stored event party; dues year is present only for dues/mixed classifications. Source payer/group/name digests remain alias evidence and never enter the candidate identity.
- A classification with `classification_kind='non_economic'` is a terminal documentary decision and must not create an `economic_event_claims`, `economic_events`, cashbook or receipt row. Every other approved classification creates or binds through the claim pipeline; quarantined rows create no financial child, while a reject item creates no classification row at all.
- The claim candidate-key preimage is RFC-8785 canonical JSON `{candidate_key_version:"event-candidate-v2",party_identity,dues_year_or_null,amount,occurred_date_kst,direction,party_kind}`. `party_identity` is exactly `"member:"+lower(member_uid)` for a member or `"event-party:"+lower(event_party_uid)` for group/unmatched/non-dues. No payer/name/group display digest is included. `candidate_key=sha256(canonical_json_bytes)`; equal keys are search/lock buckets only and never event uniqueness. Fixtures must prove two different payer spellings linked to the same member/party collide in the same bucket, while two explicitly distinct party UIDs remain distinct.
- The active adapter→schema-family mapping is exhaustive and literal: `final-ledger-v2→final-ledger-coordinate-v2`; `dues-policy-sheet-v2→policy-row-v1`; `toss-bank-sheet-v2→bank-row-v2`; `ibk-bank-sheet-v2→bank-row-v2`; `group-roster-v2→allocation-roster-row-v2`; `membership-integrated-address-book-v2|v3→member-identity-row-v2`; `agm-period-boundary-v2→period-boundary-v1`; `notion-organization-role-history-v2→role-row-v3`, `v3→role-row-v4`, `v4→role-row-v5`; `notion-dues-draft-v2→policy-row-v1`; `legacy-payments-v2→legacy-payment-row-v1`. Earlier releases remain immutable historical registration evidence; a new import batch must select the unique latest exact approved descriptor for that source revision. Every active source release records exactly its mapped schema plus JSON Schema, normalizer, approved input-map and mapping-approval-receipt SHA. Import rejects an unlisted pair, unapproved/profile-stale mapping, undeclared keys, wrong timezone/money encoding, or a release whose four digests differ. Adapters and their checked-in approved mappings may only select/parse/normalize; they may not classify, match or allocate. An approved `source_decision_set` and its item are the sole bridge from a normalized row to member/party alias, classification, claim/event and group allocation. A parsed row without an approved decision item remains `previewed` or receives an explicit `quarantine` item and creates no financial child.
- The admin-readable v2 amendment in this section is normative over earlier table prose that still names v1 source fields. For those table/trigger descriptions, read role `name_digest` as `name_key_digest`, group `member_name_digest|group_digest` as `member_name_key_digest|group_key_digest`, economic `payer_digest` as `payer_name_key_digest`, and v1 family names as their active v2 family above. The corresponding `*_snapshot` stays only in the immutable source row and restricted GET projection; downstream cases, candidates, claims, groups, cashbook rows, manifests and receipts bind the source content/key digest or stable member/party UID and do not duplicate the display text. Any description-generation digest formerly specified as `HMAC-SHA256(ACCOUNTING_PII_HMAC_KEY_V1,...)` is instead the domain-separated unkeyed SHA-256 rule above. No accounting source import, matching or description path may require that secret.

Exact material-digest registry:

- Every digest below is SHA-256 of RFC-8785 canonical JSON UTF-8 bytes. Objects include the literal `digest_version`; nullable fields are JSON `null`; UUID/SHA/code are lowercase or NFC as declared; money is a canonical decimal string; arrays are sorted by the printed tuple and carry explicit ordinal where order is material. Todo 2 emits one positive and one field-order/null negative vector per digest and both SQL/TypeScript implementations must reproduce them.
- `bank_transactions.row_fingerprint`: `{digest_version:"bank-row-v1",account_code,provider_row_id,source_content_digest,posted_date,occurred_at,direction,amount,balance_after}`.
- `dues_payment_groups.snapshot_digest`: `{digest_version:"group-snapshot-v1",receipt_uid,roster_batch_uid,children:[{ordinal,source_content_digest,member_uid_or_null,match_case_uid_or_null,proposed_amount}]}` sorted by `(source_content_digest,member_uid_or_null,proposed_amount)` before assigning ordinal.
- `legacy_payment_decisions.decision_key`: `{digest_version:"legacy-decision-v1",payment_id,source_content_digest,decision,member_uid_or_null,candidate_event_uid_or_null,created_event_uid_or_null,reason_code,timezone_snapshot}`.
- `member_dues_tier_history.derivation_digest`: `{digest_version:"tier-derivation-v1",member_uid,dues_year,effective_at,candidates:[{candidate_kind,assignment_uid_or_null,assignment_version_or_null,mapping_id,mapping_version,priority,tier_code,policy_id,policy_version}],selected:{candidate_kind,assignment_uid_or_null,assignment_version_or_null,mapping_id,tier_code,policy_id,priority}}`. `candidate_kind` is exactly `assignment|member_baseline|honorary_baseline`; assignment rows require both assignment fields nonnull, while either baseline requires both JSON null and uses the literal `member` or `member_kind:honorary` mapping. Candidates sort by `(priority,candidate_kind,assignment_uid_or_empty,mapping_id)` and the selected object must byte-match the greatest eligible candidate under the highest-tier rule.
- `dues_pledges.derivation_digest`: `{digest_version:"pledge-v1",member_uid,effective_from,origin_kind,monthly_amount,policy_id,policy_version,mapping_id,mapping_version,tier_history_id,tier_history_version}`.
- `dues_status_snapshots.pledge_derivation_digest` is the ordered month-input object already printed in the rights contract with added `digest_version:"pledge-status-v1"`; `assessment_derivation_digest` is `{digest_version:"assessment-status-v1",member_uid,dues_year,evaluation_date,chains:[{assessment_uid,published_version,amount,due_date,rights_effect,allocations:[{allocation_id,effect_kind,rights_effect_mode,amount,effective_at}]}]}` sorted by assessment UID then allocation ID.

- Bank account seeds are `TOSS_OFFICER_2026` / institution `TOSS` / owner `reported_officer` / half-open active interval `[2026-01-01,2026-03-17)`, and `IBK_ASSOCIATION_2026` / institution `IBK` / owner `association` / active from `2026-03-16`. The bank-source/account mapping is literal and one-to-one: `BANK_TOSS_2026→TOSS_OFFICER_2026`, `BANK_IBK_2026→IBK_ASSOCIATION_2026`; no other logical source can create a transaction or balance anchor for those accounts. `masked_identifier='미수집'` is an explicit non-identity display placeholder until a masked value is provided; account UUID/code remains the identity.

Literal event-authority and canonical-root contract:

- Provenance rows are immutable candidates/support only. Initial authority is established by appending VCHAIN version 1 in `economic_event_authority_decisions`; a late source appends its provenance first and then appends version N+1 with `supersedes_id` equal to the locked current decision tip. Greatest-rank ties with different evidence identities append a `quarantine` decision and block approval/close; no timestamp tie-break silently chooses authority.
- A canonicalization may be inserted only by the exact childless collision command after its chosen duplicate has transitioned to rejected and while the canonical event remains proposed; both events' pre-command financial-child sets must be empty. The canonical root of event E is computed by a cycle-checked recursive CTE following `duplicate_event_id→canonical_event_id` until no parent exists; the trigger guarantees one outgoing edge and no cycle. Event/provenance search resolves source evidence to that root. Cashbook, receipt, bank, reconciliation and period-close writes accept only a non-rejected canonical-root event ID, and reporting groups by canonical root. The rejected canonicalized duplicate contributes zero monetary rows and cannot remain a proposed close blocker; the canonical root's later monetary children are counted exactly once.
- If late authority arrives after either event has a financial child, canonicalization is forbidden. The transaction appends candidate provenance and a `quarantine` authority decision, retains both approved event records and their existing children, and excludes the quarantined pair from close/cutover until an explicit corrective/reversal workflow resolves it. It never reparents, rewrites or double-counts financial children.

Literal cashbook/receipt/allocation invariant:

- The cashbook invariant uses exactly the six category roots above; this sentence is a reference, not a second seed registry.
- For each approved economic event, `event.amount = SUM(active approved cashbook tip amount WHERE event_id=event.id)` and every active tip direction equals the event direction. Every active approved cashbook tip for the event has the same `period_id`, and that period is the unique row satisfying `period.starts_at<=event.occurred_at AND (period.ends_at IS NULL OR event.occurred_at<period.ends_at)`; zero, multiple or cross-period tips fail deferred commit. Historical approved parents remain visible but never contribute after an approved correction child exists. A bank transfer requires exactly one approved debit and one approved credit event, opposite active transfer categories, equal amount and globally unused legs.
- Every cashbook create/approve trigger requires `category_id` to be the current approved structural category tip for `category_code` on the event's KST date, `active_from<=event_date<active_to or infinity`, and `category_label_snapshot=category.display_name`; its direction/report section and dues/refund behavior are derived from that approved tip, never supplied independently. Direction is a closed map: `DUES_INCOME|OTHER_INCOME→credit`, `DUES_REFUND|GENERAL_EXPENSE→debit`, `INTERNAL_TRANSFER_IN→credit`, `INTERNAL_TRANSFER_OUT→debit`; no other category/direction pair is legal. Todo 17 approves exactly the six seeded category tips with the frozen admin receipt before Todo 18 imports any economic row; a draft/missing/out-of-range category blocks classification and event approval.
- For a bound claim/event with an approved classification, the ordered classification `category_splits` and the active approved cashbook projection are equal as a multiset of `{category_code,amount}` after grouping by category code, and both total `event.amount`; the classification direction equals the event and every mapped category direction. Initial publish proves the equality at commit. A cashbook correction copies `description_digest` and may only replace entry granularity while the resulting active grouped projection remains byte-equivalent to the immutable classification splits. V1 has no general category/amount reclassification after a financial child: anything other than the exact bank-refund or immutable-equivalent/noncash-correction branches is quarantined and blocks close until a separately approved future remediation design; it cannot silently alter the old classification or active totals.
- For each credit event, `SUM(active approved cashbook tip amount where category.dues_effect='dues_credit') = SUM(approved dues_receipts.gross_amount for that event)`. For each debit event, `SUM(active approved cashbook tip amount where category.dues_effect='dues_refund') = SUM(approved dues_allocations.amount where effect_kind='refund' and funding_event_id=event.id)`.
- Before period close, every approved receipt satisfies `gross_amount - SUM(approved dues_receipt_reversals.amount) = SUM(approved payment allocations.amount) - SUM(approved cancellation allocations.amount) - SUM(approved refund allocations.amount)` for allocations tied to that receipt. Cancellation is noncash and may be followed by a replacement payment; it never links a receipt reversal or funding event. Refund is cash, links exactly one receipt reversal and debit funding event, and is not also counted as cancellation. Approved cancellation+refund against one original payment cannot exceed that original. Net member payment uses the same mutually exclusive signed equation; no floating-point arithmetic is permitted.
- Allocation `effective_at` is server-derived and never accepted from HTTP/import input. An ordinary payment uses its approved receipt event's `occurred_at`; a group payment uses the same receipt event instant. An ordinary reassignment cancellation uses the operation's canonical approval instant and `next_month_negative`, while its replacement payment uses that same instant with `immediate_positive`. A normal cash refund uses the approved refund event's `occurred_at` and `next_month_negative`. Only `allocation:correct` or `receipt:refund` carrying the exact closed `correction_reason_code=source_error|false_transaction` and bound correction-evidence digest may use `retroactive_error`: its cancellation/refund and any replacement all store the original payment allocation's `effective_at`, the source-decision/classification correction evidence and before/after audit digest must be present, and the operation effective instant remains separately recorded in actor/audit time. A `receipt:refund` with both correction inputs NULL remains the ordinary next-month branch. No other branch may backdate. These predicates are part of the allocation shape CHECK/deferred trigger and drive the rights equations without session-timezone inference.
- For every published approved special-assessment tip A, chain credit is computed across the logical UID, never only the tip ID: `SUM(CASE allocation.effect_kind WHEN 'payment' THEN allocation.amount WHEN 'cancellation' THEN -allocation.amount WHEN 'refund' THEN -allocation.amount END)` over approved allocations joined through any `member_assessments AV` with `AV.id=allocation.assessment_id AND AV.assessment_uid=A.assessment_uid`. The operation first locks all assessment-version IDs for that UID ordered by ID, then all matching allocation IDs ordered by ID, and requires chain credit in `[0,A.amount]`. Excess can never become assessment credit or an approved unallocated balance: in the same receipt-approval operation it must be covered by an approved ordinary-dues classification split/allocation, otherwise the receipt remains proposed and the operation aborts; a later cash return uses the exact bank-refund topology. A reversed/superseding assessment tip cannot reduce the cap below existing chain credit unless the same transaction appends compensating cancellation/refund allocations under the global lock order. Allocations retain the version they paid, while cap and status derive from the logical chain. Negative net, over-cap, allocation to a reversed tip, or any commit-time receipt-net inequality blocks approval.
- An approved payment group satisfies `expected_total = receipt.gross_amount = SUM(current included approved group_member.proposed_amount)`. Only the latest structural tip per `group_member_uid` participates; a cancel tip contributes zero and its historical include parent is excluded. Every current included approved group member has exactly one approved payment allocation with the same receipt/member/group-member/amount, while canceled members have zero net approved allocation after their matching cancellation/reassignment. Unmatched/candidate/rejected current tips block group approval and close.
- An approved receipt reversal satisfies `reversal.amount = SUM(approved refund allocations WHERE receipt_reversal_id=reversal.id AND funding_event_id=reversal.refund_event_id)` and equals the unique linked debit reversal event's active approved `DUES_REFUND` cashbook-tip total. That debit event is an approved opposite-direction economic reversal referencing the original receipt event; the original economic event, receipt and payment allocations remain approved/immutable. Noncash cancellations never appear in this equation.
- For reconciliation R, the monetary interval is exactly `(anchor.as_of_at, R.statement_end_at]`; it may begin before `period.starts_at`, but `R.period_id` must be the unique period satisfying `period.starts_at <= statement_end_at AND statement_end_at < period.ends_at`, and `anchor.as_of_at < statement_end_at < period.ends_at`. Before the first approved reconciliation for an account, an anti-join must prove there is no authoritative transaction ordered before or equal to the opening-anchor evidence row that lacks prior active approved coverage; because first-anchor creation requires the earliest authoritative row itself, any earlier balance-null row is impossible and blocks the source batch/reconciliation rather than being skipped. The expected set is every authoritative `bank_transactions BT` with `BT.account_id=R.account_id`, normalized `occurred_at` in that interval and no coverage by another **active** approved reconciliation tip; a superseding revalidation treats only its own approved ancestor as replaceable coverage. Items equal that set by anti-join in both directions. A matched item has NULL note code; a timing item has exactly one closed `note_code` and one stable carry chain keyed by its bank transaction/source account/source period/target account/target period; unresolved has NULL code and exists only under a draft parent. Rejected and superseded reconciliation item sets are permanent attempt evidence but never cover transactions, create duplicate carry obligations, contribute balances, or satisfy close.
- A carryforward is created before the future reconciliation is knowable: its open root stores only the first timing item and immediately next `target_account_id+target_period_id`. Revalidation of the source interval derives the same stable tuple and reuses that root. When the target period first receives a draft reconciliation, the create-reconciliation operation locks every open carry for the pair and appends one `bound` successor with that reconciliation ID; a missing/multiple binding blocks approval. A later draft that supersedes that target reconciliation is a descendant of the stored bound ID and needs no second binding. Resolution appends only a `resolved` successor. `transaction` resolution must point to a bank transaction in the current nonrejected bound-reconciliation descendant's interval/account and matching the timing code; noncash resolution requires one closed resolution-reason code, one supporting-document digest and the resolution actor. No explanation text is stored. Parent rows are unchanged. The original timing bank transaction participates in only the current source reconciliation tip and one balance sum.
- An approved bank reconciliation is never a comparison of two operator-entered numbers. Its anchor is the latest immutable source-bound anchor for `R.account_id` at or before the interval start. `ending_transaction_id` is nonnull and resolves to the greatest authoritative `bank_transactions BT_END` by `(BT_END.occurred_at,BT_END.id)` satisfying `BT_END.account_id=R.account_id AND BT_END.occurred_at<=R.statement_end_at`; `bank_balance=BT_END.balance_after`, which must be nonnull. Signed ledger activity is the literal account-scoped projection `SUM(CASE E.direction WHEN 'credit' THEN CE.amount WHEN 'debit' THEN -CE.amount ELSE NULL END)` from `bank_transactions BT JOIN economic_events E ON E.id=BT.event_id AND E.status='approved' AND E.id=dgkma_canonical_event_root(BT.event_id) JOIN active_approved_cashbook_tips CE ON CE.event_id=E.id`, with `BT.account_id=R.account_id AND BT.occurred_at>anchor.as_of_at AND BT.occurred_at<=R.statement_end_at`; any noncanonical/unapproved event, duplicate canonical root, NULL/other direction or cross-account row fails. `ledger_balance=anchor.balance+COALESCE(signed_sum,0)`. The approval procedure runs the literal earlier-row anti-join plus interval anti-joins/recomputation under locks and rejects missing/extra/unapproved/unclassified rows, source mismatch, later eligible anchor/ending row or fabricated total.
- Period membership is uniformly half-open: every event, source-row, batch, claim, group, receipt, allocation and bank-transaction close predicate uses `period.starts_at <= occurred_at AND occurred_at < period.ends_at`. The close account set is exactly accounts whose active date range overlaps the period and which have at least one authoritative bank transaction in that half-open interval. Define `account_end_instant = CASE WHEN account.active_to IS NULL THEN 'infinity'::timestamptz ELSE account.active_to::timestamp AT TIME ZONE 'Asia/Seoul' END`; for each included account, `statement_end_at=LEAST(period.ends_at,account_end_instant)-interval '1 microsecond'`. This explicit KST conversion is independent of the database/session timezone. Each account requires one current approved zero-difference reconciliation ending at that instant. Accounts with no transaction in the period are excluded, never inferred from display seeds. An open next period needs no ending transaction/balance/reconciliation merely to receive a carryforward target; those become mandatory only when its reconciliation draft is later constructed.
- Cross-row financial closure is commit-time and noncircular. `event↔cashbook`, credit `cashbook↔receipt`, debit-refund `cashbook↔refund-allocation`, receipt net allocation, group totals, reversal totals, assessment-chain caps and correction-pair equations are implemented as `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED` constraint triggers on every participating table. Each mutation registers touched event/receipt/group/assessment/correction keys in transaction-local state; after the operation's already ordered first-class locks are acquired, the final deferred trigger recomputes each key once from the complete post-DML state. The canonical new-source operation order is classification/open claim root → proposed event → bound claim successor → provenance/authority → cashbook drafts → receipt/group/allocation/reversal drafts → event/cashbook approval → receipt → group/member → allocation/reversal approval; temporary cross-row incompleteness is legal inside the transaction, but immediate event/parent FKs are always satisfied before their child insert and any unsatisfied deferred equation at commit aborts all rows and audits. Row-local type/state/FK checks remain immediate.
- Close blockers are scoped to the same half-open period. An import blocker is an active economic source whose validity overlaps the period and either (a) a previewed batch/decision set contains a normalized economic date/instant with `period.starts_at <= occurred_at AND occurred_at < period.ends_at`, or (b) an applied batch has a blocked row, non-approved decision item, classification or claim in that interval. Group/receipt/allocation blockers are only roots whose event occurred in that interval. A carryforward whose `target_period_id` is the period blocks while its current tip is `open|bound`; only `resolved` clears it. Schema exception blocking is exact: every open `pre_anchor_blocking` blocks; an open `legacy_not_valid` blocks when `table_name='payments'` and its payment dues year is at most the closing KST year. Future-source rows/batches/events never block an earlier close. Missing audit rows are scoped to mutations contributing to those sets.
- Approval/close code uses the exact global locks. Period close requires `reconciling`, the account set above satisfied, zero scoped import/claim/group/unresolved-item/carryforward/schema/audit blockers and zero differences; no separate unallocated-receipt blocker exists because an approved unequal receipt is DB-impossible.
