After every `closed→open`, existing approved reconciliations remain immutable historical tips and re-close always requires a new draft with `supersedes_reconciliation_id=current approved tip`, followed by a newly approved successor whose `revalidation_digest` recomputes the exact anchor/ending/balance/item bytes. Unchanged inputs may reproduce the same digest but never reuse the old reconciliation row. Only the new approved tip may satisfy the subsequent `reconciling→closed`.

Exact legacy-payment compatibility contract:

- Eligible for automatic preview only when the frozen legacy row has `user_id IS NOT NULL`, `amount_parse.status='valid_signed'`, exact bigint(`source_amount_signed`) `>0`, `year BETWEEN 2024 AND 2026`, `type='연회비'`, `status='completed'`, `created_at IS NOT NULL`, and no open `schema_data_exceptions` exists for that `payments.id`. The first failing predicate is evaluated only in that printed order and stores respectively `LEGACY_USER_NULL|LEGACY_AMOUNT_INVALID|LEGACY_AMOUNT_NONPOSITIVE|LEGACY_YEAR_OUT_OF_RANGE|LEGACY_TYPE_NOT_ANNUAL_DUES|LEGACY_STATUS_NOT_COMPLETED|LEGACY_CREATED_AT_NULL|LEGACY_DATA_EXCEPTION_OPEN`. Zero, negative or invalid amounts remain frozen evidence but are terminal `ineligible` with no claim/event/receipt/allocation. Otherwise unresolved timezone/identity/evidence stores exactly `LEGACY_TIMEZONE_UNRESOLVED|LEGACY_IDENTITY_AMBIGUOUS|LEGACY_EVIDENCE_AMBIGUOUS` and is nonterminal `review|quarantine`.
- `payments.year` is the authoritative dues-year attribution. `created_at` is interpreted using the source database `SHOW TimeZone` captured in the preview receipt; absence/invalid timezone or a timestamp/year contradiction produces `review`.
- Candidate search is `member_id + dues_year + amount + occurred calendar date within ±1 KST day`; it is evidence discovery, never an identity/UNIQUE. Acquire the three date−1/date/date+1 candidate advisory locks using the exact 256→64-bit transform above before searching or inserting. Exactly one finalized-ledger/2026-bank event with a current non-quarantine authority decision → atomically create the source-row claim/binding, persist `cross_link`, and add `supporting` legacy provenance; zero candidates → create the proposed compatibility event, bind the unique `payments:<id>` source-row claim to that now-existing event, then append provenance/authority and financial children in the exact execution topology; more than one candidate or identity ambiguity → persist `quarantine`. Late equal evidence may canonicalize only through the exact both-proposed/childless duplicate-reject→canonicalization→collision-resolve command; otherwise the decision becomes quarantine. It never silently creates/merges by tuple. Group-roster evidence never creates a receipt/event.
- `cross_link` is legal only when the one candidate already has an approved dues receipt and its locked net approved allocation projection contains exactly this legacy member/year/amount; otherwise the decision is `review` and creates no financial child. The legacy provenance supports that existing topology and never adds a second receipt/allocation. `new_compatibility_event` is one atomic operation that creates an approved legacy-compatibility credit event, authority selection for the rank-200 legacy row, approved `DUES_INCOME` cashbook entry, approved receipt for the full amount, and one approved ordinary-dues payment allocation to the resolved member/year; every cashbook/receipt/allocation equation must pass. `review|quarantine` for an otherwise eligible row may create only its decision and open claim root but no bound claim/event/financial child and blocks cutover. `ineligible` creates only its terminal decision and frozen source/exception evidence—never a claim. No proposed compatibility root may count in reads.
- The exact cutover comparison partitions every payment ID through the watermark from its frozen `payments:<id>` row version and terminal decision, never from the post-fence live `payments.user_id`. Every excluded row must have terminal decision `ineligible` and exact reason, appears in neither comparison side, and cannot contribute an event/allocation. Eligible old rich rows `{payment_id,member_uid,dues_year,amount,status:"completed",source_content_digest}` are obtained from decision→preview batch→immutable row version and compared with new `{payment_id,decision,current_event_uid,member_uid,dues_year,net_approved_allocation_amount,source_content_digest}` through decision→provenance/created event→receipt→allocation. It projects both sides to identical array items `{payment_id,member_uid,dues_year,amount,source_content_digest}` sorted by payment ID and hashes `RFC8785({digest_version:"legacy-cutover-comparison-v1",watermark_payment_id,rows})`; that is the fenced/new/read-rollback `comparison_digest`. Success requires every eligible row to have `cross_link|new_compatibility_event`, identical member/year/amount/digest, no duplicate event/allocation contribution, and equal grouped totals by `(member_uid,dues_year)` and globally. `review|quarantine`, a wrong excluded decision or any mismatch blocks the switch.
- Freeze/cutover order is the DB-resident contract above: atomically append `fenced` under the table/state lock and record the stable watermark → backfill exactly through that watermark while writes are rejected → compare complete decisions/signed reads → atomically recheck watermark/digests and append `new` with the read switch → retain `read_rollback`. No polling-based zero scan authorizes cutover.
- The sole post-legacy `payments` mutation exception preserves existing account-deletion behavior: an account-deletion operation whose receipt actor/target user is the locked deleted user may update only rows with `payments.user_id=actor_user_id`, and the exact transition is `user_id: actor_user_id→NULL` with every other column byte-identical. The same operation must enumerate the payment rows in its result registry, hold actor/operation/payment locks in global order, append the deletion audit/identity unlink evidence, and then delete the user. INSERT, DELETE, another-column UPDATE, another user, absent receipt or use outside account deletion is rejected in `fenced|new|read_rollback`.

Round-32 execution-closure contract (normative; this section replaces any earlier abbreviated import, provenance, reservation, deletion or restore wording that is less specific):

- **Preview and apply are separate durable transactions.** `import_batch:preview` atomically inserts the `accounting_import_batches(status='previewed')` root, source release, coordinates, immutable new-or-reused row versions, batch-row links, one complete `source_decision_sets(status='previewed')` row and all required decision items. It creates no classification, match case/candidate, party alias, period, claim, event, provenance, bank transaction, cashbook, receipt, group or allocation. The committed preview is what the authenticated GET displays. A later `import_batch:apply` command is the sole approval command: under one operation receipt it locks/revalidates the batch, release, live source fingerprint, complete manifest and item coverage; transitions the set `previewed→approved`; transitions the batch `previewed→applied`; materializes approved period rows first; then creates the remaining approved downstream graphs in the orders below. Human reject transitions only the set `previewed→rejected`, leaves the batch `previewed`, creates no downstream row and releases the live-set key for a fresh complete preview; only an immutable source-integrity failure may transition the batch `previewed→failed`. Supersession remains the separately printed complete replacement-set transaction. No row/version/link/decision-item insert is legal in the apply transaction, and no batch can be `applied` before its already-persisted set becomes approved.
- **Exact source-family and classification mapping is closed.** `final-ledger-coordinate-v1/economic→historical_cashbook`; `bank-row-v1→bank`; `legacy-payment-row-v1→legacy_decision_only`; final-ledger `period_metadata`, `period-boundary-v1`, `role-row-v1`, `allocation-roster-row-v1` and `policy-row-v1` create no economic event. Legacy decision-only rows never enter the classification trigger and are checked by the dedicated legacy graph trigger. An approved `non_economic` classification is exactly `(event_kind=non_economic,direction=none,party_kind=non_dues,dues_year=NULL,splits=[])`. A positive dues credit is exactly `(dues,credit,member|group,dues_year nonnull)` with only `DUES_INCOME` splits. A mixed credit is exactly `(mixed,credit,member|group,dues_year nonnull)` with at least one `DUES_INCOME` and one `OTHER_INCOME` split and no other category. A non-dues row is exactly `(non_dues,credit,non_dues,NULL,[OTHER_INCOME])` or `(non_dues,debit,non_dues,NULL,[GENERAL_EXPENSE])`. A transfer is exactly `(transfer,credit,non_dues,NULL,[INTERNAL_TRANSFER_IN])` or `(transfer,debit,non_dues,NULL,[INTERNAL_TRANSFER_OUT])`. A dues refund is the sole debit dues shape: `(dues,debit,member,dues_year nonnull,[DUES_REFUND],reverses_event_uid and refund_receipt_uid both nonnull)`. Each bracketed split set is nonempty, positive, totals the normalized positive amount and uses only the listed codes. `unmatched` is never an approved economic party: it remains reject/quarantine with no child. A deferred trigger joins nonlegacy source family, decision item, classification, event, cashbook and receipt/refund branch and rejects every unlisted combination.
- **Payer/member matching is physically representable and coordinate-bound.** `member_match_cases` adds `subject_kind text NOT NULL CHECK IN ('role_roster','economic_payer','group_roster','legacy_payment')`, `subject_name_digest char(64) NOT NULL` and nullable `subject_generation_snapshot text`. Role/group cases copy the normalized name digest and require the normalized generation; economic-payer cases copy the normalized `payer_digest` and require generation NULL; legacy cases copy the frozen identity digest and allow generation NULL. Every source-backed case stores its `source_row_version_id` and approved `member_match` `decision_item_id`; every candidate stores that same item. `source_row_classification_decisions` adds nullable `member_match_case_id`, `member_match_candidate_id` and `member_match_decision_item_id`. When `party_kind='member'`, all three and `member_id` are required and a deferred trigger proves: the classification item and match item belong to the same approved set, coordinate and row version; the match case is the current approved successor; its sole approved candidate references that match item; and candidate/member/classification member IDs are identical. For every other party kind all three are NULL.
- **Receipt and allocation provenance is exhaustive.** The strict classification payload adds `receipt_uid_or_null` and `allocation_request_uid_or_null` in addition to the existing refund fields. An approved individual dues or mixed credit preallocates both, and its classification item authorizes the same event/member/year/dues-split amount, new receipt and one ordinary-dues allocation. An approved group credit preallocates the receipt UID; each group-allocation item preallocates its own allocation request/group-member UID and authorizes its member/year/amount. `dues_receipts` adds nullable `decision_item_id` and nullable `legacy_decision_id`; `dues_allocations` adds nullable `legacy_decision_id`. The root-provenance XOR is exact: imported individual receipt/allocation reference the approved classification item; imported group receipt references the classification item while each allocation references its approved group-allocation item; legacy compatibility receipt/allocation reference the terminal `legacy_payment_decisions` successor; correction/refund rows use their printed original/reversal topology and both source-decision/legacy fields are NULL. Same-coordinate, UID, member, year and amount equality is deferred and mandatory. A legacy NULL-decision shortcut is forbidden.
- **Period materialization and boundary identity are exact.** Decision kind additionally permits `period_materialization` with strict payload `{outcome,period_code,starts_at,ends_at,boundary_source_code,boundary_coordinate_key,boundary_content_digest}`. Each finalized-ledger metadata or period-boundary coordinate has exactly one such item; no economic item is allowed for it. `accounting_periods` stores both `boundary_source_row_version_id` (the period metadata row) and `boundary_evidence_row_version_id` (the exact referenced boundary evidence). The normalized metadata/boundary object replaces the ambiguous lone key with `{period_code,starts_at,ends_at,boundary_source_code,boundary_coordinate_key,boundary_content_digest}`. The trigger resolves `(boundary_source_code,boundary_coordinate_key)` through that logical source's unique coordinate, locks the immutable row version, requires the content digest and instant to match, and stores its ID. Apply creates/validates all approved periods before any economic classification. Source code plus coordinate plus digest—or the stored direct FK—always identifies the boundary; coordinate key alone never does.
- **Legacy source amounts have a signed evidence envelope.** `legacy-payment-row-v1` alone defines `source_amount_signed` with pattern `^(0|[1-9][0-9]*|-[1-9][0-9]*)$` and `amount_parse` as the strict object `{status:"valid_signed"|"invalid",raw_digest,source_amount_signed_or_null}`; the other financial schemas retain unsigned `money`. The normalized legacy object preserves the frozen signed value (including zero/negative) or a redacted parse-error digest. Only `status='valid_signed'` and bigint value `>0` may enter the eligible claim/event path. Zero and negative database amounts receive the exact `legacy_not_valid` exception plus terminal `ineligible` decision; an adapter parse failure carries only its immutable parse-error digest plus terminal `LEGACY_AMOUNT_INVALID` decision because no invalid scalar is written back to the typed legacy source table. All three create no claim/event/receipt/allocation. Signed values are used only in the frozen old-side audit projection; all financial business amounts remain positive bigint; `-0`, `+0`, leading zeroes and leading plus signs are invalid evidence strings.
- **Identity-ID reservation covers every new row, including support and audit rows.** After rank-10/rank-20 locks and committed-retry lookup, the generator expands a complete `reservation_slots` array. Sort key is `(phase,result_ordinal,slot_kind_order,qualified_table_name,local_ordinal)` with phases `0=operation_receipt`, `10=result_business`, `20=support`, `30=audit` and slot-kind order `receipt=0,business=10,history_or_activity=20,audit=30`. Slot 0 reserves the root `business_operation_receipts.id`; every result then lists every optimistic identity row it creates; support slots include all VCHAIN/history/activity rows even when they reuse a parent result/audit; audit slots include exactly one `accounting_audit_events.id` per result. Composite-key `business_operation_entities` consumes no slot. The manifest owns the literal per-command slot template and catalog sequence for every slot, proves the expansion is a bijection with the execution graph, calls `nextval` once per slot in the sorted order, and puts the full array in the hashed payload. Race, abort and retry rules from the reservation contract apply to the whole array, not only primary business rows.
- **Account deletion includes prospective actor-FK rows.** After ID/correlation reservation, the registry enumerates both pre-existing references and every prospective row created by the same deletion operation whose actor/target FK will be nulled by the final user delete, including the root operation receipt, mutable history/activity or identity-link support rows and all newly appended audit rows. The hashed list is `{origin:"preexisting"|"prospective",table,primary_key,column,operation_uid,action_correlation_uid_or_null}` sorted by the printed lock key. The transaction locks the pre-existing set and verifies prospective reserved keys are absent, then inserts only the declared prospective rows, re-enumerates the union immediately before delete and requires byte-identical equality. The mechanical-null trigger accepts a prospective row only when its operation UID/root target snapshot and, where present, action correlation equal this same locked account-delete operation. Any actor FK added by another operation, any undeclared newly created row or any mismatch aborts. Thus the deletion's own receipts/history/audits are durable with scalar actor snapshots and NULL live FKs after delete without escaping the predeclared set.
- **New-source and legacy dependency order is literal.** After the apply transitions and period creation: per economic coordinate execute member match case/candidate approval or party/alias creation → classification → open claim root → proposed event → bound claim successor → provenance → authority → bank transaction if applicable → cashbook/receipt/group/allocation drafts → their approvals → event approval. The refund-classified branch stops at the proposed bank graph until `receipt:refund`. Legacy `new_compatibility_event` executes open claim root → proposed event → bound claim successor → provenance → authority → terminal `legacy_payment_decisions` successor → cashbook/receipt/allocation drafts referencing that terminal decision → financial approvals → event approval. Reverse-order fixtures must fail for classification-before-match-or-party, provenance-before-bound-claim, claim-bind-before-event, receipt/allocation-before-authorizing terminal decision, period-after-classification and approval-before-complete-deferred equations.
- **Restore comparison uses one retained MVCC snapshot.** Connection A runs `BEGIN ISOLATION LEVEL REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY`, then records `pg_export_snapshot()`, `txid_current_snapshot()`, `current_database()`, `current_user`, `clock_timestamp()` and `pg_current_wal_lsn()` and remains open. `pg_dump` connects to the same database with `--snapshot=<exported_snapshot_id>` before A ends; all source row counts/content digests and target-neutral catalog digests are queried on connection A under that same snapshot. Only after dump completion and source evidence serialization may A commit. A write freeze is not inferred. The restore receipt adds `source_snapshot_id_sha256`, `source_txid_snapshot`, `source_snapshot_started_at`, `source_snapshot_finished_at` and binds the dump SHA plus all source digests to them; snapshot ID raw text is never logged. Any lost exporter transaction, snapshot import error, query outside A, source database mismatch or digest collected before/after that snapshot fails the drill.

Round-33 execution-closure contract (normative; it narrows and, where wording differs, replaces the earlier abbreviated contracts):

- **Every import composite is registered.** `import_batch:preview` has one root receipt and these dependency-topological execution steps: zero-or-one `source_release:create` → `import_batch:preview` root referencing that existing/new release → coordinates by key → new row-version roots/successors by coordinate → batch-row links by ordinal → `source_decision_set:preview` → decision-item creates by `(coordinate,kind)`. Canonical receipt results remain independently rank-sorted and therefore also place rank-100 source release before rank-110 batch when a new release exists. Its reservation slots contain the operation receipt, every new business/VCHAIN row, and one audit per result. `import_batch:apply` has set-approval and batch-apply transitions plus the downstream results below. `source_decision_set:reject` transitions only the set and leaves the batch previewed; its slots are receipt plus one audit. `source_decision_set:repreview` then creates only a fresh complete set/items on that existing batch with the exact result/slot/order contract printed above. `import_batch:fail` is reserved solely for parser/source-integrity failure before any approved set and transitions the batch `previewed→failed`; it is not a human decision. The manifest action/composite registry lists all five templates and proves result/slot/execution bijection. A fixture requiring a new release must fail if either the canonical result order or DML schedule places the batch before its immediately referenced release.
- **Member/party prerequisites precede classification.** For a member classification: approved match item → case root → candidate root → candidate approval → case approval → classification. For group/non-dues: event-party root → required aliases → classification. Only then may the operation create an open claim root, proposed event, bound claim successor, provenance and authority. Immediate FKs are never deferred. Reverse-order fixtures cover classification before case/candidate approval or party creation.
- **Rejection and re-review have one live path.** A rejected decision set releases only the live-set key; its batch remains `previewed`, and a new complete set with fresh UID/items may be previewed and approved. A decision item with outcome `reject` creates no classification/match/party/receipt/allocation row at all. A `quarantine` classification root may exist and uses the existing zero-descendant `quarantined→approved` successor path. Re-review of an applied batch uses complete approved-set supersession; a formerly rejected item had no classification root, so its newly approved replacement creates the first root, while a quarantined item creates the allowed successor. `accounting_import_batches(source_release_id,source_fingerprint)` remains unique because re-review reuses the batch. Failed batches are terminal source-integrity evidence and require a new source release/fingerprint, never same-byte human retry.
- **The physical classification status/action domain follows that rejection path.** This bullet supersedes the earlier table-row shorthand: `source_row_classification_decisions.status` is exactly `approved|quarantined`; its event-kind domain is exactly `bank|historical_cashbook|adjustment|non_economic`; a root is created only for an approved-set item whose outcome is `approve|quarantine`; and the exhaustive action map is `source_row_classification:approve|quarantine|resolve|supersede`. There is no classification `rejected` row, no `legacy_compatibility` source classification, and no `source_row_classification:reject` result. The manifest expander, actor/action fixture, table CHECKs, lifecycle registry and audit allowlist must all emit this narrowed domain; any generated artifact that retains the broader shorthand fails plan lint.
- **Group payments use a multi-batch atomic apply.** The primary bank classification payload adds `group_roster_batch_uid_or_null`; group allocation payload adds `primary_bank_batch_uid_or_null,primary_bank_coordinate_key_or_null` and the same `receipt_uid_or_null`. A group classification requires those cross-references; ordinary shapes require them NULL. The authenticated apply of the primary bank decision set deterministically discovers every referenced roster batch/set, locks the primary plus companion batches/sets in UID order, validates each roster batch is previewed, each receipt UID/coordinate/payer/group digest and sum agree, and in one operation approves every set, applies every batch and materializes the whole graph. A companion roster set cannot be directly applied and returns `requires_primary_bank_batch`. The receipt returns ordered `applied_batch_uids` and `approved_decision_set_uids`; rollback restores all statuses and rows.
- **Legacy uses decision-only authority, not source classification.** `legacy-payment-row-v1` creates batch/coordinate/row-version/link evidence but is excluded from `source_decision_sets`, `source_decision_items` and `source_row_classification_decisions`. Its closed mapping is `legacy-payment-row-v1→legacy_decision_only`; only a terminal `legacy_payment_decisions` successor may authorize a `legacy_compatibility` event and its receipt/allocation graph. The legacy trigger verifies frozen row version, eligibility, member/year/signed-positive amount, event direction/kind/category, claim/provenance/authority and terminal-decision FKs directly. Todo 19 owns this preview/materialization; Todo 18's decision coverage excludes legacy coordinates.
- **Imported financial roots never persist a rejected attempt.** An import apply inserts proposed receipt/allocation rows and approves them in the same transaction; any failed equation rolls back the whole operation, leaving the preallocated UID unused. Outcome `reject|quarantine` creates no such row. Rejected terminal receipt/allocation rows exist only for separately authorized non-import attempts; a retry uses a fresh caller UUID and operation UID after the partial natural key is released. An imported semantic retry requires complete decision-set replacement with fresh preallocated UIDs before any descendant; it never reuses a committed rejected UID.
- **There is no approved unallocated receipt balance.** The receipt-net-allocation equality holds on every commit that approves a receipt. Special-assessment excess must, in the same operation, be allocated to ordinary dues under an approved classification split or the receipt remains proposed and the transaction cannot approve; a later cash refund requires the bank-refund topology. No table/state represents approved unallocated cash, and period close has no separate unallocated-balance blocker because the deferred receipt equality already makes it impossible.
- **Account deletion identifies non-FK session/pending rows exactly.** The root input contains `current_session_sid_digest=sha256(authenticated request sid)` and deletes exactly that locked current session row; other stale sessions cannot authenticate after the user row is deleted and expire through retention. Under the shared rank-15 Kakao-identity lock it snapshots the locked target user's Kakao ID, checks/inserts the termination identity and enumerates/deletes every `pending_registrations` row whose `kakao_id` is byte-equal, ordered by ID. Their prelock projections are respectively `{sid_digest}`, `{identity_hash}` and `{pending_id,kakao_id_digest}`; all are explicit registered results/audits and included in union graph rechecks. Pending writers acquire the same stable key and recheck termination, so no equal-identity phantom may commit after enumeration. No email/name match, digest-only equality or JSON-session scan is allowed.
- **N1 authoring cutover precedes source mapping.** The 2026-08-09 owner decisions supersede Todo 23's intermediate Sheet publication and the later direct-payload route. N1 consolidates historical workbook tabs, existing Notion pages, the current 22nd-administration roster and alumni-faculty data into the unified Notion database and leaves only the integrated address book active in Sheets. Todo 14 profiles those two activated sources and approves mappings before Todo 13 or 18 consumes them. The dependency path is `N1→14→13→18`.
- **Match scoring is closed.** `score_basis` is included in the strict member-match payload, GET response and digest. It is derived one-to-one from evidence kind: `name_only→name_only_unapprovable`, `existing_fk→exact_existing_user_fk`, `roster_member_uid→exact_roster_member_uid`, `canonical_email→exact_canonical_email`, `canonical_phone→exact_canonical_phone`, `canonical_mobile→exact_canonical_mobile`, `board_roster_row→exact_board_name_generation_position`, `bank_payer_reference→exact_bank_payer_alias`, `manual_document→manual_document_reference`. Caller-selected alternatives fail; fixtures cover every mapping and digest.
- **Reasons and reconciliation explanations are code-only.** Every audit row derives `reason_code='audit/'||entity_type||'/'||action` from its matched operation result; it is never caller text. Business tables use their own closed codes. Legacy static-failure priority is exactly `LEGACY_USER_NULL`, `LEGACY_AMOUNT_INVALID`, `LEGACY_AMOUNT_NONPOSITIVE`, `LEGACY_YEAR_OUT_OF_RANGE`, `LEGACY_TYPE_NOT_ANNUAL_DUES`, `LEGACY_STATUS_NOT_COMPLETED`, `LEGACY_CREATED_AT_NULL`, `LEGACY_DATA_EXCEPTION_OPEN`; ambiguity codes are `LEGACY_TIMEZONE_UNRESOLVED|LEGACY_IDENTITY_AMBIGUOUS|LEGACY_EVIDENCE_AMBIGUOUS`. `bank_reconciliation_items.note` is replaced by nullable `note_code` with `TIMING_POSTING_DATE|TIMING_BANK_FEE|TIMING_DUPLICATE_PENDING|TIMING_OTHER_DOCUMENTED`; carryforward `noncash_explanation` is replaced by nullable `resolution_reason_code` with `NONCASH_BANK_ERROR|NONCASH_LEDGER_CORRECTION|NONCASH_DOCUMENTED_WAIVER` plus nullable `supporting_document_digest char(64)`. No free text is stored; shape checks and digests cover the codes/document digest.
- **The physical business-reason registry is exhaustive and DB-enforced.** This bullet supersedes every earlier nullable/free-form shorthand for the named columns: each is `text NOT NULL` with a named literal CHECK, and a paired transition CHECK/trigger derives or validates the one legal code before DML. `mutable_entity_action_history.reason_code` is exactly `MEMBER_ENDED|MEMBER_REACTIVATED|MEMBER_IDENTITY_CORRECTED|ACCOUNT_DELETE_UNLINK|PERIOD_RECONCILE_REQUESTED|PERIOD_CLOSE_APPROVED|PERIOD_REOPEN_APPROVED`: member `end|reactivate` use the first two, ordinary admin `correct` uses `MEMBER_IDENTITY_CORRECTED`, the self-delete `correct` uses `ACCOUNT_DELETE_UNLINK`, and period `reconcile|close|reopen` use the last three respectively. `member_match_cases.reason_code` is exactly `SOURCE_MATCH_REQUIRED|MANUAL_MATCH_REQUIRED|NAME_ONLY_UNAPPROVABLE|NO_CANDIDATE|MULTIPLE_CANDIDATES|MATCH_APPROVED|MATCH_REJECTED|MATCH_SUPERSEDED`: a version-1 unmatched source/manual/name-only/no-candidate case uses the corresponding first/fourth code, a version-1 candidate uses `SOURCE_MATCH_REQUIRED|MANUAL_MATCH_REQUIRED|MULTIPLE_CANDIDATES` as derived from source kind and candidate count, approved/rejected successors use their exact terminal code, and a same-state successor uses only `MATCH_SUPERSEDED`. `member_identity_link_history.reason_code` is exactly `IDENTITY_LINKED|IDENTITY_CORRECTED|IDENTITY_USER_UNLINKED|IDENTITY_ALL_UNLINKED|ACCOUNT_DELETE_UNLINK`: operations `link|correct|unlink_all` map one-to-one to the first, second and fourth; ordinary admin `unlink_user` uses the third and the self-delete branch uses the fifth. `economic_event_authority_decisions.reason_code` is exactly `HIGHEST_AUTHORITY_SELECTED|HIGHER_AUTHORITY_SUPERSEDED|AUTHORITY_TIE_QUARANTINED|AUTHORITY_INVALID_QUARANTINED`: `select` uses the first, `supersede` the second, and `quarantine` one of the last two according to the validated tie/invalid predicate. `economic_event_canonicalizations.reason_code` is exactly `CHILDLESS_DUPLICATE_CANONICALIZED`. `economic_event_collisions.reason_code` is exactly `POTENTIAL_DUPLICATE_OPEN|COLLISION_REVIEW_REQUIRED|CHILDLESS_DUPLICATE_RESOLVED`: an open root uses one of the first two from the collision predicate, an unresolved same-state successor uses `COLLISION_REVIEW_REQUIRED`, and only the printed zero-child resolved successor uses the last. `legacy_payment_decisions.reason_code` is exactly the eight static-failure codes and three ambiguity codes in the preceding bullet plus `LEGACY_CROSS_LINK_MATCHED|LEGACY_COMPATIBILITY_EVENT_CREATED`; `cross_link` and `new_compatibility_event` use those two success codes, `ineligible` uses exactly the first failing static code, and `review|quarantine` uses exactly one ambiguity code. `dues_receipt_reversals.reason_code` is exactly `BANK_DUES_REFUND` for every proposed/approved/rejected version.
- The same physical manifest adds two nullable, closed correction-code columns that are not covered by the preceding NOT NULL rule. `member_position_assignments.override_reason` has named CHECK `override_reason IS NULL OR override_reason='OWNER_APPROVED_21ST_TERM_TO_AGM36_CLOSE'`; it is nonnull iff the row is the authorized corrective 21st-administration assignment version whose `effective_to` is exactly `2026-02-28T12:38:00+09:00`, whose administration number is 21 and whose member, position, appointment date/basis and source provenance are byte-copied from its parent. Every other root/version requires NULL. `dues_allocations` adds nullable `correction_reason_code text` with named CHECK domain exactly `source_error|false_transaction`; it is nonnull iff `rights_effect_mode='retroactive_error'`, and that mode is legal only in `allocation:correct` or the strict correction branch of `receipt:refund`. Every cancellation/refund/replacement allocation in either authorized operation copies the same code, original effective instant and bound correction evidence; `immediate_positive|next_month_negative` require NULL. The per-action input schema exposes this enum only for those two authorized commands, with the refund null/non-null pair and shape fixed above; the dues audit JSON allowlist adds `override_reason` only for position rows, the financial audit JSON allowlist adds `correction_reason_code` only for allocation rows, and both columns participate in the exact canonical operation payload/before-after digest. Unknown, free-text, mismatched or missing codes fail before DML.
- `schema_data_exceptions.rule_code` is also manifest-owned and never caller text. The exact `pre_anchor_blocking` codes are `USERS_EMAIL_CANONICAL_BLANK|USERS_EMAIL_CANONICAL_DUPLICATE|USERS_PHONE_CANONICAL_INVALID|USERS_PHONE_CANONICAL_DUPLICATE|USERS_BIRTHDAY_TYPE_UNKNOWN|CATEGORIES_BADGE_VARIANT_UNKNOWN|CATEGORIES_SORT_ORDER_NEGATIVE|ALUMNI_MOBILE_CANONICAL_INVALID|ALUMNI_MOBILE_CANONICAL_DUPLICATE|ALUMNI_MATCHED_USER_DUPLICATE|COMMUNITY_EVENT_LEGACY_OBITUARY_ORPHAN|COMMUNITY_EVENT_TYPE_UNKNOWN|COMMUNITY_EVENT_STATUS_UNKNOWN|EVENT_PARSE_REQUEST_COUNT_NEGATIVE|EVENT_PARSE_UPDATED_BEFORE_WINDOW|PENDING_EMAIL_BLANK|PENDING_KAKAO_DUPLICATE|PENDING_EMAIL_CANONICAL_DUPLICATE|PENDING_STATUS_NULL|PENDING_STATUS_UNKNOWN|KAKAO_OAUTH_EXPIRES_NOT_AFTER_START`. The exact `legacy_not_valid` codes are `PAYMENTS_AMOUNT_NONPOSITIVE|PAYMENTS_YEAR_OUT_OF_RANGE|PAYMENTS_TYPE_UNKNOWN|PAYMENTS_STATUS_UNKNOWN`. Each code maps one-to-one to the correspondingly named additive CHECK/UNIQUE/FK predicate in the existing-table manifest; the sequence-15 capture query has one explicit branch per code and no generic fallback. Thus every brownfield value that could make a sequence-20 object fail is representable before DDL, while an unlisted violation aborts capture rather than inventing a rule code. `resolution_code` is NULL on an open root and exactly `SOURCE_FIXED|DUPLICATE_RESOLVED` on `resolved` or `OWNER_WAIVER` on `waived`; the duplicate code requires a duplicate-class rule, and the existing no-successor predicate still makes every `pre_anchor_blocking` root terminal in this plan. Every external per-action command schema exposes an enum only when a human must select among otherwise legal codes; deterministic codes are absent from caller input and DB-derived. Audit JSON only copies a validated live code. Unknown code, free-text explanation, status/code mismatch, extra command property or a value not reproduced from locked evidence fails before any business DML/audit row. The manifest validator and Todo 6/7/20 negative fixtures enumerate every code/predicate/status tuple, require `USERS_EMAIL_CANONICAL_BLANK` for nonnull `btrim(email)=''`, and reject one altered-case, unknown, uncovered-domain and free-text value for every registry.
- **Signed legacy zero is canonical.** The only valid signed-money regex is `^(0|[1-9][0-9]*|-[1-9][0-9]*)$`; `-0`, plus signs and leading zeros fail parsing and preserve only the invalid raw digest. Parser/digest fixtures prove `0` has exactly one representation.
- **Performance work cannot mutate the manifest opportunistically.** Todo 8 measures the fixed mandatory FK/idempotency/period/state indexes and records proposed future candidates, but adds none. Todo 21 remeasures only those manifest-owned indexes and may reject a plan regression; no composite/expression/trigram/FTS index is adopted in this plan. Any future candidate requires a separately approved manifest/artifact migration.
- **Annual member population is one predicate.** `dues_year_members(Y)` is every association member whose append-only activity intervals overlap `[Y-01-01T00:00:00+09:00,(Y+1)-01-01T00:00:00+09:00)`, regardless of live status at activation time. Members ended before Y or first joined after Y are excluded; any member active for at least one instant in Y is included, and honorary members are included with zero current assessment. Tier derivation, highest-position scan, default-pledge creation and activation completeness all use this exact set and the same locked interval projection.

- The committed `docs/source-authority/22nd-officers.json` at payload commit `9922cf3eccb65fa565380f9e7549628721602041` is retained only as immutable N1 seed and AGM-boundary provenance; its source revision, 33×10 payload and self-hash remain verifiable.
- No Drive capability receipt, Sheet prewrite receipt, Sheet outcome receipt or post-tab workbook revision is part of the post-N1 source contract. Those former authorization surfaces are superseded and cannot authorize sequence 50.
- Todo 14 profiles only `MEMBERSHIP_INTEGRATED_ADDRESS_BOOK` and `NOTION_ORGANIZATION_ROLE_HISTORY`; it never re-fetches a removed role tab or promotes the frozen payload to a current direct-import source.
- Todo 14 descriptor-opens and rehashes the frozen payload receipt solely to bind provenance and the `AGM36_PERIOD_BOUNDARY` constant-authority release. Role rows come only from the activated Notion profile/map; member identity rows come only from the surviving integrated-address-book profile/map.
- Todo 18 imports the two live editorial sources under the ordinary preview/decision/apply receipt contract. Repeating identical source bytes is a verified no-op; changed source bytes require a new revision, profile, mapping approval and release.
- F4 validates the frozen payload receipt, N1 activation receipt, both Todo 14 source profiles/maps/approvals and the new canonical manifest binding; it rejects any capability/prewrite/outcome artifact as an executable dependency.

Exact authenticated source-decision approval and accounting-self API contracts:

- Todo 15 owns `GET /api/admin/accounting/source-decisions/:decisionSetUid` and `POST /api/admin/accounting/source-decisions/:decisionSetUid/decision`. Both require the existing saved session, `req.session.userId`, `requireAdmin`, a live user whose ID+UID equal Todo 17's frozen Development-admin receipt, and the configured same-origin `Origin|Host` plus same-origin fetch metadata; missing/mismatched auth returns `401|403` with zero DB write. Neither route accepts a user/batch/source query parameter. GET returns `additionalProperties:false` `{schemaVersion:"source-decision-review-v2",decisionSetUid,batchUid,manifestSha256,sourceFingerprint,status,items}` where status is `previewed|approved|rejected|superseded` and items is the stored ordinal-sorted exact array `{ordinal,coordinateKey,sourceContentDigest,reviewDisplay,decisionKind,decisionPayload,decisionPayloadSha256}`. `reviewDisplay` is an `additionalProperties:false` per-source projection of only the approved work-relevant snapshots (such as member/payer/group name and transaction description) joined from the immutable row version; it is never copied into the decision manifest, POST body, operation receipt, log or Git evidence. Contact/address/workplace, account/CMS identifiers, token/provider body and actor snapshot are never returned.
- POST requires `Content-Type: application/json` and the exact `additionalProperties:false` body `{schemaVersion:"source-decision-command-v1",operationUid,manifestSha256,sourceFingerprint,decision,replacementDecisionSetUid,replacementManifest,replacementItems,replacementManifestSha256}` where operation UID is lowercase UUIDv4 and decision is `approve|reject|repreview|supersede`. For `approve|reject`, all four replacement fields are JSON null. For `repreview|supersede`, they are respectively a new lowercase UUIDv4, the complete strict `source-decision-preview-v1` manifest for the same batch/source rows, the full ordinal-sorted array of exact item objects, and the RFC-8785 manifest SHA; partial replacement is invalid. `replacementItems` is `additionalProperties:false` at both array-item and nested payload level and each item is exactly `{ordinal,coordinateKey,sourceContentDigest,decisionKind,decisionPayload,decisionPayloadSha256}`. The payload is one of the four strict redacted classification/member-match/group-allocation/period-materialization shapes printed for `source_decision_items`; ordinal, coordinate, source digest, kind and payload SHA must byte-match exactly one manifest item, SHA-256 must equal the RFC-8785 payload bytes, every manifest item must occur once, and no extra item is legal. Under the actor→operation→primary batch→companion batch→coordinate/row→decision-set/item locks, the server re-fetches/recomputes source fingerprints, canonical manifests and coverage and invokes the registered composite. Approve discovers companion roster sets from group payloads; applies the primary alone or all primary+companions atomically and forbids direct companion apply. Reject performs only set `previewed→rejected`, leaves batch `previewed` and consumes no downstream UID. Repreview is legal only when the path set is rejected and its batch remains previewed; it creates the supplied fresh preview set/items only, returns them for another GET review, and performs no approval/apply/downstream materialization. Supersede is legal only for an approved set with the printed zero-descendant predicates and performs atomic old-approved→superseded plus complete replacement-preview→approved and only the permitted resolution successors. Stale, missing, already-terminal with different bytes, cross-target, reused replacement UID, incomplete/cross-batch coverage, missing/extra item or manifest/payload SHA mismatch fails before DML. Same operation UID/identical canonical command bytes is the universal idempotent retry.
- A successful POST returns canonical JSON `dgkma-source-decision-approval-v2`; its exact `additionalProperties:false` shape is `{schema_version,operation_uid,primary_decision_set_uid,primary_batch_uid,applied_batch_uids,approved_decision_set_uids,manifest_sha256,source_fingerprint,decision,replacement_decision_set_uid,replacement_manifest_sha256,actor_user_id,actor_user_uid,authorization_version,target_fingerprint,decided_at,operation_payload_sha256,receipt_sha256}`. Ordered batch/set arrays contain one item for ordinary approve, every locked primary+companion for group approve, and are empty for reject and repreview. Reject leaves the primary batch previewed with no live set; repreview names the fresh replacement UID/SHA but leaves that new set previewed for the next GET and later distinct approve command. Supersede names the replacement and returns the applied batch/set arrays affected by its resolution. Self-hash excludes only itself; every field equals locked operation/set/batch/actor rows. Todo 18's receipt collection remains the nonempty exact receipt array sorted by `(primary_decision_set_uid,operation_uid)` and self-hashed. The authenticated command, operation receipt, all set/batch transition actors and audit correlations bind the live administrator to the exact manifest/apply result; loose rows/flags/fixtures cannot substitute.

- Todo 15 owns `GET /api/accounting/me`. It requires the existing saved session and `req.session.userId`; missing/invalid session returns `401` with the existing generic auth error and performs zero DB write. The path accepts no user/member/year query parameter, so an administrator cannot use it to read another member. A live user with no current association-member link returns `200` with the exact null projection rather than leaking match candidates.
- Response schema `accounting-self-v1` is `additionalProperties:false` and exactly `{schemaVersion:"accounting-self-v1",asOf,currentDuesYear,member,dues}`. `asOf` is RFC-3339, year is integer `2024..2100`; `member` is either null or exactly `{memberUid,memberKind,status,displayName,generation}`; `dues` is either null or exactly `{tierCode,monthlyMinimum,annualMinimum,personalPledgeMonthly,paidTotal,policyShortfall,pledgeShortfall,specialAssessmentRequired,specialAssessmentPaid,rightsStatus,reasonCode}`. Every amount is a canonical nonnegative decimal **string** or null, never a JavaScript number. `rightsStatus` is `rights_member|member|honorary|unavailable`; `unavailable` requires null tier/minima/shortfalls/reason and is returned when no approved live policy/tier/snapshot exists. `displayName` and generation are self-only; bank row, payer name/digest, contact/address/workplace, match candidates, actor snapshots and audit JSON are forbidden.
- The query locks nothing and selects the current active/ended link for the session user, then the latest published tier/pledge/rights tips as of `asOf`; it does not derive rights from draft policy or legacy `payments`. Tests require anonymous 401, linked self 200 with exact string amounts, unmatched self 200 with `member:null,dues:null`, draft-policy `unavailable`, honorary, ended member, another user's seeded data absent, extra response key rejection and zero mutation/audit rows.

## Verification strategy
> All verification is agent-executed. Explicit owner/admin approvals are input gates and never count as verification or inferred consent.
- Test decision: tests-after using `tsx --test server/*.test.ts`, focused Node test files, transactional PostgreSQL integration/concurrency tests and catalog SQL assertions.
- Canonical evidence: every task writes `<attemptDir>/task-<N>-database-architecture-audit.json`; every final verifier writes `<attemptDir>/final-<F>-database-architecture-audit.json`. A task envelope binds its own post-task commit SHA (`task_commit_sha`), plan digest, manifest digest available at that task, DB target/read-write mode, command/exit/assertions and attachment digests; it must not guess the later final implementation SHA. Todo 22 freezes `implementation_sha`, then F1–F4 each bind that exact SHA plus a clean detached-worktree digest. Human-readable `.md`, TAP, SQL, screenshot and log files are attachments only.
- Attempt directories are working storage, not the audit archive. After F1–F4 approve, the orchestrator creates an isolated Git worktree on ref `refs/heads/codex/database-architecture-evidence/<implementation_sha>`, whose root contains only `F1.json|F2.json|F3.json|F4.json`, strict-schema redacted attachment files, and `index.json`. `index.json` is RFC-8785 canonical JSON+LF with exactly `{schema_version:"dgkma-evidence-index-v1",pre_attestation_sha,manifest_sha256,files:[{path,sha256}],parent_evidence_commit_sha:null,index_sha256}`; `files` lists every committed file except `index.json`, sorts by UTF-8 path, and hashes each exact blob byte sequence. The index self-hash excludes only `index_sha256`; no credential/name/contact/bank/source row is admitted. Commit message is `evidence(db): archive F1-F4 for <implementation_sha>`; its immutable `f_evidence_commit_sha` is recorded in the owner display, owner receipt, A1 input and attestation JSON. A1 descriptor-reads the four receipts from that exact commit via `git show <sha>:<path>`, never from `<attemptDir>`.
- After A1 terminal resolution, the same evidence ref receives one child commit adding `A1.json` and replacing `index.json` with the same schema but `parent_evidence_commit_sha=f_evidence_commit_sha` and all five receipt files. The final report records this immutable `evidence_archive_commit_sha`; a later audit retrieves every original outer-evidence byte with `git show <evidence_archive_commit_sha>:<path>` and rechecks its self-hash/provider resolution. The evidence ref is outside the product branch/protected-tree digest, so archival cannot mutate the F-approved implementation. Missing ref/commit/file, a PII scan finding, hash mismatch or garbage-collected/unreachable commit blocks A1/final handoff.
- Runtime: static inspection may run locally, but `npm test`, `npm run check`, `npm run build`, catalog checks, migration dry-runs and all DB assertions run through the configured Replit SSH in `/home/runner/workspace` against Development unless a todo explicitly says read-only Production.
- Database mutation safety: before every Development apply, record `current_database()`, `current_user`, migration-ledger state and affected table counts; apply in a transaction where supported; reconnect and repeat catalog/count checks.
- Production boundary: a read-only catalog may run only through dedicated `PROD_DATABASE_READONLY_URL`. If it is absent, Production remains `unverified`; do not substitute the owner URL, even inside a read-only transaction. Any Production mutation, Republish or production smoke check remains a separate user-approved execution.
- Executable QA harness: Todo 1 creates the verifier harness/fixtures and materializes the **already approved** five distinct prompts plus six `additionalProperties:false` schemas at `docs/verifier-prompts/{F1,F2,F3,F4,A1}.md` and `docs/verifier-schemas/{F1,F2,F3,F4,F3-browser,A1}.schema.json`; those files are committed before any implementation todo. Every prompt is the exact six LF-terminated lines below, with no BOM, CR or trailing spaces; substitute only `<LANE>` and the one exact `<ROLE>` value in the following map:
  ```text
  schema_version: dgkma-verifier-prompt-v1
  lane: <LANE>
  role: <ROLE>
  input: Verify only the supplied frozen implementation SHA, manifest SHA, protected-tree digest, target fingerprints, and attachments.
  method: Independently recompute every lane assertion; repository harness verdicts are untrusted evidence.
  output: Emit only the receipt-free dgkma-verifier-assertion-payload-v1 JSON and APPROVE only when every assertion is approved.
  ```
  Role map: `F1=Verify plan, manifest, catalog, scope, and source-authority traceability.`; `F2=Verify physical schema, authorization, lock order, append-only invariants, security, and privacy.`; `F3=Verify Development bootstrap, restore, repository commands, browser flows, and negative authorization.`; `F4=Verify source reconciliation, officer assignments, draft-policy nonpersistence, legacy crosswalk, Sheet fidelity, and Production unchanged.`; `A1=Resolve provider-owned F receipts and owner approval, then verify marker-only attestation staging.`. The literal lane replaces `<LANE>` and the literal sentence replaces `<ROLE>`; angle-bracket tokens may not remain.
- The six JSON Schema bytes are also deterministic rather than prose-selected: each file is RFC-8785 canonical JSON plus one LF and has top-level `$defs.assertion_payload` plus the outer evidence schema. For F1–F4, the receipt-free assertion definition is `additionalProperties:false` and requires exactly `{schema_version,lane,prompt_sha256,implementation_sha,manifest_sha256,protected_tree_digest_v1,target_fingerprints,assertions,attachment_digests,result,payload_sha256}`; `schema_version` is const `dgkma-verifier-assertion-payload-v1`, `lane` is the file's lane, and self-hash excludes only `payload_sha256`. The A1 receipt-free definition instead requires exactly `{schema_version,lane,prompt_sha256,owner_receipt_sha256,pre_attestation_sha,manifest_sha256,f_evidence_commit_sha,f1_sha256,f2_sha256,f3_sha256,f4_sha256,protected_tree_digest_v1,staged_paths,marker_hunks,assertions,attachment_digests,result,payload_sha256}` with lane `A1`. Each outer schema requires its printed envelope and requires every duplicated field to equal the decoded `$defs.assertion_payload`. Scalar dictionary is exact: SHA=`{"type":"string","pattern":"^[0-9a-f]{64}$"}`, RFC-3339=`{"type":"string","format":"date-time"}`, nonempty string=`{"type":"string","minLength":1}`, boolean=`{"type":"boolean"}`, nonnegative integer=`{"type":"integer","minimum":0}`, result=`{"enum":["approved","rejected"]}`. `attachment_digests|screenshot_digests` are arrays of `{"type":"object","additionalProperties":false,"required":["path","sha256"],"properties":{"path":{"type":"string","minLength":1},"sha256":SHA}}` sorted by path; `staged_paths|marker_hunks` are unique nonempty-string arrays sorted UTF-8. Nested `verifier_identity`, `platform_receipt`, `target_fingerprints`, lane `assertions`, browser `routes`, browser `masking`, owner receipt and A1 assertions use exactly the key sets and constants printed in the adjacent contracts, each with `additionalProperties:false` and all keys required. `target_fingerprints.disposable_runs` is a nonempty unique SHA array and `production_readonly` is `oneOf` SHA or const `unverified`; every assertion property uses result but cannot be `rejected` when top-level result is approved. The frozen verifier inventory is exactly twelve files: five prompts, six evidence JSON Schemas, and the one non-schema `browser-pii-regex-v1.json` support artifact. Todo 1 records all twelve SHA-256 values in one closed-key sidecar; Todo 22 must reproduce all twelve. Any semantic edit after Todo 1 restarts implementation review.
- Final verifiers use provider `codex-app`, model `gpt-5.6-sol`, thinking/reasoning effort `xhigh`, and five distinct provider-owned tasks. The five thread tools are intentionally deferred rather than guaranteed in the upfront inventory. Todo 1 must first call `tool_search` with exact query `Codex app list_projects create_thread list_threads wait_threads read_thread exact schemas project worktree startingState model thinking` and require the returned callable schemas named `codex_app.list_projects`, `codex_app.create_thread`, `codex_app.list_threads`, `codex_app.wait_threads`, and `codex_app.read_thread`; execution re-discovers them and fails closed if any is absent or changed. Before implementation work, Todo 1 pauses for the owner's explicit permission to create one disposable capability probe. The executor calls `codex_app.list_projects()` with no arguments, selects exactly the one project whose root path equals `/Users/raphael/Playground/dgkmaWeb` and is a Git repository, and records its opaque `projectId`; zero/multiple matches block. It then calls `codex_app.create_thread({prompt:"Return exactly PROVIDER_CAPABILITY_OK:<preflight_run_uid>",target:{type:"project",projectId,environment:{type:"worktree"}},model:"gpt-5.6-sol",thinking:"xhigh"})`; when creation returns only `clientThreadId` plus optional `hostId`, it calls `codex_app.list_threads` and requires exactly one ready thread matching that client ID, project, prompt/worktree context and host. Zero/multiple matches, unresolved queued-only state or any mismatch blocks. It calls `codex_app.wait_threads({targets:[{threadId,hostId:<resolved-hostId-if-any>}],timeoutMs:120000})`, repeating only bounded waits on that same resolved thread while state remains terminally resolvable, then calls `codex_app.read_thread({threadId,hostId:<resolved-hostId-if-any>,includeOutputs:false,maxOutputCharsPerItem:200000,turnLimit:20})`; no elapsed-time inference or alternate thread is allowed. The external receipt is exact canonical JSON `{schema_version:"dgkma-provider-capability-v2",preflight_run_uid,provider,project_id,thread_id,host_id_or_null,model,reasoning_effort,create_request_sha256,list_projects_response_sha256,create_response_sha256,list_threads_response_sha256,wait_response_sha256,read_response_sha256,prompt_sha256,final_message_sha256,projection_sha256,status,result,receipt_sha256}`. The PII-safe source projection is uncommitted at `<attemptDir>/provider-capability-projection.json`; its separate non-inventory closed schema and committed descriptor are `docs/provider-contracts/provider-capability-projection.schema.json` and `docs/verifier-provider-capability-projection.json`. The harness descriptor-validates the source projection and independently recomputes every request/response digest plus the projection and receipt self-hashes. Provider/model/effort are `codex-app|gpt-5.6-sol|xhigh`, status/result are `completed|verified`, and the final message bytes equal the nonce response. Missing fields or inability to resolve them stops all later work as `blocked_provider_capability`; no repository fixture, inferred metadata or alternate task mechanism substitutes. The provider projection schema/descriptor are support contracts outside and do not change the frozen exact twelve-file verifier inventory. At Todo 22 the executor pauses again, shows the frozen SHA/digests and asks the owner to authorize four F task creations; A1 separately requires the later explicit attestation approval. Each F/A create call uses the same exact project target but `environment:{type:"worktree",startingState:{type:"branch",branchName:"<implementation_sha>"}}`, the explicit model/thinking pair, and prompt equal to exact committed prompt bytes followed only by content-addressed attachment descriptors.
- Provider resolution uses only fields the callable tools expose. Creation may return an immediate ready `threadId`, or the current API's `clientThreadId` plus optional `hostId`; the latter is accepted only after `list_threads` resolves exactly one ready thread with the same project, prompt/worktree context, client ID and host. Nullable returned `hostId` is copied as `host_id_or_null` and included in later list/wait/read arguments when nonnull. `wait_threads` uses the callable maximum `timeoutMs:120000` and must report terminal completion; repeated waits remain bound to the same resolved thread. `read_thread` must return the exact original prompt plus one terminal assistant message parseable as the receipt-free assertion payload. Zero/multiple project or thread resolution, unresolved queued-only creation, context mismatch, timeout/nonterminal state, nonce mismatch or missing/multiple terminal payloads rejects. The canonical resolved projection is RFC-8785 `{provider:"codex-app",model:"gpt-5.6-sol",reasoning_effort:"xhigh",project_id,thread_id,host_id_or_null,create_request_sha256,list_projects_response_sha256,create_response_sha256,list_threads_response_sha256,wait_response_sha256,read_response_sha256,prompt_sha256,projection_sha256,final_message_sha256,status:"completed"}`. `provider_resolution_sha256` hashes this projection. Each platform receipt is exactly `{schema_version:"dgkma-platform-receipt-v2",provider,model,reasoning_effort,project_id,thread_id,host_id_or_null,create_request_sha256,list_projects_response_sha256,create_response_sha256,list_threads_response_sha256,wait_response_sha256,read_response_sha256,prompt_sha256,projection_sha256,provider_resolution_sha256,receipt_sha256}`; its self-hash excludes only `receipt_sha256`. Thread IDs and prompt SHA values are pairwise distinct across F1–F4/A1.
- A verifier's terminal message is only the receipt-free assertion payload. After terminal completion, the orchestrator hashes those exact bytes, builds the platform receipt whose provider projection includes that `final_message_sha256`, and only then builds the common F1–F4 outer envelope exactly `{schema_version:"dgkma-final-evidence-v1",verifier_identity,platform_receipt,assertion_payload_sha256,committed_prompt_path,prompt_sha256,implementation_sha,manifest_sha256,protected_tree_digest_v1,target_fingerprints,assertions,attachment_digests,result,evidence_sha256}`. `assertion_payload_sha256` is the exact terminal-message SHA and every duplicated business field must equal the decoded payload; neither payload nor its hash contains the platform receipt, so no self-hash cycle exists. `verifier_identity` is exactly `{provider,model,reasoning_effort,project_id,thread_id,host_id_or_null}` and must equal those fields in the resolved platform receipt. `target_fingerprints` is exactly `{development,disposable_runs,production_readonly}`, where `disposable_runs` is an ordered nonempty array and Production is a lowercase SHA or literal `unverified`; a single ambiguous target fingerprint is forbidden. `result` is `approved|rejected`; outer self-hash excludes only `evidence_sha256`. Lane assertion sets remain the exact F1–F4 sets printed below, with values only `approved|rejected`.
- Assertion objects are literal: F1 `{plan_manifest_traceability,catalog_equality,scope_guards,source_authority}`; F2 `{physical_schema,actor_authorization,lock_order,append_only_invariants,security_privacy}`; F3 `{bootstrap_upgrade_restore,repository_commands,browser_receipt,negative_authorization}`; F4 `{source_reconciliation,officer_assignments,draft_policy_nonpersistence,legacy_crosswalk,sheet_fidelity,production_unchanged}`. Every value is `approved|rejected`; top-level approval requires all approved.
- `final-F3-browser.json` has exactly `{schema_version:"dgkma-f3-browser-v1",implementation_sha,platform_session_id,session_user_uid,is_admin,admin_receipt_sha256,started_at,finished_at,routes,masking,screenshot_digests,console_error_count,server_error_count,result,receipt_sha256}`. `platform_session_id` must equal F3's resolved provider `thread_id`—not a local browser ID. `routes` is the ordered exact array of `{id,method,path,mode,expected_status,observed_status,result}`: `public_home|GET|/|browser|200`, `profile_self|GET|/api/auth/me|browser|200`, `admin_dashboard|GET|/admin|browser|200`, `alumni_sync_preview|POST|/api/admin/sync-alumni/preview|browser|200`, `member_accounting_self|GET|/api/accounting/me|browser|200`, `account_deletion_fixture|DELETE|/api/users/me|isolated_fixture|204`; no query string is stored. The destructive route runs only against a disposable isolated fixture, never the live Development browser user. `masking` is exactly `{mask_version:"dgkma-browser-mask-v1",masked_selectors,ocr_scan_sha256,pii_regex_scan_sha256,pii_finding_count}`; `masked_selectors` is the sorted literal array `["[data-pii='account']","[data-pii='address']","[data-pii='email']","[data-pii='name']","[data-pii='phone']"]`. Todo 1 commits `docs/verifier-schemas/browser-pii-regex-v1.json` as RFC-8785 canonical JSON plus LF with this exact top-level structure, key set, array order and regex flags: `{"schema_version":"dgkma-browser-pii-regex-v1","flags":"iu","patterns":[{"name":"secret","source":"(password|secret|token|authorization|api[_-]?key)\\s*[:=]\\s*\\S+"},{"name":"email","source":"[A-Z0-9._%+-]+@[A-Z0-9.-]+\\.[A-Z]{2,}"},{"name":"phone","source":"(?:\\+?82[- ]?)?0?10[- ]?[0-9]{4}[- ]?[0-9]{4}"},{"name":"account","source":"\\b[0-9]{2,6}[- ][0-9]{2,6}[- ][0-9]{2,8}\\b"},{"name":"address","source":"(서울|부산|대구|인천|광주|대전|울산|세종|경기|강원|충북|충남|전북|전남|경북|경남|제주)[^\\n]{0,40}(시|군|구|로|길)\\b"}]}`. Every object has only `name|source`; names are unique and remain in that printed order; the scanner constructs JavaScript `RegExp(source,"iu")` and never adds or overrides flags. Its SHA joins the Todo-1 sidecar. Before every screenshot the agent injects the mask, captures only the assertion rectangle, runs OCR plus those patterns, and rejects any finding. Both scan digests bind the cropped attachments and `pii_finding_count` must be zero. Approval requires exact actor receipt, all route outcomes approved, exactly five masked/cropped browser-route digests and both error counts zero.
- Freeze uses both the pre-attestation `implementation_sha` and `protected_tree_digest_v1`. Each tracked plan has exactly one byte-exact marker pair `<!-- ARCHITECTURE_ATTESTATION_GATE_BEGIN v1 -->\n` and `<!-- ARCHITECTURE_ATTESTATION_GATE_END v1 -->`; the payload between them is canonicalized to `<GATE-CONTENT-EXCLUDED-v1>\n`. The protected path set is every path in `git ls-tree -r --name-only <implementation_sha>` plus the synthetic path `docs/database-architecture-attestation.json`. For each UTF-8 byte-sorted path, canonical bytes are ordinary blob bytes, gate-canonicalized plan bytes, or always `<ATTESTATION-FILE-EXCLUDED-v1>\n` for the attestation path whether absent at freeze or present in A1's staged index. Hash input is repeated `path_bytes || 0x00 || ascii_decimal(content_byte_length) || 0x00 || content_bytes || 0x00`; its SHA-256 is `protected_tree_digest_v1`.
- After freeze, any changed protected byte invalidates F1–F4 except the narrowly canonicalized A1 change: creation of `docs/database-architecture-attestation.json` matching its strict schema and payload-only edits inside the two tracked plan gate marker pairs. A1 recomputes the digest from staged-index bytes; it must equal the frozen digest, and `git diff --cached --name-only` must be exactly the attestation plus those two plans. Diff hunks crossing either marker, a second marker pair, path add/delete/rename, `.omo` staging or any other changed path invalidates all F receipts. The attestation commit has a new SHA but retains the F-approved pre-attestation SHA/digest as its subject; this exception is part of the freeze contract, not a post-review relaxation.

<!-- ARCHITECTURE_ATTESTATION_GATE_BEGIN v1 -->
<!-- ARCHITECTURE_ATTESTATION_GATE_END v1 -->
- The attestation JSON has exactly `schema_version:"database-architecture-attestation-v1"`, `pre_attestation_sha`, `protected_tree_digest_v1`, `manifest_sha256`, `f_evidence_commit_sha`, `f1_sha256`, `f2_sha256`, `f3_sha256`, `f4_sha256`, and `owner_approval:{thread_id,message_id,observed_at,text_sha256}`; no extra keys. Each F digest must equal the exact file retrieved from `f_evidence_commit_sha`. Each plan gate payload is exactly one line `<!-- database-architecture-attestation-v1:<sha256(attestation RFC-8785 canonical JSON)> -->` plus LF. The harness validates lowercase 64-hex digests, RFC-3339 time, platform receipt equality and that both gate digests equal the staged attestation bytes.
- Agent-only surfaces are separate from Node: the harness may orchestrate shell/SSH/HTTP fixture checks but cannot claim to control the in-app browser or an authenticated external API session. F3's assigned QA task directly uses the in-app browser and stores screenshots/route/console observations; Todo 23's assigned executor uses only the exact Drive-v3/Sheets-v4 methods and OAuth scopes printed above. Their JSON receipts are attachments that the final harness validates by schema, digest, timestamp and expected assertions.

## Execution strategy
### Parallel execution waves
> Target 5-8 todos per wave. Fewer than 3 (except the final) means you under-split.

- **Wave 1 — authority, target evidence and workload facts:** Todos 1, 3, 4, 5 and 8 establish the frozen baseline, deterministic target/catalog contracts, restore specification and measured index candidates.
- **Wave 2 — manifest and brownfield integrity:** Todos 2, 6, 7, 9 and 10 consume those facts to expand the sole manifest, define integrity/identity changes and prepare startup/retention replacement.
- **Wave 3 — accounting manifest and migration artifacts:** Todos 11–16 and 23 correct the accounting member/source/role/receipt/audit model, update policy/source documents, create the approved additive 22nd-officer authority tab and generate deterministic additive migration artifacts.
- **Wave 4 — Development proof and rollout handoff:** Todos 17–22 apply and backfill Development, prove source/legacy/role/cashbook invariants, measure workloads, run full regression/browser QA and prepare a read-only Production drift/rollout dossier.

## Commit strategy

- Before creating the task-owned `codex/` worktree, `$start-work` descriptor-opens and hashes this approved plan, copies only this plan and its accounting plan/drafts into the worktree's untracked `.omo/`, then verifies byte equality; it never reads/copies `KIKcd_B*`. Todo 1 materializes the approved architecture/accounting plans as tracked `docs/plans/*.md` so execution provenance survives worktree cleanup. Historical Todo 1 evidence remains bound to product baseline `0e299abce9509456de0e8bfe224cb7ef392228d8` (which contains ancestor `bb88a029a4d42bf21a0bf7e89403b4a4037565cd`); all resumed implementation after this reconciliation must additionally descend from current shared-source authority `f0850820c0dd401cd316c08456eb427a1f0780ca`. Never stage the user's original untracked `.omo/`, `KIKcd_B*` or unrelated files.
- Use the commit boundaries named in each todo. No environment evidence is committed to the product branch except four pre-mutation authorization receipts: Todo 17's redacted `docs/database-targets/development-admin-approved.json` must be committed/re-proved before sequence 50, and Todo 23's three strict receipts must be committed in their printed order before the Sheet mutation. They contain only immutable IDs/UIDs, public roster or non-secret digests/statuses—no name/contact/token/raw ETag. Raw post-write outcome/logs/attachments remain uncommitted; after F approval, only the exact strict-schema PII-safe receipts and admitted redacted attachments are committed on the separate evidence ref as specified above.
- Before each commit run the focused tests plus `git diff --check`; before the final commit run `npm test`, `npm run check` and `npm run build` in Replit Development.
- Never commit credentials, private accounting/bank/member source rows, contact data, full account identifiers, dumps or snapshots. The sole source-row exception is the user-approved public 22nd officer roster's ten non-contact columns and exact 33×10 payload in `docs/source-authority/22nd-officers.json`; it contains no address/phone/email/workplace/account data and is authorized specifically for reproducible publication. Every other raw row remains prohibited.
- The architecture plan owns baseline/ledger/catalog/target/security foundations and the corrected accounting schema manifest. The accounting/dues feature plan remains blocked until A1 records the frozen pre-attestation implementation SHA and manifest digest as its prerequisite after F1–F4 and user approval.

## Success criteria

- The final Development catalog is reproducible from a clean database and from the verified 13-table/112-column brownfield baseline using the same ordered, checksummed ledger; a second apply is a no-op and checksum/target/capability drift fails closed.
- Existing schema behavior remains compatible: all repository tests pass, account deletion preserves required history while removing live account links, Kakao/session/pending flows remain secure, and no existing table/column or legacy payment is destructively removed.
- Every approved schema object has one owner artifact and appears exactly once in the manifest/catalog. Owners, ACLs, RLS stance, functions, triggers, extensions, constraints, indexes and ledger rows are observable by the canonical read-only catalog.
- Every change to a table, column, constraint, index, sequence, view, trigger, RLS/policy, routine, enum/domain, runtime DDL or migration updates `docs/database-schema.md` in the same PR, reruns the metadata-only catalog against the authorized target, and updates `docs/database-operations.md` in that PR whenever operating or recovery procedures change.
- The corrected accounting manifest has stable member IDs, durable match/relink history, authoritative `users.id` actors plus immutable snapshots, version-safe overlapping positions, policy-gated tier derivation, source-row economic-event identity/provenance and exact cashbook↔receipt/reversal equations.
- All 21 primary and 11 concurrent 22nd assignments are present in the additive authority tab and Development role history; 21st normalization ends at `2026-02-28 12:38 KST`; the non-persistent draft preview shows 총회의장 at 30,000/400,000, while Development persists zero tier/rights rows until real board approval metadata exists.
- Legacy-payment preview/backfill is idempotent, quarantines ambiguous/null/invalid rows, survives concurrent catch-up, and cannot double-count finalized-ledger/bank economic events. The legacy table and rollback read path remain available.
- Security/retention/workload evidence passes: no unexpected PUBLIC grant or direct-browser DB surface, safe connection/pool/function configuration, no sensitive values in logs/evidence, only the manifest-owned fixed indexes measured, completed restore drill and policy-pending rather than invented RPO/RTO.
- `npm test`, `npm run check`, `npm run build`, `git diff --check`, Development catalog/ledger assertions and four externally identified, prompt-pinned F1–F4 verifiers approve the same frozen implementation SHA; any later protected-byte change invalidates them, and only a platform-bound explicit user approval may authorize A1.
- Production Database, Replit Secrets, deployment and existing accounting workbook rows are unchanged. The handoff states whether Production read-only drift was verified or remains unverified and requires a new explicit production action.
